# Evidence for the public claim: MathDB problems resolved by AI with kernel-checked Lean proofs

Generated by `public-claim-100/scripts/build_evidence.py` from `status.json` and the lane outputs. Every statement below is traceable to a file named in the row. Nothing here has been submitted or published.

## Counts

| Group | Meaning | Rows |
|---|---|---|
| Tier 1 | cleared by adversarial review of a non-author model: literal, not found public, not routine, not trivial | 105 |
| Tier 2 | correct, literal, not found public, kernel-checked refutation; a generic computer search finds a counterexample in seconds or minutes at or near the first size the source did not check | 52 |
| Tier 3 | the MathDB record text is a faulty transcription; the conjecture as printed in the cited paper is resolved and kernel-checked | 5 |

Checks applied to every row: (1) kernel-checked Lean endpoint with only the axioms propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound, rebuilt from extracted sources (replay record named in the row); (2) a statement-fidelity audit of the endpoint against the MathDB record; (3) a uniform prior-art sweep (source versions, citing papers through OpenAlex and Semantic Scholar, key-term web search, the openai/math and omega registries, ProbXiv, GitHub search) plus a search by MathDB id of GitHub and of the Principia archive of public MathDB solutions (300 ids); (4) an adversarial test by a model that did not author the result. Not checked: mathdb.com itself (it refuses automated clients), Google Scholar and the arXiv search API (both refuse automated clients), and the publisher texts listed per row as unread.

## Tier 1 (105): cleared by adversarial review of a non-author model: literal, not found public, not routine, not trivial

### 317750 · The prism B-factorizability conjecture

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 158)
- **Source:** Kenji Kashiwabara, “Sports scheduling for not all pairs of teams”, arXiv:0809.3682 (2008).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture holds for every k ≥ 3: whenever a home-away pattern table for the k-gonal prism (a round-robin style schedule in which only the pairs of teams joined by a prism edge play) admits a fractional compatible schedule, it admits an integral one. The proof starts from Kashiwabara's own Corollary 9, which reduces the question to finding a compatible proper 3-edge-colouring, and then uses three prism-specific steps: the differences along the two rails alternate, the one-turn digraph of the pattern can be spliced into cycles with the right cut parity, and the exclusions on the rungs propagate around the prism to give the colouring. Kashiwabara had verified the cube (k = 4) and the pentagonal prism (k = 5) by Hilbert-basis computation. Lean states Definition 4 of the source literally (the monoid generated by compatible perfect matchings, its saturation, B-factorizability) and proves it for prism k for all k ≥ 3, together with the equivalent statement that the feasible polytope has an integral point when it is non-empty.
- **MathDB record statement:** For a regular graph $G=(V,E)$, call $G$ **B-factorizable** if every saturated monoid element with edge component equal to the graph's characteristic vector belongs to the monoid generated by compatible perfect matchings. A $k$-gonal prism is the graph formed by two $k$-cycles together with corresponding matching edges. **Prism B-factorizability conjecture.** The graph of every $k$-gonal prism is B-factorizable. The source reports verification for the 3-dimensional cube and the pentagonal prism by Hilbert-basis calculations, but does not establish the claim for every $k$.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Prism317750.mathdb317750`, `Prism317750.prism_B_factorizable`, `Prism317750.mathdb317750_polytope`
- **Replay record:** `prism-b-factorizable-317750-root-replay-opus55-20261121-r1 (root, Claude Opus 5.5; public-claim-100/replay-317750.log all_ok, 8 modules)`; acceptance record `prism-b-factorizability-317750-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/317750-prism-b-factorizability`
- **Prior art searched:** `t1-sol-cross-317750-sol62-20261123-r1/CROSS-REVIEW-317750.md (GPT-6.2 Sol, the second model: CONFIRMED_TIER1, not routine 0.85, correct 0.995, not public 0.95; no Lean definition issue; fractional and integral feasibility compared independently on all 220 triangular-prism and 7,770 cube pattern tables; paper PASS WITH EDITORIAL EDITS); revised paper used`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (candidate review and final check); see lane queries and routes recorded
- **Adversarial verdicts:** PASS (t1-sol-cross-317750-sol62-20261123-r1/CROSS-REVIEW-317750.md (GPT-6.2 Sol, the second model: CONFIRMED_TIER1, not routine 0.85, correct 0.995, not public 0.95; no Lean definition issue; fractional and integral feasibility compared independently on all 220 triangular-prism and 7,770 cube pattern tables; paper PASS WITH EDITORIAL EDITS); revised paper used)
- **Status note:** round 3, accepted 158; Kashiwabara Conjecture 17 proved (every k-gonal prism, k >= 3, is B-factorizable; from the source's Corollary 9 by rail alternation, cycle splicing with cut parity and rung propagation); proof, same-model review and Lean by Opus (8 modules); cross-model review and final check by GPT-6.2 Sol: CONFIRMED, not routine 0.85; k = 4, 5 were verified by the source
- **Caveats the row carries:**
  - The result is the existence of a compatible integral schedule on prisms whenever a fractional one exists, for all k ≥ 3; it does not assert that every vertex of the polytope is integral and does not touch Briskorn's conjecture for complete graphs. Kashiwabara had already verified k = 4 and k = 5. No earlier public solution was found in the sources searched; two peripheral citing papers were screened at abstract level only and one omega registry surname query was rate-limited. ### 317945 · The three-small-triangles conjecture for five points

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 103)
- **Source:** Matthew Kahle, “Points in a triangle forcing small triangles”, arXiv:0811.2449 (2008).
- **What is proved (exact):** For any triangle of area 1 in the plane and any five points of the closed triangle (not necessarily distinct), at least three of the ten unordered triples of the points span a triangle of area at most 1/4, where degenerate triples count with area 0. This is Kahle's three-small-triangles conjecture; three cannot be replaced by four. The proof is a case analysis on the midpoint subdivision with a determinant identity; the reduction from an arbitrary triangle to a standard one is proved in Lean.
- **MathDB record statement:** Call a triangle **small** if its area is at most $1/4$. Given five points in a triangle of unit area, consider the triangles determined by triples of the five points. **Three-small-triangles conjecture.** The five points determine at least three small triangles. The claim would strengthen the preceding result, which guarantees only one small triangle. The source notes that three cannot be replaced by four for the displayed configuration; if true, the conjecture would imply corresponding lower bounds for larger point sets by averaging.
- **Lean endpoint:** `kahle_conj_3_1`
- **Replay record:** `five-points-317945-root-final-replay-fable-20261119-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2)`; acceptance record `kahle-three-small-triangles-317945-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/317945-kahle-three-small-triangles`
- **Prior art searched:** `shard019-021-candidates-review-opus-20261119-r1/REVIEW-317945.md (Claude Opus 5.5, PASS, FLAGS none)`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (candidate review and final check); see lane queries and routes recorded
- **Adversarial verdicts:** PASS (shard019-021-candidates-review-opus-20261119-r1/REVIEW-317945.md (Claude Opus 5.5, PASS, FLAGS none))
- **Status note:** replacement, accepted 103
- **Caveats the row carries:**
  - Degenerate triples are counted as small, the convention forced by the source's use of Soifer's one-triangle theorem. Soifer's book chapter on the problem (Springer) was not read; it is understood to reprint the 2008 material. Bounded literature search only.

### 321730 · Locating chromatic numbers of Cartesian products with cycles and paths

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 171)
- **Source:** Ali Behtoei and Behnaz Omoomi, “On the Locating Chromatic Number of the Cartesian Product of Graphs”, arXiv:1106.3453 (2012).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture holds with explicit thresholds: for all m ≥ 7 and n ≥ 4 the locating chromatic numbers of K_m □ C_n and K_m □ P_n are equal, both being m + 1 when n ≤ m + 1 and m + 2 otherwise. The path value is Behtoei and Omoomi's own Theorem 2 (re-proved here); the new part is the cycle. Lower bound: each copy of K_m uses m distinct colours, and counting runs of copies that miss the same colour shows that m + 1 colours cannot locate once there are more than m + 1 copies. Upper bounds: for n ≤ m + 1 a cyclic colouring in which each copy misses a different colour, completed by a Hall-type filling; for n ≥ m + 2 two singleton colour classes whose distances separate the copies, with a correction when the cycle is odd. Lean defines the products, graph distance, locating colourings (proper colourings in which the vectors of distances to the colour classes are pairwise distinct) and the locating chromatic number as a least number of colours that is proved to be attained, and proves both formulas and the conjecture.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $K_m$ be the complete graph, and let $C_n$ and $P_n$ denote the cycle and path on $n$ vertices, respectively. Write $\square$ for the Cartesian product and $\chi_L$ for the locating chromatic number. **Locating chromatic number conjecture for products with cycles and paths.** If $m$ and $n$ are sufficiently large, then $$ \chi_L(K_m\square C_n)=\chi_L(K_m\square P_n). $$ The conjecture is motivated by the similarity between the structures of $K_m\square C_n$ and $K_m\square P_n$; no resolution is supplied in the source.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Locating321730.mathdb_321730`, `Locating321730.locChrom_KC`, `Locating321730.locChrom_KP`
- **Replay record:** `locating-chromatic-321730-root-replay-fable-20261123-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2; public-claim-100/replay-321730.log all_ok, 10 modules)`; acceptance record `locating-chromatic-complete-times-cycle-321730-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/321730-locating-chromatic-complete-times-cycle`
- **Prior art searched:** `t1-prelean-X99-sol62-20261123-r1/REVIEW-321730.md (GPT-6.2 Sol, the second model, before the Lean was written: CONFIRMED_TIER1, not routine 0.76; the published paper still states the cycle equality as Conjecture 3; fixed the Lean endpoint with thresholds 7 and 4)`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (candidate review and final check); see lane queries and routes recorded
- **Adversarial verdicts:** PASS (t1-prelean-X99-sol62-20261123-r1/REVIEW-321730.md (GPT-6.2 Sol, the second model, before the Lean was written: CONFIRMED_TIER1, not routine 0.76; the published paper still states the cycle equality as Conjecture 3; fixed the Lean endpoint with thresholds 7 and 4))
- **Status note:** round 3, accepted 171; Behtoei-Omoomi Conjecture 3 proved with explicit thresholds: for m >= 7, n >= 4 the locating chromatic numbers of K_m x C_n and K_m x P_n (Cartesian products) are equal (m+1 if n <= m+1, else m+2); cycle value new, path value is the source's Theorem 2; proof by Opus; GPT-6.2 Sol cross-model review before Lean: CONFIRMED, not routine 0.76 (same-model Opus review had 0.5); Lean by Sol (10 modules); final check by a separate Sol lane PASS, COUNTS; omega and mxym/math screens by root clean
- **Caveats the row carries:**
  - The conjecture says 'sufficiently large' without thresholds; the result gives the thresholds m ≥ 7 and n ≥ 4 and says nothing about smaller cases. The path formula is Behtoei and Omoomi's Theorem 2 (valid for m ≥ 3, n ≥ 2); the contribution is the cycle formula. Closest related work: Ghanbari and Mojdeh (2024) treat the same family for the stronger neighbour-locating invariant and give an m + 1 colouring for triangles. ProbXiv was unavailable to the final check. Proof and first (same-model) rev…

### 322805 · Congruence conjecture for signed power sums of Franel numbers

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 119)
- **Source:** Victor J. W. Guo, “Proof of two conjectures of Z.-W. Sun on congruences for Franel numbers”, arXiv:1201.0617 (2012).
- **What is proved (exact):** For all integers n >= 1 and r >= 0, 2n divides the sum over k from 0 to n - 1 of (-1)^(rk) (3k + 2) f_k^(r), where f_k^(r) is the sum of the r-th powers of the binomial coefficients C(k, j). This is Conjecture 4.1 of Guo, who proved the cases r <= 3. The proof deforms the sum to a polynomial in q with Gaussian binomials, putting the weight q^(n-1-k)[k+j+1] on half of the sum, shows by the q-Lucas theorem and a block factorization with its phase signs that the deformation vanishes at every n-th root of unity other than 1, divides by the monic polynomial [n] over the integers, and sets q = 1; the factor 2 comes from the symmetry of the half-sum. Lean states the congruence in the integers exactly as printed, for every n >= 1 and every natural r.
- **MathDB record statement:** For nonnegative integers $n$ and $r$, define $$ f_{n}^{(r)}=\sum_{k=0}^n{n\choose k}^r. $$ In particular, $f_n^{(3)}=f_n$ are the Franel numbers. **The signed Franel congruence conjecture.** Let $n\geq 1$ and $r\geq 0$ be two integers. Then $$ \sum_{k=0}^{n-1}(-1)^{rk}(3k+2)f_{k}^{(r)}\equiv 0\pmod {2n}. $$ This conjecture unifies the preceding congruences and Sun's fourth-power case; its status is not resolved in the supplied text.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Franel.guo_conj_4_1`
- **Replay record:** `franel-power-sum-322805-root-replay-fable-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2; public-claim-100/replay-322805.log all_ok)`; acceptance record `franel-power-sum-signed-congruence-322805-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/322805-franel-power-sum-signed-congruence`
- **Prior art searched:** `t1-prelean-X8-opus-20261120-r1/REVIEW-322805.md (Claude Opus 5.5, before Lean: every step checked, the congruence computed for n <= 80 and r <= 12, public and routine tests, required Lean endpoint fixed; provisional CONFIRMED_TIER1) and t1-prelean-X13-opus-20261120-r1/CONFIRM-322805.md (Claude Opus 5.5, after Lean: CONFIRMED_TIER1 final; no definition gap; 540 cases agree; the Zhang-Yuan abstract and reference list read through Crossref and the three divisibilities reconstructed)`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (candidate review and final check); see lane queries and routes recorded
- **Adversarial verdicts:** PASS (t1-prelean-X8-opus-20261120-r1/REVIEW-322805.md (Claude Opus 5.5, before Lean: every step checked, the congruence computed for n <= 80 and r <= 12, public and routine tests, required Lean endpoint fixed; provisional CONFIRMED_TIER1) and t1-prelean-X13-opus-20261120-r1/CONFIRM-322805.md (Claude Opus 5.5, after Lean: CONFIRMED_TIER1 final; no definition gap; 540 cases agree; the Zhang-Yuan abstract and reference list read through Crossref and the three divisibilities reconstructed))

### 323800 · Hole-one-descent conjecture for generalized monotone triangles

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 165)
- **Source:** Lukas Riegler, “Generalized Monotone Triangles: an extended Combinatorial Reciprocity Theorem”, arXiv:1207.4437 (2012).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture holds for all n ≥ 1 and 1 ≤ i ≤ n − 1. Here α(n; k₁, …, kₙ) is the polynomial that counts monotone triangles with bottom row k₁ < … < kₙ, evaluated at a row with one descent. The proof finds a specific identity among four evaluations of α at neighbouring rows (the four-position identity W1) and certifies it with Fischer's operator formula, by the method of Fischer–Riegler's Lemmas 8.1–8.2; it turns each successive difference in i into a value of α at a row with a repeated entry, which equals the total number A_n of alternating sign matrices, and telescoping from Riegler's published case i = 1 gives the formula. Lean defines α by Fischer's operator formula, proves that it counts monotone triangles at strictly increasing rows, and proves the identity over the full printed range, with A_{n,j} given as α at the deletion rows (and, in a second endpoint, as monotone-triangle counts).
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $A_{n,j}$ denote the number of alternating-sign matrices of size $n$ whose unique $1$ in the first row is in column $j$, and let $n\geq1$ and $i=1,\ldots,n-1$. **Hole-one-descent conjecture.** The identity $$ \alpha(n+1;1,2,\ldots,i-1,i+1,i,i+1,\ldots,n)=-\sum_{j=1}^{n}(j-i)A_{n,j} $$ should hold. The source proves the case $i=1$ and obtains part of the general decomposition, but explicitly says that the remaining contribution from two structural classes is an open problem.
- **Lean endpoint:** `MT.riegler_conjecture6`, `MT.riegler_conjecture6_mt`, `MT.alpha_eq_mtNum`
- **Replay record:** `monotone-triangle-323800-root-replay-opus55-20261123-r1 (root, Claude Opus 5.5; public-claim-100/replay-323800.log all_ok, 17 modules)`; acceptance record `monotone-triangle-hole-one-descent-323800-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/323800-monotone-triangle-hole-one-descent`
- **Prior art searched:** `t1-sol-cross-323800-sol62-20261123-r2/CROSS-REVIEW-323800.md (GPT-6.2 Sol, the second model, round 2 after round 1 stopped at the dissertation: CONFIRMED_TIER1, not routine 0.59, correct 0.999, not public 0.96; all four certificates reproduced and the conjecture checked for every index through n = 9; paper PASS WITH EDITORIAL EDITS); revised paper used`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (candidate review and final check); see lane queries and routes recorded
- **Adversarial verdicts:** PASS (t1-sol-cross-323800-sol62-20261123-r2/CROSS-REVIEW-323800.md (GPT-6.2 Sol, the second model, round 2 after round 1 stopped at the dissertation: CONFIRMED_TIER1, not routine 0.59, correct 0.999, not public 0.96; all four certificates reproduced and the conjecture checked for every index through n = 9; paper PASS WITH EDITORIAL EDITS); revised paper used)
- **Status note:** round 3, accepted 165; Riegler Conjecture 6 (hole-one-descent identity for the monotone-triangle polynomial) proved for all i by a new four-position identity certified with Fischer's operator formula and telescoping from i = 1; proof, same-model review and Lean by Opus (17 modules; alpha by the operator formula, counting bridge at increasing rows proved); cross-model review and final check by GPT-6.2 Sol (two rounds): CONFIRMED, not routine 0.59; polynomial uniqueness and the ASM bijection cited; dissertation still states it open
- **Caveats the row carries:**
  - Lean proves the statement with α defined by Fischer's operator formula; that this α counts monotone triangles at increasing rows is proved in Lean, while two standard identifications are on paper only: polynomiality and uniqueness (so that the operator formula is Riegler's polynomial also at the descent row), and the classical bijection between monotone triangles and alternating sign matrices behind A_{n,j}. ### 324112 · The three conjectural sporadics in the entropic order on a parabolic quotient of $S_6$

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 137)
- **Source:** Gary McConnell, “An entropic partial order on a parabolic quotient of S6”, arXiv:1209.2674 (2012).
- **What is proved (exact):** All three relations hold on the whole closed ordered domain, ties and zero entries included. Arguments that use only convexity of x log x (majorization, Schur concavity, Karamata) cannot work, because the inequalities fail when x log x is replaced by x squared, as the source remarks, and the author wrote that these three relations do not yield to the identric-mean technique that proved the others. The proof reduces to a = b by the sign of the derivative in a, uses concavity along a direction that keeps the positive marginals fixed to reduce to five faces of the domain, treats each face by one-variable monotonicity, and on the hardest face uses a weighted gradient bound (log x >= 1 - 1/x with an explicit nonnegative polynomial) followed along the curves A/C^2 = constant. Lean states the mutual information with Mathlib's negMulLog, keeps the joint-entropy term, fixes the direction of the order, and writes the four arrays out explicitly.
- **MathDB record statement:** In the above notation, let the displayed arrays denote the corresponding elements of the parabolic quotient, and let $\rhd$ denote the entropic partial order. **Three sporadic entropic-order conjectures.** The following three relations hold: $$ \begin{array}{rcl} \mathbf{37}\rhd\mathbf{11}:\quad \left(\begin{array}{ccc}a & c & f\\b & d & e\end{array}\right)&\rhd&\left(\begin{array}{ccc}a & b & d\\f & c & e\end{array}\right),\\ \mathbf{43}\rhd\mathbf{11}:\quad \left(\begin{array}{ccc}a & d & e\\b & c & f\end{array}\right)&\rhd&\left(\begin{array}{ccc}a & b & d\\f & c & e\end{array}\right),\\ \mathbf{49}\rhd\mathbf{11}:\quad \left(\begin{array}{ccc}a & d & f\\b & c & e\end{array}\right)&\rhd&\left(\begin{array}{ccc}a & b & d\\f & c & e\end{array}\right). \end{array} $$ These are the remaining sporadic relations identified in the paper; the preceding discussion explains that the author was…
- **Lean endpoint:** `McConnell.mcconnell_conj19`
- **Replay record:** `entropic-order-324112-root-replay-opus55-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Opus 5.5; public-claim-100/replay-324112.log all_ok, 17 modules)`; acceptance record `entropic-order-s6-sporadics-324112-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/324112-entropic-order-s6-sporadics`
- **Prior art searched:** `t1-prelean-X30-opus-20261120-r1/REVIEW-324112.md (Claude Opus 5.5, before the Lean was finished: every step checked with exact polynomial identities and numerics; two harmless coefficient typos found; convexity-only routes shown impossible; required Lean endpoint; CONFIRMED_TIER1 provisional, not routine 0.60)`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (candidate review and final check); see lane queries and routes recorded

- **Status note:** round 3, accepted 137; proof of McConnell's three sporadic entropic-order relations 37, 43, 49 > 11 (Conjecture 19) on the closed domain; two Lean stages (17 modules); Opus CONFIRMED_TIER1 before Lean (not routine 0.60), endpoint matched by root; Sol final check PASS, arrays derived independently; modest; ProbXiv unchecked

### 324571 · Equal-distribution pair discrepancy bound

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 56)
- **Source:** Richard Arratia and Stephen DeSalvo, “On the Random Sampling of Pairs, with Pedestrian examples”, arXiv:1211.6486 (2013).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every discrete probability law p on a countable colour set, the total variation distance between the common colour of two independent p-draws conditioned to match and the first chronological opposite-side matching colour in independent strictly alternating left/right arrivals is at most 255/256. The supremum over such laws is therefore strictly less than one.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** LITERAL_STRONGER
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $D({\bf p},{\bf q})$ denote the discrepancy between the color distributions of the matching left-right pair when the left and right shoes have distributions ${\bf p}$ and ${\bf q}$. In the equal-distribution case, write $D({\bf p},{\bf p})$. **Equal-distribution discrepancy bound.** One should have $$ \sup_{\bf p}D({\bf p},{\bf p})<1. $$ This is the analogue of the universal discrepancy bound under the constraint that the two shoe distributions agree. The paper describes it as plausible and leaves it open.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Shoe.sourceDiscrepancy_bound`, `Shoe.source_strict_universal_supremum`, `Shoe.countable_color_strict_universal_supremum`
- **Replay record:** `see shoe-discrepancy-acceptance.json`; acceptance record `shoe-discrepancy-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/324571-uniform-discrepancy-bound-for-equal-law-alternating-shoe-pai`
- **Kernel-checked versus paper-only:** Lean checks the product law, conditioning, chronological stopping, null-event fallback independence, total variation, countable relabelling and bound. The paper supplies the same argument and comparison with optimal-constant and collision-time questions.
- **Prior art searched:** `uniform-prior-art-sweep-B5-sol62-20261119-r1`; sweep verdict PARTIAL_OVERLAP; 41 queries and routes recorded; routes refused: MathDB direct — not checked (documented automated-client refusal).; Google Scholar /scholar — disallowed for automated clients by robots.txt. Pilot on 352972 returned HTTP 429.; arXiv keyword API — export.arxiv.org robots.txt disallows all paths for automated…
- **Closest prior work read:** Arratia and DeSalvo, On the Random Sampling of Pairs, with Pedestrian Examples, AAP 47, Theorem 3 and §9 (full text) https://doi.org/10.1239/aap/1427814592
- **Adversarial verdicts:** SOLID (adversarial-triage-E6-sol62-20261119-r1); CONFIRMED_TIER1 (tier1-confirm-G2-opus-20261119-r1, confidence medium)
- **Status note:** G2 Opus confirmed, medium confidence: ten-line argument giving a weak bound 255/256; follows from no published theorem
- **Caveats the row carries:**

### 325374 · Lower bounds for the eigenvalue growth rate when p equals 2

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 176)
- **Source:** Kevin Garbe, “Patterns In The Coefficients Of Powers Of Polynomials Over A Finite Field”, arXiv:1304.4635 (2013).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture holds for every polynomial over the field with two elements that has at least two nonzero terms: the growth rate λ of the number of nonzero coefficients in the first 2^k powers of f is at least 3, it equals 3 exactly when f is similar to 1+x, there is no value strictly between 3 and 1+√5, and it equals 1+√5 exactly when f is similar to 1+x+x^2. The bound λ ≥ 3 is short: after removing common powers every state of the section recursion has at least three children. The gap and the two classifications need more: multiplicities are projected modulo the direction of f, each three-way choice is deferred for a fixed number of steps, and states are weighted by (1+√5) to the number of pending choices; a count to any fixed depth cannot give 1+√5, as the polynomials 1+x+x^(2^m) show. For monomials the rate is 2 and for the zero polynomial it is 1, so the first sentence of the conjecture as printed fails there; the source's own tables and remarks concern non-monomials only. Lean defines the cumulative count, the growth rate as the limit of its k-th root along 2^k, and similarity…
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $f(x)$ be a polynomial and let $\lambda=\lambda(f)$ be the associated growth rate. Two polynomials are **similar** when they are related by the transformations described in the paper. **Eigenvalue-bound conjecture.** When $p=2$, $\lambda\geq 3$. Moreover, $\lambda=3$ only for polynomials similar to $1+x$. If $p=2$ and $\lambda>3$, then $\lambda\geq 1+\sqrt{5}$; moreover, $\lambda=1+\sqrt{5}$ only if $f(x)$ is similar to $1+x+x^2$. These numerical bounds are inferred from computations and are presented as conjectural classifications of the first possible values of $\lambda$.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Garbe.garbe_conj_3_7`, `Garbe.garbe_monomial`, `Garbe.garbe_zero`

### 329202 · Generalisation of the frustrated-triangle extremal conjecture to frustrated cycles

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 68)
- **Source:** Teeradej Kittipassorn and Gabor Meszaros, “Frustrated Triangles”, arXiv:1411.1749 (2015).
- **What is proved (exact):** For each integer k≥3 and t≥0 there is N(k,t)≥t+2 such that for all n≥N and all simple graphs G on n vertices, if the count of ambient injective unoriented k-cycles with an odd number of edges in G is strictly below that of the (t+1)-edge star plus isolates, then G or its whole complement differs from a complete bipartite graph in at most t unordered vertex pairs. Empty bipartition parts are allowed.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** LITERAL
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $G$ be a graph, let $\overline{G}$ denote its complement, and for $k\geq 3$ let $f_k(G)$ be the number of frustrated $k$-cycles, where a cyclic ordering $v_1v_2\dots v_k$ is frustrated when an odd number of its consecutive cycle edges belongs to $G$. For integers $t\geq 0$, let a $(t+1)$-star on $n$ vertices be the graph consisting of a star with $t+1$ leaves together with the remaining isolated vertices. **Frustrated-cycle generalisation conjecture.** For every $k\geq 3$, $t\geq 0$, and all sufficiently large $n$, if $$ f_k(G)<f_k\big((t+1)\text{-star on }n\text{ vertices}\big), $$ then either $G$ or $\overline{G}$ can be obtained from a complete bipartite graph by flipping at most $t$ edges or non-edges. This proposes an extension of the paper's structural result for frustrated triangles to frustrated cycles; its resolution is not given in the source.

### 330858 · The Q-index extremal conjecture for $K_{t,s+1}$-free graphs

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 62)
- **Source:** Maria Aguieiras A. de Freitas, Vladimir Nikiforov and Laura Patuzzi, “Maxima of the Q-index: graphs with no K_s,t”, arXiv:1507.00625 (2015).
- **What is proved (exact):** For integers s >= t-1 >= 1 there is N(s,t) such that every K_{t,s+1}-free graph G of order n >= N(s,t) satisfies q(G) <= n/2 + s + t - 2 + (1/2) sqrt((n-2+2s)^2 - 8s(n-2) + 4(t-1)(n-t+1)); among K_{t,s+1}-free graphs, equality holds exactly for joins of K_{t-1} with an s-regular graph of order n-t+1.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** READING
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $G$ be a graph of order $n$, with adjacency matrix $A$ and diagonal degree matrix $D$. Its signless Laplacian is $Q(G)=A+D$, and its $Q$-index $q(G)$ is the largest eigenvalue of $Q(G)$. A graph is **$K_{t,s+1}$-free** if it contains no subgraph isomorphic to the complete bipartite graph $K_{t,s+1}$. Let $s\geq t-1\geq1$, and let $n$ be sufficiently large. **The Q-index extremal conjecture.** If $G$ is a $K_{t,s+1}$-free graph of order $n$, then $$ q(G)\leq\frac{n}{2}+s+t-2+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\left(n-2+2s\right)^2-8s\left(n-2\right)+4\left(t-1\right)\left(n-t+1\right)}. $$ Equality holds if and only if $G$ is the join of $K_{t-1}$ and an $s$-regular graph of order $n-t+1$. The conjecture is proposed as a spectral counterpart to extremal problems for $K_{s,t}$-free graphs; the authors state that they expect Problem C to be resolved completely in the next few years, but the asserted bou…
- **Lean endpoint:** `QIdx.qindex_kts_endpoint`, `QIdx.qIndex_isGreatest`

### 331692 · Extremal maximal-matchings conjecture for benzenoid chains

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 108)
- **Source:** Tomislav Došlić and Taylor Short, “Maximal matchings in polyspiro and benzenoid chains”, arXiv:1511.00590 (2015).
- **What is proved (exact):** Among benzenoid chains with n hexagons, the helicene has the fewest maximal matchings. Lean proves: for every finite simple graph G carrying a hexagon-chain witness of length n (an ordered list of n six-cycles covering all vertices and edges, consecutive ones sharing exactly one edge, non-consecutive ones disjoint, the two shared edges of an interior hexagon disjoint), the number of inclusion-maximal matchings of the explicit helicene graph with n hexagons is at most that of G. The equivalence between the source's definition (a plane graph whose bounded faces are hexagons arranged in a chain) and the existence of such a witness, and the identification of the Lean helicene with the source's helicene, are proved in the paper in prose, because Mathlib has no plane embeddings. The minimizer is not unique at n = 4 (the zigzag chain ties at 288); for n = 5 to 10 the helicene and its mirror image are the only minimizers (referee's enumeration of 9,841 chains).
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $B_n$ be a benzenoid chain of length $n$, and let $H_n$ denote the helicene benzenoid chain of length $n$. For a graph $G$, write $Psi(G)$ for its number of maximal matchings. **Extremal maximal-matchings conjecture.** For every benzenoid chain $B_n$ of length $n$, $$ \Psi(H_n) \leq \Psi(B_n). $$ The conjecture asserts that the helicene has the smallest number of maximal matchings among all benzenoid chains of the same length. The surrounding discussion presents this as an unresolved extremal problem and notes that maximal matchings appear to decrease as the number of kinky hexagons increases, while their value may also depend on the direction of successive turns.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Helicene.MathDB331692`
- **Replay record:** `helicene-matchings-331692-root-final-replay-fable-20261119-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2)`; acceptance record `helicene-minimizes-maximal-matchings-331692-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/331692-helicene-minimizes-maximal-matchings`
- **Prior art searched:** `candidates-review-R4-opus-20261119-r1/REVIEW-331692.md (Claude Opus 5.5, PASS; required the graph-with-witness endpoint)`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (candidate review and final check); see lane queries and routes recorded
- **Adversarial verdicts:** PASS (candidates-review-R4-opus-20261119-r1/REVIEW-331692.md (Claude Opus 5.5, PASS; required the graph-with-witness endpoint))
- **Status note:** replacement, accepted 108; Lean endpoint over graphs with a hexagon-chain witness; equivalence with the source plane-graph definition is prose (referee: sound and plainly stated); no uniqueness claim
- **Caveats the row carries:**

An independent referee judged that bridge sound and complete. No uniqueness claim (the helicene ties with the zigzag chain at n = 4). Chen and Zhang (Filomat 2025) still list the problem as open.

### 335033 · Asymptotic dumbbell conjecture for the minimum sum-Balaban index

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 67)
- **Source:** Martin Knor, Jaka Kranjc, Riste Škrekovski and Aleksandra Tepeh, “On the minimum value of sum-Balaban index”, arXiv:1701.02716 (2017).
- **What is proved (exact):** Every eventually minimizing sequence of positive-integer dumbbell triples (a,b,a′) with a+b+a′=n and a≤a′ satisfies a/√n,a′/√n→(√2 log(1+√2))^(1/4) and b/n→1 as n→∞. The minimized objective is the sum-Balaban index of the graph formed by disjoint cliques and a disjoint b-vertex path, with every vertex of each clique attached to its respective path endpoint.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** LITERAL_STRONGER
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $D_{a,b,a'}$ be a dumbbell graph on $n$ vertices, with clique sizes $a$ and $a'$ and connecting path size $b$, so that $a+b+a'=n$. Let $$ c=\sqrt[4]{\sqrt 2\log\big(1+\sqrt 2\big)}. $$ **Asymptotic dumbbell conjecture.** Among all dumbbell graphs $D_{a,b,a'}$ on $n$ vertices, the minimum is achieved for one with $$ a=c\sqrt n+o(\sqrt n),\qquad a'=c\sqrt n+o(\sqrt n),\qquad b=n-o(n). $$ This conjecture gives the predicted asymptotic shape of an optimal dumbbell graph: the two cliques have equal asymptotic size, while the path contains almost all vertices. Its status is not resolved in the supplied text.

### 335309 · The eventual non-$\ell$-distance-balance conjecture for generalized Petersen graphs

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 141)
- **Source:** Stefko Miklavic and Primoz Sparl, “-distance-balanced graphs”, arXiv:1702.05257 (2017).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every k ≥ 2 and n > n_k, GP(n,k) is connected and is not ℓ-distance-balanced for any 1 ≤ ℓ < D; at n = n_k some 1 ≤ ℓ < D is balanced, so n_k is the least threshold. Published work covers only k = 3, 4 (arXiv:2309.01900) and distance 1 with weaker distance-2 bounds (Ma, Wang and Klavžar, arXiv:2407.02635). The proof gives an explicit distance formula for GP(n,k) with n ≥ k(k+2), uses the rotation and reflection automorphisms to cancel the common layer-distance profiles of a pair at distance ℓ, and reduces |W_xy| − |W_yx| to sparse 'axis pulses' whose sum is shown to be nonzero for every 1 ≤ ℓ < D by explicit witnesses; k = 2 and the sharpness at n_k are handled by finite certificates and explicit balanced distances. Lean defines GP(n,k) on Bool × ZMod n with Mathlib's SimpleGraph, W_xy as the vertices strictly closer to x, and proves connectivity for all n > n_k so that the diameter condition cannot hold vacuously.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $GP(n,k)$ be the generalized Petersen graph, let $d$ denote graph distance, and let $D$ be its diameter. For an integer $k\geq 2$, define $$ n_k=\begin{cases}11,&k=2,\\(k+1)^2,&k\text{ odd},\\k(k+2),&k\geq4\text{ even}. \end{cases} $$ **The eventual non-$\ell$-distance-balance conjecture.** For any $n>n_k$, the graph $GP(n,k)$ is not $\ell$-distance-balanced for any integer $\ell$ with $1\leq\ell<D$. Moreover, $n_k$ is the smallest integer with this property. The conjecture formalizes computational evidence that, for each fixed $k$, sufficiently large generalized Petersen graphs are diameter-distance-balanced but fail to be $\ell$-distance-balanced at every smaller distance.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Petersen.miklavic_sparl_4_4`
- **Replay record:** `petersen-335309-root-replay-opus55-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Opus 5.5; public-claim-100/replay-335309.log all_ok, 77 modules)`; acceptance record `petersen-eventual-nonbalance-335309-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/335309-petersen-eventual-nonbalance`

### 335354 · Arithmetic conjectures for the normalized denominators of the Fabius function

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 127)
- **Source:** Juan Arias de Reyna, “Arithmetic of the Fabius function”, arXiv:1702.06487 (2017).
- **What is proved (exact):** All three clauses of Conjecture 16 of Arias de Reyna on the common denominators of the values of the Fabius function at dyadic rationals hold: the stated coincidence of consecutive terms, the divisibility by 2(2n - 1)!, and the oddness and integrality of the normalized sequence H_n; moreover D_n is the least integer clearing all values of the function at the dyadic points of level n. Lean proves that a function satisfying the Fabius functional equation exists and is unique, derives its dyadic values from the moment recurrence, and proves the three clauses. The new mathematics is a triangular convolution with the Thue-Morse sequence, a propagation step through integer-valued polynomials, and a p-adic lemma on the first weighted minimum of a sequence of valuations.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $D_n$ be the denominator sequence associated with the rational values of the Fabius function, and define $$ A_n=2^{-\binom{n}{2}}D_n. $$ For $n\geq 1$, set $K_n=A_{2n-1}$; thus $K_1=A_1$, and for $n\geq 2$, $$ K_n=2^{-\binom{2n-1}{2}}D_{2n-1}=2^{-\binom{2n-2}{2}}D_{2n-2}. $$ Finally, define $$ H_n=\frac{K_n}{2(2n-1)!}. $$ **Conjectured arithmetic properties.** The following statements appear to be true: $A_{2n}=A_{2n+1}$ for $n\geq 1$; $2(2n-1)!$ divides $K_n$; and $H_n$ is an odd integer. These claims describe a proposed parity and divisibility pattern for the normalized denominator sequence arising in the arithmetic of the Fabius function. The supplied text presents them as observations believed to hold, but gives no proof or resolution.
- **Lean endpoint:** `FabiusProofLane.arias_de_reyna_conjecture_16`, `FabiusProofLane.mathdb335354_arithmetic`, `Fabius.existsUnique_fabius`
- **Replay record:** `fabius-denominators-335354-root-replay-fable-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2; public-claim-100/replay-335354.log and replay-335354-resume.log: all 39 modules compile and kernel-check)`; acceptance record `fabius-common-denominators-335354-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/335354-fabius-common-denominators`

### 335487 · Unboundedness conjecture for the 2-adic valuations of $b_m(n)$

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 160)
- **Source:** Maciej Gawron, Piotr Miska and Maciej Ulas, “Arithmetic properties of coefficients of power series expansion of _n=0^(1-x^2^n)^t (with an Appendix by Andrzej Schinzel)”, arXiv:1703.01955 (2017).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture is false, and not just at one value: for every m = 2^K(4^j + 1) − 1 with K ≥ 4 and j ≥ 1 (none of the form 2^k − 1), 32 never divides b_m(n), so ν₂(b_m(n)) ≤ 4 for all n; the same holds for m = 11. Here b_m(n) is the number of m-coloured binary partitions of n, the coefficient of x^n in the m-th power of the generating function of partitions into powers of 2. The proof reduces b_m(n) modulo 32 to a finite automaton reading the binary digits of n (from the Mahler functional equation (1 − x)H(x) = H(x²)), and handles all j at once by a second finite-state argument on pairs of digits of n and of the exponent, using a closed set of 31 polynomials. Lean defines binary partitions as partitions into powers of 2 (checked against the first values of OEIS A018819), proves the functional equation bridge, and checks the certificates in the kernel. Sobolewski and Ulas (Results Math. 2023) proved automaticity at each fixed modulus and stated that boundedness was not known for any admissible m.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $b_m(n)$ be the sequence considered in the paper and let $m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq2}$ be such that $m$ is not of the form $2^k-1$ for any $k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}$. **Unbounded valuation conjecture.** The sequence $$ \bigl(\nu_2(b_m(n))\bigr)_{n\in\mathbb{N}} $$ is unbounded. This is proposed as a generalization of the preceding valuation observations, with the excluded values $m=2^k-1$ forming a separate family. The paper gives no general proof.
- **Lean endpoint:** `mathdb_335487_refuted`, `not_conjecture53`, `b11_not_dvd`, `family_not_dvd`, `family_counterexample`
- **Replay record:** `binary-partitions-335487-root-replay-opus55-20261123-r1 (root; public-claim-100/replay-335487.log all_ok, 10 modules)`; acceptance record `binary-partition-valuations-bounded-335487-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/335487-binary-partition-valuations-bounded`

### 336183 · Variance-optimal functions on trees are centered at a vertex

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 101)
- **Source:** Matthew Yancey, “Probabilistic and Geometrical Applications to Graph Theory”, arXiv:1705.09725 (2017).
- **What is proved (exact):** Conjecture 1.4 of Yancey (every variance-optimal function on a tree is centred at a vertex r, with |X(u) - X(r)| = d(u, r) for all u, and r of degree at least 3 when the tree is not a path) is false. On an explicit tree with 34 vertices a feasible function has score 14892, while every vertex-centred function has score at most 14888 (the best centres are vertices 0, 2 and 7), so no variance-optimal function on this tree has a centre. Lean proves that an optimal function exists on the witness and that the first clause fails for every optimum, hence the negation of the whole conjecture. The result contradicts Lemma 19 of Farhadi, Louis, Singh and Tetali (arXiv:2003.05582v3), whose proof treats three sign choices as independent; on the witness the three moves change the variance by -1/289, -72/289 and -1/289 rather than giving the claimed gain of 71/289. Every tree with at most 18 vertices satisfies the conjecture (referee's unpublished computation).
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $T$ be a tree and let $X$ be a variance-optimal function over $T$. For vertices $u,r\in V(T)$, write $d(u,r)$ for their graph distance. **Tree characterization conjecture.** There exists a vertex $r$ such that, for every vertex $u\in V(T)$, $$ |X(u)-X(r)|=d(u,r). $$ Moreover, if $T$ is not a path, then $r$ may be chosen so that $d(r)\geq 3$. The conjecture is proposed as a simple characterization of variance-optimal functions on trees and as a strengthening of the preceding structural result about hairs. The supplied text gives no resolution.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Yancey.mathdb336183`, `Yancey.counterexample`
- **Replay record:** `yancey-variance-336183-root-final-replay-fable-20261119-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2)`; acceptance record `yancey-variance-optimal-tree-centre-336183-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/336183-yancey-variance-optimal-tree-centre`
- **Prior art searched:** `shard018-020-candidates-review-opus-20261119-r1/REVIEW-336183.md (Claude Opus 5.5, PASS, FLAGS none)`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (candidate review and final check); see lane queries and routes recorded
- **Adversarial verdicts:** PASS (shard018-020-candidates-review-opus-20261119-r1/REVIEW-336183.md (Claude Opus 5.5, PASS, FLAGS none))
- **Status note:** replacement, accepted 101; review PASS no flags + final check
- **Caveats the row carries:**
  - Bounded literature and registry search only; no erratum or later paper on Lemma 19 of Farhadi-Louis-Singh-Tetali was found. The statement that every tree with at most 18 vertices satisfies the conjecture is an unpublished computation by the referee and is not kernel-checked. No claim that 34 vertices is minimal.

### 337410 · The incremental conjecture for set multipartite Ramsey numbers

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 128)
- **Source:** Claudia J. F. Gonçalves, Emerson L. Monte Carmelo and Irene N. Nakaoka, “Projective planes and set multipartite Ramsey numbers for C_4 versus star”, arXiv:1710.06383 (2017).
- **What is proved (exact):** For all s >= 2 and n >= 2, M_s(n+1) <= M_s(n) + 1, where M_s(n) is the least positive c such that every spanning subgraph G of the complete multipartite graph with c classes of s vertices either contains a 4-cycle or leaves some vertex with at least n non-neighbours in other classes (a star K_{1,n} in the complement relative to the host). This is Conjecture 2.3 of Goncalves, Monte Carmelo and Nakaoka, who proved the inequality only under an additional numerical hypothesis by keeping the original partition. The proof here proves the stronger step that the property at (n, c) implies the property at (n+1, c+1) by abandoning the partition: delete s suitable vertices and re-partition the rest into c independent s-sets, by a one-vertex swap forced by C4-freeness when s >= 3, and for s = 2 by a friendship-type lemma (two vertices with no common neighbour) with a perfect matching in the complement, plus explicit small cases. Lean defines the host graph, the property and the minimum literally, and proves that the minimum exists.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $s\geq 2$ and $n\geq 2$ be integers. For positive integers $s,n$, let $M_s(n)$ denote the set multipartite Ramsey number for $C_4$ versus the star $K_{1,n}$, namely the least $c$ such that every spanning subgraph $G$ of the complete balanced $s$-partite graph $K_{c\times s}$ contains a copy of $C_4$ or its complement contains a copy of $K_{1,n}$. **Incremental conjecture.** For any integers $s\geq 2$ and $n\geq 2$, $$ M_s(n+1)\leq M_s(n)+1. $$ The preceding proposition proves this bound under an additional numerical hypothesis; the conjecture asserts that the hypothesis can be removed.
- **Lean endpoint:** `MultipartiteRamsey.conj_2_3`, `MultipartiteRamsey.ramseyProp_exists`
- **Replay record:** `set-multipartite-ramsey-337410-root-replay-fable-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2; public-claim-100/replay-337410.log all_ok)`; acceptance record `set-multipartite-ramsey-step-bound-337410-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/337410-set-multipartite-ramsey-step-bound`

### 337947 · Solid $k$-minor essentiality conjecture

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 151)
- **Source:** Anna Brosowsky, Sunita Chepuri and Alex Mason, “Parametrizations of k-Nonnegative Matrices: Cluster Algebras and k-Positivity Tests”, arXiv:1712.05037 (2021).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every n, every 1 ≤ k ≤ n and every solid position, there is an n×n matrix (rational for rational t) whose distinguished solid k-minor is 0 while every other minor of order at most k is positive, so every k-positivity test made only of minors must contain all solid k-minors. The source proved k ≤ 3 with ε-power constructions around a 0/1 central block and noted that its method fails beyond k = 3. The new construction perturbs a weighted rank-(k−1) polynomial moment kernel by two opposite rank-one terms built from a discrete binomial spline with exactly one zero divided difference; with two opposite perturbations the leading term of every other minor is a sum of two nonnegative quantities, at least one positive (one perturbation provably fails). Lean states minors over sorted index sets and k-essential as in the source, and proves the statement for every n, k and position by a general argument (a function-sum expansion in place of Cauchy–Binet, which the pinned Mathlib lacks; Lagrange divided differences; a continuity argument for the common parameter).
- **MathDB record statement:** Let a **solid $k$-minor** be a $k\times k$ minor using consecutive rows and consecutive columns, and call a minor **$k$-essential** if it is necessary in a $k$-positivity test in the sense developed in the paper. **Solid-minor essentiality conjecture.** Solid $k$-minors are $k$-essential. The conjecture is motivated by the preceding construction of $k$-positivity tests and the observed prevalence of solid $k$-minors; proving it would identify a distinguished class of minors that every such test must include.
- **Lean endpoint:** `KEss.solid_minors_k_essential`
- **Replay record:** `k-essential-337947-root-replay-opus55-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Opus 5.5; public-claim-100/replay-337947.log all_ok, 6 modules)`; acceptance record `solid-k-minors-k-essential-337947-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/337947-solid-k-minors-k-essential`
- **Prior art searched:** `t1-prelean-X41-opus-20261120-r1/REVIEW-337947.md (Claude Opus 5.5, before the Lean: exact check of every n <= 8, k and position, 783,069 minors; CONFIRMED_TIER1 provisional, not routine 0.65)`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (candidate review and final check); see lane queries and routes recorded

- **Status note:** round 3, accepted 151; proof of Brosowsky-Chepuri-Mason Conjecture 4.6 (solid k-minors are k-essential, every k); Sol proof, Opus review (not routine 0.65), Lean 6 modules; final check by a separate Opus lane (same family; Sol unavailable) PASS

Wording: the MathDB record paraphrases k-essential as 'necessary in a k-positivity test'; the result concerns tests made of minors, not tests that may use non-minors. ### 338558 · The generic-rank conjecture for bipartite circulant graphs

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 117)
- **Source:** Daniel Irving Bernstein, Grigoriy Blekherman and Rainer Sinn, “Typical and Generic Ranks in Matrix Completion”, arXiv:1802.09513 (2019).
- **What is proved (exact):** For all n > 0 and 0 <= l <= n, the bipartite circulant graph G(n, l) has generic completion rank n - floor(sqrt(n(n - l))), the value predicted by the dimension count. This is Conjecture 5.6 of Bernstein, Blekherman and Sinn, who proved it for special families and named the step that was missing. The upper bound uses a new construction: Fourier vectors with an evenly spaced (Beatty) set of frequencies, so that each frequency class has at most d elements, and a Vandermonde system on the missing diagonals that forces the tangent map to be injective. The lower bound is the dimension count. Lean states generic completion rank literally, as a statement about a nonempty Zariski-open set of partial matrices in both directions, and proves the passage from a nonzero Jacobian minor to such an open set.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $G(n,l)$ be a bipartite circulant graph of the form considered in the paper, and let the generic completion rank be the rank attained by a generic matrix completion. The dimension-count prediction is $$ n- \left\lfloor\sqrt{n^2-nl}\right\rfloor. $$ **Bipartite circulant generic-rank conjecture.** Every graph $G(n,l)$ has generic completion rank predicted by the dimension count; equivalently, $$ \operatorname{gcr}(G(n,l))=n-\left\lfloor\sqrt{n^2-nl}\right\rfloor. $$ The paper proves the dimension-count prediction for a subset of these graphs and conjectures it for all bipartite circulant graphs of this form. The claim is presented as unresolved.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Circulant.mathdb338558`, `Circulant.literalConjecture_proved`
- **Replay record:** `circulant-generic-rank-338558-root-replay-fable-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2; public-claim-100/replay-338558.log all_ok)`; acceptance record `circulant-generic-completion-rank-338558-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/338558-circulant-generic-completion-rank`
- **Prior art searched:** `t1-prelean-X2-opus-20261120-r1/REVIEW-338558.md (Claude Opus 5.5, before the Lean was finished: proof checked, formula confirmed by exact computation for n <= 12, public and routine tests, Lean route designed; provisional CONFIRMED_TIER1) and t1-prelean-X9-opus-20261120-r1/CONFIRM-338558.md (Claude Opus 5.5, after Lean: CONFIRMED_TIER1 final; no definition gap; 90 exact cases agree)`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (candidate review and final check); see lane queries and routes recorded
- **Adversarial verdicts:** PASS (t1-prelean-X2-opus-20261120-r1/REVIEW-338558.md (Claude Opus 5.5, before the Lean was finished: proof checked, formula confirmed by exact computation for n <= 12, public and routine tests, Lean route designed; provisional CONFIRMED_TIER1) and t1-prelean-X9-opus-20261120-r1/CONFIRM-338558.md (Claude Opus 5.5, after Lean: CONFIRMED_TIER1 final; no definition gap; 90 exact cases agree))

Closest later work: Dressler and Krone, arXiv:2010.09777, on real typical ranks of the same graphs, which does not prove the complex formula.

### 339675 · The $d$-sequence conjecture for coefficients of uniform-matroid $Z$-polynomials

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 69)
- **Source:** Alice L. L. Gao, Linyuan Lu, Matthew H. Y. Xie, Arthur L. B. Yang and Philip B. Zhang, “The Kazhdan-Lusztig polynomials of uniform matroids”, arXiv:1806.10852 (2018).
- **What is proved (exact):** For all positive integers m,d, coefficientwise multiplication by the displayed b_m(d,i), with binomials evaluated as real falling products divided by factorials, sends every real polynomial of degree at most d that is zero or splits over ℝ to zero or a polynomial that splits over ℝ. The binomially weighted symbol Σᵢ binom(d,i)b_m(d,i)xⁱ has degree d, constant coefficient 1 and strictly negative real zeros only.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** SOURCE_NOT_RECORD
- **MathDB record statement:** For integers $m$ and $d$, and $0\leq i\leq d$, define $$ b_m(d,i)=\sum_{h=0}^{m-1}\frac{i(h-m+1)+m}{(h+1)m}\binom{i-1+h}{h}\binom{d-i+h}{h}. $$ A finite sequence of real numbers is an $n$-sequence when the associated polynomial has real zeros of one common sign. **The $d$-sequence conjecture.** The sequence $\{b_m(d,i)\}_{i=0}^{d}$ is a $d$-sequence for every $m$ and $d$. The conjecture is proposed as the analogue of the corresponding assertion for Kazhdan–Lusztig polynomials and would support real-rootedness results for uniform-matroid $Z$-polynomials; its general status remains open.

### 340107 · Submultiplicativity conjecture for the primitive-set ratio function

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 104)
- **Source:** Nathan McNew, “Counting primitive subsets and other statistics of the divisor graph of \1,2, n\”, arXiv:1808.04923 (2020).
- **What is proved (exact):** Let Q(k) be the number of primitive subsets of {1,...,k} (no element divides another; the empty set is counted, as in OEIS A051026) and g(k) = Q(k)/Q(k-1) - 1, with g(1) = 1. Then g(nm) <= g(n) g(m) for coprime n, m >= 1, and g(p^(i+1)) <= g(p^i) for every prime p and i >= 0. This is McNew's Conjecture 1. The proof uses an antichain-avoidance monotonicity lemma (an FKG-type correlation inequality) and two block reductions.
- **MathDB record statement:** For $k>1$, let $Q(k)$ be the number of primitive subsets of $\\{1,2,\ldots,k\\}$ and define $$ g(1)=1,\qquad g(k)=\frac{Q(k)}{Q(k-1)}-1. $$ Thus $g(k)$ is the ratio of primitive subsets of $\\{1,2,\ldots,k\\}$ that contain $k$ to those that do not. **Submultiplicativity conjecture for $g$.** The function $g$ is submultiplicative in the following sense: if $(n,m)=1$, then $$ g(nm)\leq g(n)g(m), $$ and, for every prime $p$, $$ g(p^{i+1})\leq g(p^i). $$ These inequalities are proposed as conjectural improved upper-bound tools for the exponential growth constant associated with counting primitive sets. The supplied text gives no resolution of this conjecture.
- **Lean endpoint:** `PrimitiveRatio.mcnew_conj1`
- **Replay record:** `primitive-ratio-340107-root-final-replay-fable-20261119-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2)`; acceptance record `mcnew-primitive-ratio-submultiplicativity-340107-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/340107-mcnew-primitive-ratio-submultiplicativity`
- **Prior art searched:** `candidates-review-R3-opus-20261119-r1/REVIEW-340107.md (Claude Opus 5.5, PASS, FLAGS none)`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (candidate review and final check); see lane queries and routes recorded

### 340121 · Positive proportion conjecture for partitions into MSTD subsets

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 98)
- **Source:** Hung Viet Chu, Noah Luntzlara, Steven J. Miller and Lily Shao, “Infinite Families of Partitions into MSTD Subsets”, arXiv:1808.05460 (2019).
- **What is proved (exact):** PROVED (Chu-Luntzlara-Miller-Shao 2018, Conjecture 1.5): for every fixed k >= 2 there are c_k > 0 and R_k such that for all r >= R_k, at least a proportion c_k of the partitions of {1,...,r} into exactly k nonempty blocks have every block MSTD (|A+A| > |A-A|). The case k = 2 was proved earlier by Asada-Manski-Miller-Suh.
- **MathDB record statement:** A **$k$-decomposition into MSTD subsets** is a partition of $I_r=\{1,2,\ldots,r\}$ into $k$ subsets, each having more pairwise sums than pairwise differences. **Positive proportion conjecture.** For any finite $k\ge 2$, the proportion of $k$-decompositions of $I_r$ into MSTD subsets is bounded below by a positive constant. This would extend the known positive-constant lower bound for $k=2$ and give a uniform abundance result for decompositions into any fixed finite number of MSTD subsets; the source does not establish the conjecture for general $k$.
- **Lean endpoint:** `mathdb_340121`, `mathdb_340121_labelled`, `labelled_positive_proportion`, `conjecture15Count`, `conjecture15`
- **Replay record:** `mstd-340121-root-final-replay-fable-20261119-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2)`; acceptance record `mstd-k-partitions-positive-proportion-340121-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/340121-mstd-k-partitions-positive-proportion`
- **Prior art searched:** `public-tracker-prior-art-sweep-sol62-20261119-r1`; sweep verdict PARTIAL_OVERLAP, top-up verdict PARTIAL_OVERLAP; 59 queries and routes recorded
- **Closest prior work read:** Asada–Manski–Miller–Suh, Theorem 1.4 on bi-MSTD sets (full text) https://arxiv.org/pdf/1509.01657v2#page=3
- **Adversarial verdicts:** CLEARED (closecall-adjudication-A3-opus-20261119-r1, confidence 0.8)
- **Status note:** A3
- **Caveats the row carries:**
  - Asymptotic in r: the proportion is 0 for small r, and the bound holds for r >= R_k.
  - The constants are astronomically small (log10 of 1/c about 3654 for k = 2); nothing is said about the true proportion or its limit.
  - k = 2 is due to Asada-Manski-Miller-Suh. The new ingredient is the antipodal modular k-colouring used as fringes; the rest is the Martin-O'Bryant/Zhao fringe method.
  - Significance modest. The prior-art search was bounded: arXiv abstract sweep, Semantic Scholar citers of the source (10) and of AMMS (12), web, ProbXiv and registries.

### 340621 · The $3$-cube-free density conjecture in $\mathbb{Z}_{2^n}$

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 130)
- **Source:** Jason Long and Adam Zsolt Wagner, “The largest projective cube-free subsets of Z_2^n”, arXiv:1810.01225 (2018).
- **What is proved (exact):** A subset A of the cyclic group Z_{2^n} that contains no 3-dimensional cube, that is no x, y, z (not necessarily distinct, zero allowed) with all seven nonempty subset sums in A, has at most (5/8) 2^n elements, and for every n >= 3 the maximum is exactly 5 * 2^(n-3). This is Conjecture 5.1 of Long and Wagner. The earlier upper bounds were 2/3 (Meng) and, three days before this result, a treatment of the case where the odd layer has density above 2/3 (Randjelovic), with the interval between 5/8 and 2/3 left open. The proof averages a certified weighted inequality for cube-free sets over odd multipliers, decomposes the density by 2-adic layers, treats the high-odd-density branch by a direct counting argument, and inducts over the subgroups 16Z and 32Z; the extremal example is the preimage of five residues modulo 8. The finite inequality is certified by one verified checker over 12,564 states and 1,441 cubes, with the coverage of all cases proved in Lean.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $n$ be a positive integer, and consider subsets of $\mathbb{Z}_{2^n}$ that contain no $3$-cube. **The $3$-cube-free density conjecture.** The largest $3$-cube-free family in $\mathbb{Z}_{2^n}$ has size $(5/8)\cdot 2^n$. Since $L_1\cup L_3$ is $3$-cube-free, the proposed bound would be sharp; the paper states that this is the first open case of the general optimality conjecture.
- **Lean endpoint:** `LongWagner.lw_upper`, `LongWagner.lw_conj_5_1`
- **Replay record:** `long-wagner-cube-340621-root-replay-fable-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2; public-claim-100/replay-340621.log all_ok)`; acceptance record `long-wagner-cube-free-five-eighths-340621-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/340621-long-wagner-cube-free-five-eighths`

### 341096 · Conjectures on successive threshold values for equal-product partitions

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 115)
- **Source:** Byungchul Cha, Adam Claman, Joshua Harrington, Ziyu Liu, Barbara Maldonado, Alexander Miller, Ann Palma, Tony W. H. Wong and Hongkwon V. Yi, “An Investigation on Partitions with Equal Products”, arXiv:1811.07451 (2018).
- **What is proved (exact):** All three parts of Conjecture 5.2 of Cha et al. hold: for every n >= 9, s*_{n-2}(n) = s*_{n-2}(n-1) + 1; for every n >= 7, s*_{n-1}(n) = s*_{n-1}(n-1) + 1; for every n >= 10, s*_n(n) = s*_n(n-1) + 1, where s*_r(n) is the least positive integer beyond which every sum has at least r different partitions into n positive parts with a common product. Both thresholds are shown to exist in every case. The proof has three parts: an elementary stability lemma (below twice the number of parts every partition contains a one, so removing a one is a bijection), explicit infinite families of partitions with equal products that settle all large n, and a certified exhaustive computation for the remaining 77 identities with n <= 34, in which 105 non-existence claims are reduced by padding with ones to 14 enumerations. Lean states partitions as multisets of positive integers and the threshold as the least positive permanent-tail bound, exactly as the source does.
- **MathDB record statement:** For integers $r\geq2$ and $n\geq3$, let $s_r^*(n)$ be the smallest positive integer, if it exists, such that every integer $s\geq s_r^*(n)$ has at least $r$ different $n$-partitions with the same sum and product, without requiring all parts across the partitions to be distinct. **Successive-threshold conjectures.** The following assertions are conjectured: 1. For all integers $n\geq9$, $s_{n-2}^*(n)=s_{n-2}^*(n-1)+1$. 2. For all integers $n\geq7$, $s_{n-1}^*(n)=s_{n-1}^*(n-1)+1$. 3. For all integers $n\geq10$, $s_n^*(n)=s_n^*(n-1)+1$. These statements are motivated by computational data. The inequalities $s_r^*(n)\leq s_r^*(n-1)+1$ are known from the theorem preceding the conjectures, but equality in the three displayed ranges remains open.
- **Lean endpoint:** `EqualProduct.conj52a`, `EqualProduct.conj52b`, `EqualProduct.conj52c`, `EqualProduct.sStar_unique`
- **Replay record:** `equal-product-partitions-341096-root-replay-fable-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2; public-claim-100/replay-341096.log all_ok)`; acceptance record `equal-product-partitions-successive-thresholds-341096-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/341096-equal-product-partitions-successive-thresholds`
- **Prior art searched:** `round2-confirm-G14-opus-20261120-r1/REVIEW-341096.md (Claude Opus 5.5, before Lean: proof re-derived, thresholds recomputed independently, public and routine tests, required Lean endpoint fixed; provisional CONFIRMED_TIER1) and round2-confirm-G23-opus-20261120-r1/CONFIRM.md (Claude Opus 5.5, after Lean: CONFIRMED_TIER1 final; a brute-force transcription of the Lean definitions reproduces all 108 thresholds, 105 failures, 14 base certificates and 77 finite endpoints, and agrees with the earlier independent table on all 88 identities for n <= 37)`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (candidate review and final check); see lane queries and routes recorded
- **Adversarial verdicts:** PASS (round2-confirm-G14-opus-20261120-r1/REVIEW-341096.md (Claude Opus 5.5, before Lean: proof re-derived, thresholds recomputed independently, public and routine tests, required Lean endpoint fixed; provisional CONFIRMED_TIER1) and round2-confirm-G23-opus-20261120-r1/CONFIRM.md (Claude Opus 5.5, after Lean: CONFIRMED_TIER1 final; a brute-force transcription of the Lean definitions reproduces all 108 thresholds, 105 failures, 14 base certificates and 77 finite endpoints, and agrees with the earlier independent table on all 88 identities for n <= 37))

### 342364 · The 2-adic valuation conjecture for even-colored 2-ary partition functions

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 29)
- **Source:** Maciej Ulas and Błażej Żmija, “On p-adic valuations of certain m colored p-ary partition functions”, arXiv:1904.03398 (2019).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every positive n the differences c_m(4n)−c_m(n), using 2m colors for parts of size one and m for each other binary part size, are nonzero and have 2-adic valuation ν₂(n)+2ν₂(k)+3 for m=2k≥2, at least ν₂(n)+3 for m≡1 mod 4, and at least ν₂(n)+6 for m≡3 mod 4. The odd bounds are attained for odd n when m≡1 mod 4 or m≡3,7 mod 16, and for n≡2 mod 4 when m≡11 mod 16, whereas m≡15 mod 16 always gives at least ν₂(n)+7, refuting the per-color infinite-equality reading and proving the some-color reading.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** READING
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $c_m(n)$ denote the $m$-colored binary partition function, and let $ u_2$ be the 2-adic valuation. For positive integers $k,n$ and nonnegative integers $k$ as specified below, consider the differences of the values of $c_m$. **2-adic valuation conjecture.** For $kin\mathbb{N}_{+}$ and each $n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}$, $$ \nu_{2}(c_{2k}(4n)-c_{2k}(n))=\nu_{2}(n)+2\nu_{2}(k)+3. $$ Moreover, for $k\in\mathbb{N}$ and $n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}$, $$ \nu_{2}(c_{4k+1}(4n)-c_{4k+1}(n))\geq\nu_{2}(n)+3, $$ $$ \nu_{2}(c_{4k+3}(4n)-c_{4k+3}(n))\geq\nu_{2}(n)+6. $$ In each case, equality holds for infinitely many $n\in\mathbb{N}$. These predictions are motivated by known sharp congruences for the ordinary binary partition function and numerical computations for $m\leq10$ and $n\leq10^5$; their general validity remains open.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Ulas.Final5.full_theorem_E2 : EvenFormula ∧ OddOneBound ∧ OddThreeBound ∧ OddOneEquality ∧ OddThreeEquality ∧ FifteenBound ∧ NeverFifteen; Ulas.Final5.mathdb_342364 : ¬ Conj61 (per-k reading P1); Ula…`

### 342705 · Caterpillar and balanced species tree growth-factor conjecture

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 73)
- **Source:** Cedric Chauve, Yann Ponty and Michael Wallner, “Counting and sampling gene family evolutionary histories in the duplication-loss and duplication-loss-transfer models”, arXiv:1905.04971 (2019).
- **What is proved (exact):** For each k≥1, one cutoff N(k) works for all unranked rooted full binary k-leaf species trees: for each n≥N(k), the count of source duplication–loss event histories with n extant gene leaves is minimized exactly by the recursively leaf-balanced shape and maximized exactly by the caterpillar. Each species tree’s positive nth-root growth limit is the inverse critical radius, the caterpillar growth divided by k tends to infinity, and the balanced growth lies between 2k and 1000k for all k≥1.

An **unranked species tree** is a species tree considered up to isomorphism, and a **caterpillar tree** and a **balanced species tree** are, respectively, the extreme unbalanced and balanced species-tree shapes of size $k$. The ${\mathbb{D}\mathbb{L}}$-histories are histories in the duplication-loss model. **Caterpillar and balanced species tree conjecture.** For a given $k$ and sufficiently large $n$, the unranked species tree of size $k$ having the largest number of ${\mathbb{D}\mathbb{L}}$-histories of size $n$ is the caterpillar tree. Moreover, the exponential growth factor of the number of histories for a caterpillar of size $k$ grows superlinearly as a function of $k$. Species trees having the smallest number of ${\mathbb{D}\mathbb{L}}$-histories are balanced species trees of size $k$, and the exponential growth factor of the num…

### 343104 · Unique permutation with maximal stabilization

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 110)
- **Source:** Connor Ahlbach, “Tableau Stabilization and Rectangular Tableaux Fixed by Promotion Powers”, arXiv:1907.00105 (2020).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every n >= 1 and every permutation w of size n, the stabilization index of w equals n if and only if w is the identity: the increasing permutation stabilizes at n, and every permutation with a descent stabilizes by n-1. This proves Ahlbach's conjecture that the increasing permutation is the unique permutation with maximal stabilization. Lean defines stabilization through the row-insertion tableau of the k-fold reading word and checks that every entry of the last copy stays in its original row; the identification of this with the source's definition by jeu de taquin rectification is the source's Lemma 2.10 (a classical theorem of Schutzenberger, cited from Fulton), which is not checked in Lean. The hypothesis n >= 1 is needed and matches the source.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $w$ be a permutation of $\{1,2,\dots,n\}$, viewed as a tableau, and let $\stab(w)$ denote its stabilization time. **Maximal-stabilization conjecture.** The increasing permutation $$ w=1\,2\,\dots\,(n-1)\,n $$ is the only permutation with $\stab(w)=n$. This asks for the rightmost entry in the stabilization distribution on permutations to be unique. The statement is posed as an open problem after the paper notes that the leftmost entry is always $1$; its truth is not established there.
- **Lean endpoint:** `TableauStabilization.mathdb_343104`, `TableauStabilization.stab_id`, `TableauStabilization.stab_le_pred_of_ne_one`
- **Replay record:** `tableau-stabilization-343104-root-final-replay-fable-20261119-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2)`; acceptance record `ahlbach-unique-maximal-tableau-stabilization-343104-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/343104-ahlbach-unique-maximal-tableau-stabilization`
- **Prior art searched:** `candidates-review-R6-opus-20261119-r1/REVIEW-343104.md (Claude Opus 5.5, PASS, FLAGS none)`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (candidate review and final check); see lane queries and routes recorded
- **Adversarial verdicts:** PASS (candidates-review-R6-opus-20261119-r1/REVIEW-343104.md (Claude Opus 5.5, PASS, FLAGS none))
- **Status note:** replacement, accepted 110; Lean uses the row-insertion form of stabilization; bridge to rectification is the classical Lemma 2.10 (Schutzenberger, via Fulton), not in Lean; n >= 1
- **Caveats the row carries:**
  - The kernel-checked definition of stabilization is the row-insertion form; the bridge to the source's rectification form is the classical identity Rect(S) = P(reading word of S) (source Lemma 2.10, from Fulton), trusted and not formalized. An independent referee computed stabilization both ways for all 46,233 permutations with n <= 8 and found agreement, and agreement with Ahlbach's published distribution. Bounded literature search; two citation indexes refused requests.

### 343577 · Phase-transition conjecture for unimodality of Fuss–Catalan distributions

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 118)
- **Source:** Wojciech Młotkowski, Noriyoshi Sakuma and Yuki Ueda, “Free self-decomposability and unimodality of the Fuss-Catalan distributions”, arXiv:1908.07887 (2019).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every p > 1 there is r0 with p - 1 < r0 < p such that, for 0 < r <= p, the Fuss-Catalan distribution mu(p, r) is unimodal if and only if r = p or r <= r0. Lean proves this for the measure with the explicit density of the source's Proposition 1.1, formula (2) (due to Forrester and Liu), with unimodality in the source's sense (an atom plus a density nondecreasing before and nonincreasing after the mode). That this measure is the moment-defined distribution mu(p, r), total mass one included, is the source's published Proposition 1.1, cited and not checked in Lean. The proof shows that the logarithmic derivative of the density is strictly decreasing in r, so the unimodal parameters form an interval, and locates its endpoint strictly between p - 1 and p.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $p>1$ and let $\mu(p,r)$ denote the Fuss–Catalan distribution with parameters $p$ and $r$. **Phase-transition conjecture.** For every $p>1$ there exists $r_{0}(p)$ satisfying $$ p-1<r_{0}(p)<p, $$ such that $\mu(p,r)$ is unimodal if and only if either $r=p$ or $0<r\leqslant r_{0}(p)$. This conjecture is suggested by the results in the preceding sections and by numerical experiments; the general phase transition remains open.
- **Lean endpoint:** `FussCatalan.density_measure_phase_transition`, `FussCatalan.mu_supported`, `FussCatalan.mu_ne_zero`
- **Replay record:** `fuss-catalan-unimodality-343577-root-replay-fable-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2; public-claim-100/replay-343577.log all_ok)`; acceptance record `fuss-catalan-unimodality-phase-transition-343577-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/343577-fuss-catalan-unimodality-phase-transition`
- **Prior art searched:** `t1-prelean-X2-opus-20261120-r1/REVIEW-343577.md (Claude Opus 5.5: proof checked, threshold values recomputed, public and routine tests; provisional CONFIRMED_TIER1; proposed the density-defined endpoint as the cheaper option) and t1-prelean-X9-opus-20261120-r1/CONFIRM-343577.md (Claude Opus 5.5, after Lean: CONFIRMED_TIER1 final; the citation boundary judged acceptable because unimodality is invariant under positive scaling; Lean density checked against the source's formula and numerically)`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (candidate review and final check); see lane queries and routes recorded
- **Adversarial verdicts:** PASS (t1-prelean-X2-opus-20261120-r1/REVIEW-343577.md (Claude Opus 5.5: proof checked, threshold values recomputed, public and routine tests; provisional CONFIRMED_TIER1; proposed the density-defined endpoint as the cheaper option) and t1-prelean-X9-opus-20261120-r1/CONFIRM-343577.md (Claude Opus 5.5, after Lean: CONFIRMED_TIER1 final; the citation boundary judged acceptable because unimodality is invariant under positive scaling; Lean density checked against the source's formula and numerically))
- **Status note:** round 3, accepted 118; proof of the unimodality phase-transition conjecture for Fuss-Catalan distributions; Lean (19 modules) is for the measure with the explicit density of the source's Proposition 1.1; the identification with the moment-defined law, normalization included, is that published proposition, cited and not in Lean; Opus CONFIRMED_TIER1 final (0.95 modulo the cited proposition, not routine 0.65)

### 343652 · Strong-product immersion conjecture

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 131)
- **Source:** Karen L. Collins, Megan E. Heenehan and Jessica McDonald, “Clique immersion in graph products”, arXiv:1908.10457 (2019).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture that the immersion number is supermultiplicative for the strong product, im(G) im(H) <= im(G ⊠ H), is false. Let F_a be the complete bipartite graph K_{a,a} with, on each side, one extra vertex shared by each perfect matching (a compressed form of adding a matching). With G = F_4 on 14 vertices and H = F_6 on 22 vertices, im(G) = 8 and im(H) = 12, but K_96 does not immerse in the 308-vertex product G ⊠ H: only 96 vertices of the product have degree at least 95, so they would have to be the branch vertices, and a checkerboard cut separating them 48 against 48 has 2,224 edges while 48 * 48 = 2,304 edge-disjoint paths would have to cross it. Lean defines weak immersions, the immersion number and the strong product, proves the two immersion numbers, the degree and cut counts, and derives the negation of the universal statement.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $G$ and $H$ be graphs, and write $\operatorname{im}(G)=t$ and $\operatorname{im}(H)=r$. **Strong-product immersion conjecture.** Then $$ \operatorname{im}(G\boxtimes H)\geq tr. $$ This conjecture is motivated by the identity $K_t\boxtimes K_r=K_{tr}$ and the resulting immersion number of the strong product of complete graphs; the paper presents it as an unresolved conjecture.

### 344334 · Littlewood-admissibility conjecture for pairs with two interior zeros

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 178)
- **Source:** Kevin G. Hare and Jonas Jankauskas, “On Newman and Littlewood polynomials with prescribed number of zeros inside the unit disk”, arXiv:1910.13994 (2019).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture holds: for 1 ≤ k ≤ n−1, the pair (k, n) is inadmissible exactly when n ≥ 13, n ≡ 1 (mod 6) and k = 2 or k = n−2. There are two halves. Exclusion, for every such n (the source had it only up to n = 31, by exhaustive computation): a Littlewood polynomial with no zero on the circle and exactly two zeros inside is coprime to its reciprocal, so its Schur–Cohn form is nonsingular with exactly two negative directions; the tree of sign prefixes that survive this test is small and repeats with period 12, which is carried to every depth by stationary submatrices and 60 kernel vectors, and every completed word is excluded by an exact certificate. This half is computer-assisted: about 32,000 certificate cases, all checked in the Lean kernel together with proofs that they cover every case. Existence, for every other pair: most cases are theorems of the source; the remaining cases (even k ≥ 12 in two ranges) are filled by a new family of polynomials, whose zero counts are proved in Lean by a root-counting argument on arcs and a strict-dominance comparison. Lean defines Littlewood p…
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $n\geq 1$, and let $(k,n)$ be a valid pair for Littlewood polynomials. A pair is **Littlewood-admissible** if some Littlewood polynomial of degree $n$ has exactly $k$ zeros in the open unit disk and no zeros on the unit circle. **Littlewood-admissibility conjecture.** The pairs $(2,n)$ and $(n-2,n)$ with $n\equiv 1\pmod 6$ and $n\geq 13$ are the only non-trivial Littlewood-inadmissible pairs. Computations establish this classification for $n\leq31$, while the assertion for all larger degrees remains open.
