### 345190 · The unlabelled bridge-addable fragment-size conjecture

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 109)
- **Source:** Colin McDiarmid, “Connectivity for an unlabelled bridge-addable graph class”, arXiv:2001.05256 (2020).
- **What is proved (exact):** McDiarmid's Conjecture 3 (the expected fragment size of a uniformly random unlabelled n-vertex graph from a decomposable, bridge-addable class is bounded by a universal constant) is false: there is one fixed isomorphism-invariant, decomposable, bridge-addable class of graphs, with members at every positive order, whose mean fragment size over isomorphism classes of n-vertex members is unbounded in n. The class consists of graphs whose bridgeless pieces are single vertices or members of a staged family of cyclic-layer graphs with very many isomorphism types; the bound obtained is of order t^2/6 at stage t. The first stage begins at about 2^86 vertices, so the effect is invisible at any computable size (below 2^85 vertices the class is exactly the forests).
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $\mathcal A$ be a class of graphs that is **bridge-addable**, meaning that adding an edge between vertices in distinct components preserves membership, and **decomposable**, meaning that a graph belongs to $\mathcal A$ if and only if each of its components does. For a graph $G$, define its **fragment size** by $$ \operatorname{frag}(G)=|V(G)|-\max\{|V(C)|:C\text{ is a component of }G\}. $$ Let $\tilde{R}_n \in_u\tilde{\mathcal A}$ be sampled uniformly from the unlabelled graphs in $\mathcal A$ on $n$ vertices. **Unlabelled bridge-addable fragment-size conjecture.** For each such graph class $\mathcal A$, there is a constant $c_{\mathcal A}$ such that, for each positive integer $n$, $$ \mathbb E[\operatorname{frag}(\tilde{R}_n)]\leq c_{\mathcal A}. $$ The labelled analogue has a uniform expectation bound, while the source gives an unlabelled example showing that the expectation can g…

### 345199 · The Rail Conjecture for knight's tours

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 155)
- **Source:** Ian Parberry, “Tourneys and the Fast Generation and Obfuscation of Closed Knight's Tours”, arXiv:2001.06044 (2020).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture is false: for every t ≥ 0 the (60+8t)×(60+8t) board has a closed knight's tour with no rails at all. The first refutation, by a GPT-6.1 Sol lane, was a periodic family on boards of side 252+24t with exactly 104 rails, which reduces connectivity to a fixed cycle; a Claude Opus 5.5 review cleared it. The Lean lane (Claude Opus 5.5) certified a stronger construction instead: a periodic template found by a SAT search gives a single 60×60 rail-free tour, and an induction that inserts one 8-column or 8-row period preserves the Hamiltonian cycle and the absence of rails, using two local conditions checked on the reference board. Lean defines the knight graph, the tour as a Hamiltonian cycle (Mathlib's Walk.IsHamiltonianCycle), a rail literally as in the source's Section 4 (an unordered pair of tour edges (v0,v1), (v2,v3) such that (v0,v2) and (v1,v3) are knight moves not in the tour, without a 'parallel' condition, which only enlarges the rail set and so makes zero rails the stronger statement), and the conjecture as ∃ c > 0, ∃ N, ∀ n ≥ N, every closed tour has at least c·n…
- **MathDB record statement:** Let an $n \times n$ knight's tour be a closed knight's tour on an $n \times n$ board, and let a **rail** be the structure defined by the preceding discussion: a pair of edges that can be switched with their complementary pair while preserving vertex degrees. **Rail Conjecture.** An $n \times n$ knight's tour has $\Omega(n)$ rails. This conjecture is motivated by the authors' observation that all knight's tours they examined had many rails; its resolution would clarify the abundance of local switching operations in closed knight's tours.

### 346587 · The half-length uniqueness conjecture for bi-roots

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 129)
- **Source:** Giuseppa Castiglione, Gabriele Fici and Antonio Restivo, “Primitive Sets of Words”, arXiv:2005.10668 (2020).
- **What is proved (exact):** A primitive word w over a finite alphabet has at most one bi-root of at most half its length: if X and U are primitive pairs (two-element bases of 2-maximal submonoids) that both generate w, and twice the total length of each is below |w|, then X = U. This is the half-length uniqueness conjecture of Castiglione, Fici and Restivo, who proved uniqueness only under a square-root length bound. The proof establishes their additive conjecture in a stronger form: two distinct primitive pairs generating the same primitive word have total length at least |w| + 3. It folds the two generator graphs, glues them along the path of w into a graph of rank at most 3, shows that in rank 3 a cycle of the incidence graph would give w a period, so that the incidence graph is a forest and the bound follows by counting, and treats the remaining case by recoding w through first returns and a weighted induction on length that changes the alphabet. Lean states primitive words, generated submonoids, 2-maximality and primitive pairs literally.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $w$ be a primitive word. A **bi-root** of $w$ is a primitive pair $\{x,y\}$ whose concatenations include $w$. **The half-length uniqueness conjecture.** The word $w$ has at most one bi-root $\{x,y\}$ such that $$ |x|+|y|<\frac{|w|}{2}. $$ The preceding theorem proves the analogous uniqueness statement under the stronger bound $|x|+|y|<\sqrt{|w|}$. The supplied text presents the half-length bound as a problem related to the first conjecture, but gives no resolution.
- **Lean endpoint:** `BiRoot.half_length_biroot_unique`, `BiRoot.weighted_primitive_intersection_add_three`
- **Replay record:** `biroot-uniqueness-346587-root-replay-fable-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2; public-claim-100/replay-346587.log all_ok)`; acceptance record `biroot-half-length-uniqueness-346587-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/346587-biroot-half-length-uniqueness`

### 346681 · Fixed-point product formula conjecture for evacuation of Kreweras words

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 113)
- **Source:** Sam Hopkins and Martin Rubey, “Promotion of Kreweras words”, arXiv:2005.14031 (2021).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every n >= 1, the number of Kreweras words of length 3n fixed by dual evacuation equals the product P(n) of Hopkins and Rubey's Conjecture 6.3, and the number fixed by evacuation equals P(n) for even n and 0 for odd n. The proof passes through Stanley's correspondence between evacuation-fixed linear extensions and domino chains of order ideals, then unfolds the reachable states, which fall into three parity sectors, into lattice walks with steps (1,1), (-1,0), (0,-1) that avoid a half-line (the new step), and counts those walks by Bousquet-Melou's slit-plane method: the walk series are T and X*T^2 with T = 1 + 4t^3T^3. Lean formalizes the words, the swap operators, evacuation and dual evacuation literally, with bridge theorems to the source's poset formulation, and proves the walk enumeration for this step set from scratch (61 modules).
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $n\geq 1$, and let $\operatorname{{Evac}}$ and $\operatorname{{Evac}}^{*}$ denote the two evacuation operations on Kreweras words of length $3n$. For a word $w$, consider the fixed-point conditions $\operatorname{{Evac}}^{*}(w)=w$ and $\operatorname{{Evac}}(w)=w$. **Evacuation fixed-point conjecture.** For all $n\geq 1$, the number of Kreweras words of length $3n$ satisfying $\operatorname{{Evac}}^{*}(w)=w$ is $$ \frac{3^{\lfloor n/2 \rfloor} 4^{\lceil n/2 \rceil} \prod_{j=1}^{\lfloor n/2 \rfloor}(3j-1) \prod_{j=1}^{\lceil n/2 \rceil}(3j-2) }{(n+1)!}. $$ The number satisfying $\operatorname{{Evac}}(w)=w$ is the same when $n$ is even and is $0$ when $n$ is odd. These formulas provide product expressions for the evacuation fixed points, while their status beyond the conjecture is not established in the supplied text.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Kreweras.mathdb346681`, `Kreweras.origin_series`, `Kreweras.adjacent_series`, `Kreweras.T_coeff`, `Kreweras.T_square_coeff`

This is Conjecture A.2. The proof is a telescoping corner identity. The formula fails for every unbalanced shape tested, so the hypothesis is needed.

### 347626 · Equidistribution conjecture for type parity at fixed Frobenius number

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 172)
- **Source:** Deepesh Singhal, “Numerical Semigroups of small and large type”, arXiv:2008.08110 (2020).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture holds: among numerical semigroups with Frobenius number F, the proportion whose type has the same parity as F tends to 1/2 as F grows, along all F. Almost all such semigroups have multiplicity close to F/2 (Backelin 1990; the one-sided form needed is proved here). For those, adding or removing the single element ⌊3F/4⌋ gives another numerical semigroup with the same Frobenius number, and the toggle changes the parity of the type unless the semigroup has pseudo-Frobenius numbers at one of a few small gaps from that element; a greedy count of disjoint pairs shows that the exceptional semigroups form a vanishing fraction. So the semigroups pair up, outside a negligible set, into pairs of opposite type parity. Lean defines numerical semigroups as cofinite additive submonoids of ℕ containing 0, the Frobenius number, the pseudo-Frobenius numbers and the type, and proves that the ratio of cardinalities tends to 1/2, both for the sets themselves and for a finite encoding used in the proof.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $F(S)$ be the Frobenius number of a numerical semigroup $S$, let $t(S)$ be its type, and let the denominator count all numerical semigroups with Frobenius number $F$. **Frobenius type-parity equidistribution conjecture.** $$ \lim_{F\to\infty}\frac{\#\{S\mid F(S)=F,\ t(S)\equiv F\pmod{2}\}}{\#\{S\mid F(S)=F\}}=\frac{1}{2}. $$ The conjecture is motivated by the same numerical data for $F\leq 30$ and predicts asymptotic equidistribution of the two type parities relative to the parity of $F$.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Singhal.singhal_7_2`, `Singhal.singhal_7_2_encoded`
- **Replay record:** `semigroup-type-parity-347626-root-replay-fable-20261123-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2; public-claim-100/replay-347626.log all_ok, 9 modules)`; acceptance record `numerical-semigroup-type-parity-347626-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/347626-numerical-semigroup-type-parity`
- **Prior art searched:** `t1-prelean-X98-sol62-20261123-r1/REVIEW-347626.md (GPT-6.2 Sol, the second model, before the Lean was written: CONFIRMED_TIER1, not routine 0.68; 43,648 toggle pairs checked; a local correction to a floor inequality; fixed the unconditional Lean endpoint)`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (candidate review and final check); see lane queries and routes recorded

### 347753 · Four-point optimal-code conjecture for the Hilbert cube

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 116)
- **Source:** Emily J. King, Dustin G. Mixon, Hans Parshall and Chris Wells, “Uniquely optimal codes of low complexity are symmetric”, arXiv:2008.12871 (2025).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every set N of indices with the sum of 1/k^2 over N equal to sqrt(2) pi / 3 - 1, the four printed points form an optimal code of size 4 in the Hilbert cube: the set has four points, lies in the cube, and every four-point subset of the cube has two distinct points at distance no larger than the smallest distance of the printed set. This is Conjecture 27 of King, Mixon, Parshall and Wells, who proved the three-point case and left four points open. The proof sorts the four points by first coordinate, pushes the extreme ones to the faces, and splits into two cases at the threshold 1 - alpha/2: either one pair is already close, or a weighted sum of five squared distances with weights 1, 1, 2, 1, 1 is bounded by convexity at the corners of each coordinate box. Lean states the cube inside Mathlib's l^2 space with its own distance and quantifies over every four-element subset.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $H$ be the Hilbert cube, with standard basis vectors $e_k$, and set $$ \alpha:=\frac{\sqrt{2}\pi}{3}. $$ Choose $N\subseteq\mathbb{N}$ such that $$ \sum_{k\in N}\frac{1}{k^2}=\alpha-1, $$ and define $$ s:=\sum_{k=2}^\infty\frac{1}{k}e_k,\qquad t:=\sum_{k\in N}\frac{1}{k}e_k. $$ **Hilbert-cube four-point conjecture.** The set $$ \left\{0,\left(1-\frac{1}{2}\alpha\right)e_1+s,e_1+t,\frac{1}{2}\alpha e_1+s-t\right\} $$ is an optimal code in the Hilbert cube. Numerical experiments on truncations motivate this prediction; the supplied text later confirms that such choices of $N$ exist but notes that the resulting codes are not unique.
- **Lean endpoint:** `HilbertCode.conj27`
- **Replay record:** `hilbert-cube-code-347753-root-replay-fable-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2; public-claim-100/replay-347753.log all_ok)`; acceptance record `hilbert-cube-four-point-optimal-code-347753-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/347753-hilbert-cube-four-point-optimal-code`

### 348539 · Strict unimodality conjecture for Reiner–Stanton polynomials

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 125)
- **Source:** William Y. C. Chen and Ivy D. D. Jia, “Semi-invariants of Binary Forms Pertaining to a Unimodality Theorem of Reiner and Stanton”, arXiv:2011.01467 (2021).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every even n >= 8 and every k >= 15, the polynomial F_{n,k}(q) = [n+k, k]_q - q^n [n+k-2, k-2]_q is symmetric of degree nk, has f_0 = f_1, and its coefficients increase strictly from f_1 up to the centre nk/2. This is Conjecture 3.3 of Chen and Jia in the setting of their definition (3.4), where n is even, which is also how the MathDB record states it. The proof works in the ring of semi-invariants of binary forms modulo the unique invariant Q of degree 2: that quotient is a domain, so the set of degrees in which a coefficient difference is positive is closed under addition; Sylvester's theorem identifies the differences with dimensions; uniform seeds in a few small degrees come from a quasipolynomial certificate for restricted partitions (3,632 residue cases, each a polynomial identity plus a sign check, with the coverage of all cases proved in Lean); a covering argument and a separate induction for n = 10 and 14 finish. Lean defines the Gaussian binomial as a sum over partitions in a box and proves the statement literally.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $$ F_{n,k}(q)={n+k\brack k}-q^{n}{n+k-2\brack k-2}, $$ where $k\geq 2$ and $n$ is even. A polynomial is **strictly unimodal, except for the two terms at both ends** when its coefficients strictly increase to the middle, apart from the two end terms. **Strict unimodality conjecture.** For $n\geq 8$ and $k\geq 15$, $F_{n,k}(q)$ is strictly unimodal, except for the two terms at both ends. The conjecture is supported by numerical evidence, while the text does not report a proof or disproof.
- **Lean endpoint:** `ChenJia.chen_jia_conj_3_3`
- **Replay record:** `chen-jia-unimodality-348539-root-replay-fable-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2; public-claim-100/replay-348539.log all_ok)`; acceptance record `chen-jia-strict-unimodality-even-n-348539-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/348539-chen-jia-strict-unimodality-even-n`

### 348549 · Bruhn–Rautenbach's Fano-plane determinant conjecture

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 140)
- **Source:** Igor Araujo, József Balogh and Yuzhou Wang, “Maximum determinant and permanent of sparse 0-1 matrices”, arXiv:2011.01892 (2020).
- **What is proved (exact):** Every n×n 0-1 matrix with at most 3n ones satisfies |det A|^7 ≤ 24^n, so det A ≤ 24^(n/7), with equality for disjoint unions of Fano incidence matrices. The best published bounds were 2^(2n/3) (Araujo, Balogh and Wang) and 24^(n/6) (Scheinerman). The proof normalizes the matrix on both sides by its row and column degrees, bounds the logarithm of the determinant of the normalized Gram matrix through its trace and the trace of its square with degree-dependent centres (an integer-degree envelope that is sharp exactly at degree three), and closes with a linear edge/vertex potential that survives deletion of rows and columns of degree one; the induction runs on the potential bound, which needs no density hypothesis, so a deletion that raises the density is harmless. Lean proves the integer form |det A|^7 ≤ 24^n and derives the printed real-power form, and checks that the Fano matrix (21 ones, determinant 24) attains equality and that the hypotheses are satisfiable for every n.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $A\in\{0,1\}^{n\times n}$ have at most $3n$ non-zero entries. **Bruhn–Rautenbach's conjecture.** The determinant of $A$ is at most $$ 24^{n/7}. $$ The bound is motivated by the incidence matrix of the Fano plane, whose determinant is $24$, and by disjoint unions of such matrices. The source presents this as an open problem.
- **Lean endpoint:** `SparseDet.mathdb_348549`, `SparseDet.mathdb_348549_integer`
- **Replay record:** `sparse-determinant-348549-root-replay-opus55-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Opus 5.5; public-claim-100/replay-348549.log all_ok, 18 modules)`; acceptance record `sparse-determinant-fano-bound-348549-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/348549-sparse-determinant-fano-bound`

### 349250 · Bóna's maximal-avoidance conjecture for Hertzsprung patterns

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 88)
- **Source:** Anders Claesson, “From Hertzsprung's problem to pattern-rewriting systems”, arXiv:2012.15309 (2021).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every pair of natural lengths k,n and every permutation pattern τ of length k, the number of length-n permutations avoiding τ as a consecutive block whose values are one integer translate of τ, with the empty pattern occurring at every boundary, is at most the number avoiding the increasing pattern of length k. If n>k, this inequality is strict whenever τ is neither increasing nor decreasing.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** LITERAL_STRONGER
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $\mathcal{S}_k$ be the set of Hertzsprung patterns of length $k$, let $\tau\in\mathcal{S}_k$, and let $|\mathcal{S}_n(\tau)|$ denote the number of length-$n$ objects avoiding $\tau$. Write $\mathrm{id}_k$ for the identity pattern of length $k$. **Bóna's maximal-avoidance conjecture.** For every Hertzsprung pattern $\tau\in\mathcal{S}_k$ and every natural number $n$, $$ |\mathcal{S}_n(\tau)|\leq |\mathcal{S}_n(\mathrm{id}_k)|. $$ Bóna proved the inequality for non-self-overlapping patterns. The paper reports computational evidence suggesting that self-overlapping patterns also satisfy it, but gives no general proof.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Hertzsprung.claesson_conj_3_13`, `Hertzsprung.claesson_source`, `Hertzsprung.strict_avoidance`
- **Replay record:** `hertzsprung-349250-root-final-replay-fable-20261118-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2)`; acceptance record `hertzsprung-maximal-avoidance-349250-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/349250-hertzsprung-maximal-avoidance`
- **Kernel-checked versus paper-only:** Lean checks exact occurrence and avoidance counts, the inequality and strictness. The written proof gives the same first- and second-moment comparison.
- **Prior art searched:** `public-tracker-prior-art-sweep-sol62-20261119-r1`; sweep verdict PARTIAL_OVERLAP, top-up verdict PARTIAL_OVERLAP; 54 queries and routes recorded
- **Closest prior work read:** Bóna, Where the monotone pattern (mostly) rules, author preprint (full text) https://arxiv.org/html/0704.1489
- **Adversarial verdicts:** SOLID (adversarial-triage-E9-sol62-20261119-r1); CONFIRMED_TIER1 (tier1-confirm-G4-opus-20261119-r1, confidence medium-high)
- **Status note:** G4 Opus confirmed (brute force to n=9)

### 349778 · Strong anti-diagonal eigenvalue property conjecture for stochastic matrices

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 114)
- **Source:** John R. Britnell and Mark Wildon, “Involutive random walks on total orders and the anti-diagonal eigenvalue property”, arXiv:2102.08469 (2021).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every n and every row-stochastic n x n matrix A with n distinct eigenvalues, A has the global anti-diagonal eigenvalue property (every leading block is lower triangular and real diagonalizable, and the eigenvalues of the block times the reversal matrix are the signed diagonal entries with multiplicity) if and only if A is a binomial transform B Diag(a) B^{-1} of some vector a. This is Conjecture 2.6 of Britnell and Wildon. The source proves the easy direction, the converse away from an exceptional algebraic set, and the cases n <= 4. The proof here is by induction on the size: a maximum principle for iterated finite differences of the eigenvectors forces them into a polynomial flag when the last parameter is positive, and a determinant identity excludes the remaining zero case because it would repeat an eigenvalue. Lean states the property block by block as the conjecture does, not through the eigenbasis property of the source's Theorem 2.9, and assumes no moment representation.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $A$ be a stochastic matrix with distinct eigenvalues. Say that $A$ has the **strong anti-diagonal eigenvalue property** when it satisfies the stronger anti-diagonal eigenvalue condition defined in the paper, and let $H^\lambda$ denote the parameterized family of lower-triangular matrices introduced there. **Strong anti-diagonal eigenvalue property conjecture.** $A$ has the strong anti-diagonal eigenvalue property if and only if it is of the form $H^\lambda$. The conjecture would classify stochastic matrices with distinct eigenvalues satisfying this strengthened spectral property; the paper states that it can be proved for $n\leqslant4$, while the general case is left open.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Classification349778.mathdb349778`, `Classification349778.mathdb349778_all`

### 350694 · Vertex-cut obstruction for betweenness-uniform blow-ups

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 147)
- **Source:** David Hartman and Aneta Pokorná, “Constructions of betweenness-uniform graphs from trees”, arXiv:2105.06777 (2021).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture is false. Let G be the wedge of two 5-cycles (order 9, diameter 4, the common vertex a cut vertex). Replace the four neighbours of the cut vertex by K100, the four remaining vertices by K5, and the cut vertex by K604 ⊔ K8 ⊔ K4 ⊔ 25·K1. The resulting 1061-vertex blow-up is connected and every vertex has betweenness exactly 100. The source's obstruction for trees comes from leaves, whose parts carry no global load; in the two-C5 wedge every part lies on a cycle, and a part of the cut vertex that is a disjoint union of cliques adds load only to the neighbouring parts, so its number of non-adjacent pairs is a free parameter that balances the equations. The proof is a general formula for the betweenness of a blow-up whose parts are disjoint unions of cliques, from a lifting lemma for shortest paths; Lean proves the formula in general and evaluates it on the 9-vertex base, never enumerating the 1061 vertices. Lean's betweenness is the source's (unordered pairs), shared verbatim with the accepted 382260 packet.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $G$ be a graph of order $n$ with diameter at least three and having a vertex cut of size one. For graphs $H_1,\dots,H_n$, let $G[H_1,\dots,H_n]$ be the corresponding blow-up of $G$. **Vertex-cut obstruction.** There are no graphs $H_1,\dots,H_n$ such that $G[H_1,\dots,H_n]$ is betweenness-uniform. This obstruction identifies a class of graphs that cannot be transformed into betweenness-uniform graphs by the blow-up construction. The supplied text does not provide a resolution status beyond stating the result as a conjecture candidate.

### 351145 · Sharpness conjecture for the radio-k-number of even cycles

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 112)
- **Source:** Colin Bloomfield, Daphne Der-Fen Liu and Jeannette Ramirez, “Radio-k-Labeling of Cycles for Large k”, arXiv:2106.15059 (2022).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every even n and odd k with n/2 <= k < n-3, with h = (n-k-1)/2 and p = gcd(n,h), if n/2 lies in the subgroup of Z_n generated by h then the radio-k-number of the cycle C_n equals LB(n,k) + p - 1. This is Conjecture 1 of Bloomfield, Liu and Ramirez. The upper bound is the source's Theorem 11; the new part is the matching lower bound, where the source proves only LB + ceil(p/2) - 1. The proof makes the source's gap lemma quantitative, maps vertices to residues modulo p, and rules out the case where the resulting closed walk wraps around, which is exactly the factor of two the source left open. Lean states the conjecture literally: the graph distance of Mathlib's cycle graph, the radio inequality over the integers, the radio-k-number as a minimum span, and LB proved equal to the source's formula.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $n$ be even and $k$ be odd with $$ \frac{n}{2} \leqslant k < n-3. $$ Set $$ h=\frac{n-k-1}{2},\qquad p=\gcd(n,h). $$ Write $\langle h\rangle$ for the subgroup generated by $h$ in the relevant cyclic group. **Sharpness conjecture.** If $n/2\in\langle h\rangle$, then $$ \operatorname{rn}_k(C_n)=\operatorname{LB}(n,k)+p-1. $$ The paper notes that the values of the radio $k$-number are known for most parameters in the stated range, but cases with different parities remain open. The conjecture asserts that the upper bound from the preceding theorem is sharp whenever $n/2\in\langle h\rangle$.
- **Lean endpoint:** `RadioCycles.sharpConjecture_proved`, `RadioCycles.radio_even_cycle_sharp_source`, `RadioCycles.radio_span_sharp_lower`, `RadioCycles.radio_sharp_construction`
- **Replay record:** `radio-k-cycles-351145-root-replay-fable-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2; public-claim-100/replay-351145.log all_ok)`; acceptance record `radio-k-number-even-cycles-sharp-351145-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/351145-radio-k-number-even-cycles-sharp`

### 351157 · The $q$-analog of the Markoff injectivity conjecture over balanced sequences

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 139)
- **Source:** Sébastien Labbé and Mélodie Lapointe, “The q-analog of the Markoff injectivity conjecture over the language of a balanced sequence”, arXiv:2106.15886 (2021).
- **What is proved (exact):** The polynomial μ_q(w)_{12} determines w among all finite factors of balanced bi-infinite binary sequences. The published tool, evaluation at a primitive sixth root of unity (Labbé, Lapointe and Steiner 2023, who proved injectivity on Christoffel words), recovers only the letter counts, and two balanced words from different sequences can have the same counts. The new step is the first derivative at that root: the two-letter commutator vanishes there, so the derivative is a function of two integer statistics recording where the ones sit. A second argument shows that a balanced word is the unique minimizer of a separable quadratic energy among words with its length and counts, so the length, the number of ones and the two statistics determine the word. Lean states the source's matrices, the left-to-right product and the (1,2) entry, balanced sequences on ℤ and their language, and checks the source's example polynomial, a pair of balanced words with equal counts lying in no common balanced sequence, and the known non-injective pair outside the language.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $\mathcal{B}=\\{s\in\\{\mathtt{0},\mathtt{1}\\}^{\mathbb{Z}}:s\text{ is balanced}\\}$ be the set of all balanced bi-infinite binary sequences, and let $$ \mathcal{L}(\mathcal{B})=\bigcup_{s\in\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{L}(s) $$ be their language of finite factors. Let $\mu_q$ be the $q$-analog of the map assigning to a finite word $w$ the polynomial $\mu_q(w)_{12}\in\mathbb{Z}[q]$. **The $q$-analog of the Markoff injectivity conjecture over balanced sequences.** The map $w\mapsto\mu_q(w)_{12}$ from $\\{\mathtt{0},\mathtt{1}\\}^*$ to $\mathbb{Z}[q]$ is injective over $\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{B})$. This extends the $q$-analog from Christoffel words to the language of all balanced sequences, including periodic and Sturmian sequences; the supplied text gives no resolution.
- **Lean endpoint:** `labbe_lapointe_conj_1_3`
- **Replay record:** `q-markoff-351157-root-replay-opus55-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Opus 5.5; public-claim-100/replay-351157.log all_ok, 12 modules)`; acceptance record `q-markoff-balanced-injectivity-351157-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/351157-q-markoff-balanced-injectivity`

### 351584 · Carnevale–Voll's factorisation conjecture for multiset Euler–Mahonian polynomials

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 122)
- **Source:** Angela Carnevale and Elena Tielker, “On Denert's statistic”, arXiv:2108.04700 (2021).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every composition eta, the polynomial giving the joint distribution of Denert's statistic and excedances over the multiset permutations S_eta has a (nonconstant) unitary factor if and only if eta = (m^r) is a rectangle with r even and m odd; in that case the factor is 1 + x^{rm/2} y, it occurs once, and the cofactor has no unitary factor. This is Conjecture 23 of Carnevale and Tielker, the (den, exc) form of Conjecture B of Carnevale and Voll. Lean defines the words, excedances, Denert's statistic, the polynomial and the unitary predicate literally and proves both directions, the printed factor, multiplicity one and the statement about the cofactor, under ONE named hypothesis: the source's published generating-function formula for the polynomial (its equation (10) with Theorems 3 and 10, that is MacMahon's formula transported by Han's equidistribution), which is not formalized. The new mathematics is the exclusion of non-rectangles: a unitary factor forces poles of the generating function at roots of unity whose orders are incompatible, by a strict sine-sum inequality, unless al…
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $\eta$ be a composition, and let $S_{\eta}$ denote the associated multiset permutations. For a polynomial $f\in\mathbb{Z}[x,y]$, call it **unitary** if there are $F\in\mathbb{Z}[Y]$ and $a,b\in\mathbb{N}_0$ such that $f(x,y)=F(x^ay^b)$ and every complex root of $F$ has absolute value $1$. **Carnevale–Voll's factorisation conjecture.** The polynomial giving the joint distribution of $(\operatorname{den},\operatorname{exc})$ over $S_{\eta}$ has a unitary factor if and only if $\eta=(m^r)$ is a rectangle with $r$ even and $m$ odd. In that case, $$ \sum_{w\in S_{\eta}}x^{\operatorname{den}(w)}y^{\operatorname{exc}(w)}=\left(1+x^{\frac{rm}{2}}y\right)f^{\eta}_0(x,y), $$ where $f^{\eta}_0(x,y)$ has no unitary factor. This is a reformulation of the factorisation conjecture for joint $(\operatorname{des},\operatorname{maj})$ distributions over multiset permutations. The stated factorisation…

### 352297 · Conjecture on great-circle thrackleable trees and augmented caterpillars

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 156)
- **Source:** Karen Collins and Cleo Roberts, “Great-circle Tree Thrackles”, arXiv:2110.09679 (2021).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture holds: every great-circle thrackleable tree (with at least three vertices) is an augmented caterpillar, and the containment is proper. The open half is strictness. The 14-vertex tree T formed by an edge 01 with three pendant 2-paths at each of 0 and 1 is an augmented caterpillar under every reading of the source's Definition 3.1 (its legs are disjoint paths), but it has no great-circle thrackle drawing in general position. The proof (GPT-6.1 Sol) encodes a drawing by the signs of 3×3 determinants of the vertex vectors (a rank-3 chirotope), imposes the 3-term Grassmann–Plücker relations and an exact table saying when a short or long great-circle arc meets another exactly once, and refutes the resulting 162,578-clause formula with an LRAT certificate; the containment half (the source's Theorem 3.2) is re-proved the same way from a certificate that the 3,3,3 spider is undrawable. Lean defines drawings on the unit sphere of ℝ³ in general position, short and long arcs, the thrackle condition (every two distinct edges meet in exactly one point) and the source's Definition 3…
- **MathDB record statement:** A **great-circle thrackleable tree** is a tree admitting a thrackle drawing on the sphere in which the edges are great-circle arcs. An **augmented caterpillar** is the graph class defined in the source. **Conjecture on great-circle thrackleable trees and augmented caterpillars.** The class of great-circle thrackleable trees is properly contained in the class of augmented caterpillars. The conjecture is proposed as a further restriction on the class of trees that can be great-circle thrackleable; the source leaves it open and notes that crossing orientations may be relevant to proving or disproving it.

### 352345 · Minimality conjecture for initial threshold graphs generated by RFI

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 121)
- **Source:** Luiz Emilio Allem, Elismar R. Oliveira and Fernando Tura, “On I-eigenvalue free threshold graphs”, arXiv:2110.12107 (2021).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every real N > 0 and every depth r >= 2, the threshold graph that the source's algorithm produces with the smallest choice at every step is minimal: any threshold graph of the same depth whose block sizes are coordinatewise at most those of the initial graph and which has no adjacency eigenvalue in (0, N] is the initial graph itself. The source's authors conjectured this after exhaustive searches of millions of graphs. The proof reduces freeness of the interval to positive definiteness of a k x k matrix (k = ceil(r/2)) by the block structure and a Schur complement on the independent blocks, and shows that every single decrement of a block fails: for a clique block the pivot at the greedy threshold becomes nonpositive, and for an independent block a floor inequality makes the pivot of the pair formed with the preceding clique block nonpositive. Lean defines the graph on a sigma type, the algorithm with real floors, and the eigenvalue condition through adjacency eigenvectors, and includes a non-vacuity lemma.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $N>0$ and $r\geq 2$ be fixed, and let $I=(0,N]$. Let $T_G(a_1,\ldots,a_r)$ be the initial threshold graph obtained from RFI$(N,r)$. **Minimality conjecture.** The graph $G$ is minimal: if $\widetilde{G}\preceq G$ is also $I$-eigenvalue free, then $\widetilde{G}=G$. This conjecture asserts that no smaller threshold graph is $(0,N]$-eigenvalue free. The authors report exhaustive computational searches for several positive values of $N$ that found no smaller example, but the general claim remains open.
- **Lean endpoint:** `RFIMinimality.conj_10_1`, `RFIMinimality.conjecture352345_proved`
- **Replay record:** `rfi-threshold-minimality-352345-root-replay-fable-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2; public-claim-100/replay-352345.log all_ok)`; acceptance record `rfi-threshold-graph-minimality-352345-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/352345-rfi-threshold-graph-minimality`

### 353552 · The asymptotic threshold conjecture for complete bipartite graphs

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 60)
- **Source:** Hemanshu Kaul, Akash Kumar, Jeffrey A. Mudrock, Patrick Rewers, Paul Shin and Khue To, “On the List Color Function Threshold”, arXiv:2202.03431 (2023).

### 354228 · Even-arity complete full tree local antimagic chromatic number conjecture

Here a labelling is a bijection from the edges to 1 through the edge count with unequal incident-label sums across each edge, and the standard tree model has minimum tⁿ+1.

### 354240 · Thue–Morse arithmetic-progression bound conjecture

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 126)
- **Source:** Bartosz Sobolewski, “On monochromatic arithmetic progressions in binary words associated with pattern sequences”, arXiv:2204.05287 (2023).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every difference d >= 1, the longest monochromatic arithmetic progression of difference d in the Thue-Morse sequence has length A_t(d) <= d + 5, and A_t(d) = d + 5 exactly when d = 2^k - 1 with k even and k >= 2 (d = 3, 15, 63, 255, ...). This is Conjecture 3 of Sobolewski, for every starting point and both colours. The proof places a binomial cube S_D with D >= c - 5 inside the set of residues where the colour is forced to change, by Lucas's theorem; shows over F_2 that X^{uj}(1 + X^u)^D is divisible by (1 + X)^D, so that after multiplication by any odd u this set cannot fit in a cyclic arc shorter than D, which gives the bound; and characterizes equality by a rigidity lemma for affine cubes and three explicit families of obstructions. Lean defines the length with no cap, as the supremum over starting points of the first index at which the colour changes, in the extended naturals.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $\mathbf{t}$ denote the Thue–Morse sequence, and let $A_{\mathbf{t}}(d)$ be its associated arithmetic-progression counting function. **Thue–Morse bound conjecture.** For all $d \geq 1$, $$ A_{\mathbf{t}}(d) \leq d+5. $$ Moreover, equality holds if and only if $d=2^k-1$ for some even $k \geq 2$. The conjecture gives a sharp linear upper bound, with equality at the indicated powers-of-two-minus-one parameters.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Campaign354240Literal.sobolewski_conjecture_three`, `Campaign354240Literal.sobolewski_conjecture_three_bundled`
- **Replay record:** `thue-morse-progressions-354240-root-replay-fable-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2; public-claim-100/replay-354240.log all_ok)`; acceptance record `thue-morse-monochromatic-progressions-354240-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/354240-thue-morse-monochromatic-progressions`

### 354868 · Injectivity conjecture for evaluation maps of linearized polynomials

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 120)
- **Source:** Rod Gow and Gary McGuire, “Linearized Polynomials, Galois Groups and Symmetric Power Modules”, arXiv:2206.01551 (2022).
- **What is proved (exact):** Conjecture 1 of Gow and McGuire is false. Over F = F_3(t) the 3-polynomial L = X^81 + (t + t^2) X^27 - (t + t^2) X^9 + (t + t^2) X^3 - t (t^2 + t + 1) X has 3-degree 4, L/X is irreducible over F, L is not a 3^s-polynomial for any s > 1, and r = 2 <= q - 1, yet the evaluation map epsilon_2 on quadratic forms in a basis of the root space is not injective: an explicit seven-term quadratic form vanishes on the roots (the image has dimension 8 in a space of dimension 10). The roots are the image of u F_81, with u^80 = t, under the additive twist x -> x + x^3 of the Kummer polynomial X^81 - tX; the twist keeps irreducibility, because the automorphisms u -> cu act transitively on the nonzero roots, and it forces the kernel. Lean states the conjecture for q = p = 3 with every hypothesis of the source and proves its negation with this witness, including irreducibility and the splitting field.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $L=L(x)$ be a $q$-polynomial in $F[x]$ of $q$-degree $n$, where $F$ is a field of characteristic $p$ that contains $\operatorname{\mathbb{F}}_q$. Assume that $L$ is not a $q^s$-polynomial for $s>1$, and that $L(x)/x$ is irreducible over $F$. **Injectivity conjecture.** If $1\le r\le q-1$, then the evaluation map $\epsilon_r$ is injective. This conjecture predicts injectivity in the range $1\le r\le q-1$ under irreducibility and a non-$q^s$-polynomial hypothesis. The surrounding results establish related injectivity statements for evaluation maps, but the supplied text does not state whether this conjecture has been resolved.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Gow.mathdb_354868_false`, `Gow.concrete_counterexample`
- **Replay record:** `gow-mcguire-twist-354868-root-replay-fable-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2; public-claim-100/replay-354868.log all_ok)`; acceptance record `gow-mcguire-twisted-kummer-refutation-354868-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/354868-gow-mcguire-twisted-kummer-refutation`

### 355303 · Congruence-class bias conjecture for parts in k-regular partitions

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 76)
- **Source:** Faye Jackson and Misheel Otgonbayar, “Biases among Congruence Classes for Parts in k-regular Partitions”, arXiv:2207.04352 (2022).
- **What is proved (exact):** Let D_k(r,t;n) count all occurrences, with multiplicity, of parts congruent to r modulo t among partitions of n in which each part occurs fewer than k times. For all t≥6 and 1≤r<s≤t, D_k(r,t;n)≥D_k(s,t;n) for k≥3 and n≥1, and D_k(r,t;n)>D_k(s,t;n) for k≥2 and n>max(t+3,16).
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** READING
- **MathDB record statement:** For $k$-regular partitions, let $D_k(r,t;n)$ denote the number of occurrences of parts congruent to $r$ modulo $t$ among the partitions of $n$. Here $t\geq 6$, $1\leq r<s\leq t$, and $k\geq 3$. **Congruence-class bias conjecture.** For $n\geq 1$, $$ D_k(r,t;n) \geq D_k(s,t;n). $$ Moreover, for $k\geq 2$, if $$ n>\max\{t+3,16\}, $$ then $$ D_k(r,t;n)>D_k(s,t;n). $$ The conjecture formalizes the observed bias toward smaller parts and extends the finite computational verification to all $t\geq 6$ and all admissible congruence classes. The preceding theorem establishes related bounds for $2\leq k,t\leq 10$, while the asserted ranges beyond those finite checks remain open.
- **Lean endpoint:** `RB355303.original_weak`, `RB355303.original_strict`, `RBReplayAudit.conjecture_1_4`

### 355348 · Growth conjecture for the number of orderings of congruence-class biases

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 145)
- **Source:** Faye Jackson and Misheel Otgonbayar, “Unexpected Biases between Congruence Classes for Parts in k-indivisible Partitions”, arXiv:2207.06365 (2022).
- **What is proved (exact):** Both growth assertions hold. The orderings are determined by the second-order scores −ψ(r/t) + ψ(ρ(r)/t)/k of the source (ρ(r) the residue of k⁻¹r). The upper bound uses the source's own score formula: for each residue class of k the ordering changes only boundedly often before it settles, giving O(t) ≤ φ(t)(⌊t/H⌋ + 2H² + 2) for every H, hence o(t·φ(t)). The lower bound carries the new idea: for k = mt + q the label q is the unique label with the most right inversions, so the ordering reveals k mod t, and a counting argument forces a new ordering at every consecutive quotient m up to a power of t; Lean proves O(t)/φ(t) ≥ t^{1/4}/80 for large t (the written proof gives t^{1/3}). Lean defines ψ as Mathlib's digamma (with the series bridge proved), counts orderings over all k ≥ 2 coprime to t with no cap, and proves the statement for every relation family compatible with the score comparisons, which is how the source's Corollary 1.2 links its partition counts to the scores; the later no-ties theorem is not needed.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $\mathscr{O}(t)$ denote the number of possible orderings arising from the biases among congruence classes for $k$-indivisible partitions, and let $\varphi(t)$ be Euler's totient function. **Growth conjecture.** As $t\to\infty$, we have $$ \frac{\mathscr{O}(t)}{\varphi(t)}=o(t) $$ and $$ \log t=o\left(\frac{\mathscr{O}(t)}{\varphi(t)}\right). $$ Thus the normalized number of orderings is conjectured to grow sublinearly in $t$ and faster than logarithmically. The claim is motivated by numerical evidence and remains open.
- **Lean endpoint:** `BiasGrowth.conjecture_355348`, `BiasGrowth.conjecture_355348_strict`
- **Replay record:** `bias-orderings-355348-root-replay-opus55-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Opus 5.5; public-claim-100/replay-355348.log all_ok, 34 modules)`; acceptance record `bias-orderings-growth-355348-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/355348-bias-orderings-growth`
- **Prior art searched:** `t1-prelean-X33-opus-20261120-r1/REVIEW-355348.md (Claude Opus 5.5, before the Lean: O(t) computed exactly for t <= 120 and 11 larger t; key lemmas tested at t = 3,000,017; required endpoint in the compatible-family form; CONFIRMED_TIER1 provisional, not routine 0.60)`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (candidate review and final check); see lane queries and routes recorded
- **Adversarial verdicts:** PASS (t1-prelean-X33-opus-20261120-r1/REVIEW-355348.md (Claude Opus 5.5, before the Lean: O(t) computed exactly for t <= 120 and 11 larger t; key lemmas tested at t = 3,000,017; required endpoint in the compatible-family form; CONFIRMED_TIER1 provisional, not routine 0.60))
- **Status note:** round 3, accepted 145; proof of Jackson-Otgonbayar Conjecture 1.5 (orderings of congruence-class biases: O(t)/phi(t) = o(t) and log t = o(O(t)/phi(t))); class recovery from a unique maximum of right inversions; Lean with weaker but sufficient rates (34 modules); Opus CONFIRMED_TIER1 before Lean (not routine 0.60), endpoint matched by root; final check by a separate Opus lane (same family as the packager; Sol unavailable) PASS

The Lean proves weaker rates (t^{1/4}/80 lower; φ(t)(t/H + 2H^2 + 2) upper) than the written proof's t^{1/3} and √t log t, which the paper gives as unformalized remarks; both little-o statements are proved exactly. ### 355513 · The cycle complement stable-set polynomial conjecture

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 162)
- **Source:** Robert Davis and Florian Kohl, “Perfectly Matchable Set Polynomials and h^*-polynomials for Stable Set Polytopes of Complements of Graphs”, arXiv:2207.14759 (2022).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture holds for every n ≥ 4. The even case is Davis and Kohl's own Corollary 3.14; the new case is odd n ≥ 5, where the stable set polytope of the complement of the odd cycle is not a lattice-point-friendly compressed polytope and has the extra rank facet. The proof counts the lattice points of the dilates directly from the known facet description of the antihole (Padberg): a greedy recursion around the cycle has a unique real cyclic fixed point; expanding the integer residual weights counts matchings of the cycle, which gives the perfectly matchable set polynomial, and the half-integral fixed points give the one extra monomial z^{(n+1)/2}; a rank-2 transfer matrix assembles the generating function. Lean proves the identity for all n ≥ 4, odd and even, from the lattice-point description of the dilated polytope, so it does not depend on the source's Corollary 3.14, and checks the source's Table 2 for n = 4..7.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $C_n$ be the cycle graph on $n$ vertices, let $p(C_n;z)$ denote its perfectly matchable set polynomial, and let $\overline{h}^*(C_n;z)$ denote the normalized $h^*$-polynomial of the stable set polytope of the complement of $C_n$. **Cycle polynomial conjecture.** For all $n\geq 4$, $$ \overline{h}^*(C_n; z) = p(C_n; z) + \frac{1+(-1)^{n+1}}{2}z^{(n+1)/2}. $$ The formula agrees with the explicitly computed even-cycle case and with the listed examples through $n=9$; the asserted general identity, particularly for odd cycles, remains open in the source.
- **Lean endpoint:** `AntiHole.davis_kohl_conjecture_3_15`
- **Replay record:** `antihole-hstar-355513-root-replay-opus55-20261122-r1 (root, Claude Opus 5.5; public-claim-100/replay-355513.log all_ok, 13 modules)`; acceptance record `antihole-stable-set-hstar-355513-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/355513-antihole-stable-set-hstar`
- **Prior art searched:** `t1-sol-cross-355513-sol62-20261123-r2/CROSS-REVIEW-355513.md (GPT-6.2 Sol, the second model, round 2 after round 1 stopped at the unreadable journal text: CONFIRMED_TIER1, not routine 0.65, correct 0.995, not public 0.88; Ehrhart numerators reproduced independently for n = 4..9; no definition issue; paper PASS WITH EDITORIAL EDITS); revised paper used`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (candidate review and final check); see lane queries and routes recorded
- **Adversarial verdicts:** PASS (t1-sol-cross-355513-sol62-20261123-r2/CROSS-REVIEW-355513.md (GPT-6.2 Sol, the second model, round 2 after round 1 stopped at the unreadable journal text: CONFIRMED_TIER1, not routine 0.65, correct 0.995, not public 0.88; Ehrhart numerators reproduced independently for n = 4..9; no definition issue; paper PASS WITH EDITORIAL EDITS); revised paper used)

### 356232 · Conjecture on the UT-rank of the monoid M_5

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 157)
- **Source:** Carl-Fredrik Nyberg-Brodda, “Tropical one-relation monoids”, arXiv:2209.12612 (2022).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture holds: M5 embeds in the upper triangular 4×4 matrices over the max-plus semiring on ℤ ∪ {−∞}, and in no smaller size. The new part is the lower bound. Fix the sum of the first and last exponents of a normal form C^α A^β B^γ; the defining relation then forces every entry of the image except the (1,3) entry to be determined, while that one entry can take only linearly many integer values; there are quadratically many normal forms, so two of them collide and no homomorphism into UT_3 is injective (sizes 1 and 2 are handled the same way). The argument uses that the entries are integers: over the real max-plus semiring a faithful UT_3 representation exists, so the statement is specific to ℤ. The upper bound is the source's Lemma 2.9 with one printed entry corrected (B₁₁ = −1); Lean also proves that the matrix as printed (B₁₁ = 1) is not injective. Lean states the conjecture with M5 as a presented monoid, UT_n as the subsemigroup of upper triangular matrices over MinTropical (WithTop ℤᵒᵈ) (proved to be max-plus on ℤ ∪ {−∞}), and an embedding as an injective semigroup homomo…
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $M_5 = \operatorname{Mon}\langle a,b\mid aba=ba\rangle$ be the one-relation monoid with defining relation $aba=ba$. Its **UT-rank** is the least $n$ such that $M_5$ admits a faithful representation into $\operatorname{UT}_n(\overline{\mathbb{Z}})$. **UT-rank conjecture for $M_5$.** The monoid $M_5$ has UT-rank exactly $4$. This is the remaining question for the UT-tropical embeddings considered here: an embedding into $\operatorname{UT}_4(\overline{\mathbb{Z}})$ is known, but it is not known whether smaller matrices suffice.
- **Lean endpoint:** `M5UT.mathdb356232`, `M5UT.conjecture_2_11`
- **Replay record:** `ut-rank-m5-356232-root-replay-opus55-20261122-r1 (root, Claude Opus 5.5; public-claim-100/replay-356232.log all_ok, 12 modules)`; acceptance record `ut-rank-of-m5-356232-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/356232-ut-rank-of-m5`
- **Prior art searched:** `t1-sol-cross-356232-sol62-20261123-r1/CROSS-REVIEW-356232.md (GPT-6.2 Sol, the second model: CONFIRMED_TIER1, not routine 0.57, correct 0.99, not public 0.96; literal Conjecture 2.11; no Lean definition mismatch; paper PASS WITH EDITORIAL EDITS); revised paper used`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (candidate review and final check); see lane queries and routes recorded

### 356756 · The prime-modulus conjecture for progression-free digit sets

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 19)
- **Source:** Christian Elsholtz, Benjamin Klahn and Gabriel F. Lipnik, “Large Subsets of Z_m^n without Arithmetic Progressions”, arXiv:2211.02588 (2022).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every natural modulus p≥13 congruent to 1 modulo 4 and every natural n, any four vectors in (ℤ/pℤ)ⁿ with both coordinatewise second differences zero are equal if each uses only digits 0 through (p−1)/2 and (p+3)/2, each exactly n/((p+3)/2) times. In particular, the specified prime-modulus sets contain no four-term arithmetic progression of nonzero step.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** READING
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $p\geq 13$ be a prime with $p\equiv 1\bmod 4$, and let $n$ be an integer divisible by $(p+3)/2$. For a digit set $D\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_p$, write $S(D,n)$ for the set of vectors in $\mathbb{Z}_p^n$ in which every digit of $D$ occurs the same number of times. Define $$ D=\left\{0,1,\ldots,\frac{p-1}{2},\frac{p+3}{2}\right\}. $$ **Prime-modulus progression-free-set conjecture.** The set $S(D,n)$ does not contain any arithmetic progression of length $4$. This conjecture extends the progression-free constructions for odd progression lengths to a case not covered by the stated theorem, motivated by computations with small primes. Its status is unresolved in the source.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Digits356756.mathdb_356756 : for p prime, 13 <= p, p % 4 = 1, (p+3)/2 | n, there are no a b c d : Fin n -> ZMod p in SourceSet p n with zero second differences and a != b; Digits356756.no_affine_prog…`
- **Replay record:** `digits-parent-final-r1/replay-result.json`; acceptance record `ledger.json#accepted`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/356756-a-parity-corrected-potential-proves-the-elsholtz-klahn-lipni`
- **Kernel-checked versus paper-only:** Lean checks the source theorem and the stronger composite-modulus endpoint full_scope. The paper derives the cardinality consequence and discusses larger known progression-free constructions.

### 357511 · The even zigzag-poset LE-symmetry conjecture

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 142)
- **Source:** Judy Hsin-Hui Chiang, Anh Trong Nam Hoang, Matthew Kendall, Ryan Lynch, Son Nguyen, Benjamin Przybocki and Janabel Xia, “Bender-Knuth involutions on linear extensions of posets”, arXiv:2302.12425 (2024).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every even n the Bender–Knuth involutions generate the full symmetric group on the linear extensions of the zigzag poset Z_n (whose number is an Euler number: 5, 61, 1385, ...). The source verified n ≤ 10 by computer and proved only transitivity. The proof is an induction on n: removing the last two elements embeds the action for n − 2, and a commutator of moves supported on different blocks isolates a 3-cycle; the group is primitive because a block containing one fibre is forced to be everything, so Jordan's theorem gives the alternating group; finally the second Bender–Knuth move is an odd permutation, by a sign recurrence through the tail fibres. Lean defines Z_n literally (an even-indexed element is below its neighbours), linear extensions as position-to-vertex bijections respecting the order, the Bender–Knuth move as the swap of adjacent positions when the two elements are incomparable (proved equivalent to the literal rule), and checks the counts 5 and 61 so that the statement cannot hold by an empty or trivial set.
- **MathDB record statement:** For even $n$, let $Z_n$ be the poset on $n$ elements with relations $$ v_1 < v_2 > v_3 < v_4 > \ldots > v_{n-3} < v_{n-2} > v_{n-1} < v_n. $$ A poset is **LE-symmetric** when its Bender–Knuth group acts as the full symmetric group on its set of linear extensions. **The even zigzag-poset LE-symmetry conjecture.** The zigzag-poset $Z_n$ is LE-symmetric when $n$ is even. This is computationally verified for all even $n\leq 10$, while odd cases such as $Z_5$, $Z_7$, and $Z_9$ provide counterexamples; the even case remains open.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Zigzag.even_zigzag_LE_symmetric`
- **Replay record:** `zigzag-357511-root-replay-opus55-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Opus 5.5; public-claim-100/replay-357511.log all_ok, 75 modules)`; acceptance record `zigzag-bender-knuth-le-symmetry-357511-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/357511-zigzag-bender-knuth-le-symmetry`
- **Prior art searched:** `t1-prelean-X24-opus-20261120-r1/REVIEW-357511.md (Claude Opus 5.5, before the Lean was finished: mathematics checked; journal version read through an open copy; sibling 357512 a different conjecture; required Lean endpoint and traps; CONFIRMED_TIER1 provisional, not routine 0.80)`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (candidate review and final check); see lane queries and routes recorded
- **Adversarial verdicts:** PASS (t1-prelean-X24-opus-20261120-r1/REVIEW-357511.md (Claude Opus 5.5, before the Lean was finished: mathematics checked; journal version read through an open copy; sibling 357512 a different conjecture; required Lean endpoint and traps; CONFIRMED_TIER1 provisional, not routine 0.80))

The citation index was enumerated through OpenAlex only. The formalization took eight stages, two of them in parallel. Cross-model confidence 0.80, not routine 0.80. ### 358162 · Vanishing bounds for Romik's sequence $v(n)$ modulo odd prime powers

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 148)
- **Source:** Christian Krattenthaler and Thomas W. Müller, “The congruence properties of Romik's sequence of Taylor coefficients of Jacobi's theta function θ_3”, arXiv:2304.11471 (2024).
- **What is proved (exact):** The odd clause holds exactly as printed: for every prime p ≡ 3 (mod 4) and odd e, p^e divides v(n) for n ≥ (ep+2)(p+1)/4. For even e and n ≥ ⌈ep²/4⌉, the exceptions are classified exactly: p^e fails to divide v(n) if and only if e = (p+1)(2j+1) with 0 ≤ j < (p−1)/2 and 4n = ep² + (p+1) + 4r with 4r < p+1 and an explicit coefficient (r-th coefficient of a fixed polynomial over F_p) nonzero; at every exception v(n) misses by exactly one factor of p. The classification reproduces the source's four listed exceptions and corrected bounds, and shows the list is incomplete even within its own range (n ≤ 1500): (p,e) = (3,4) at n = 10 and (11,12) at n = 366–368. The source's own Theorem 17 is a factor of two weaker. The proof bounds the p-adic valuation of a square-root series within one factor of p, and supplies the last factor (odd e) or the exact failure set (even e) from a digit-level residue formula; the Lean proof obtains the residues mod p as coefficients of an explicit square root in characteristic p built from Frobenius. Lean defines v(n) by the source's recurrence (2.6) over ℚ, pr…
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $p$ be a prime with $p\equiv3\pmod4$, let $e\ge1$, and let $(v(n))_{n\ge0}$ be the sequence defined in the paper. **The second conjecture.** If $e$ is odd, then $$ v(n)\equiv0\pmod{p^e} $$ for $n\ge\frac{(ep+2)(p+1)}{4}$. If $e$ is even, then $$ v(n)\equiv0\pmod{p^e} $$ for $n\ge\left\lceil\frac{ep^2}{4}\right\rceil$, with very few exceptions. Based on data with $n\le1500$, the listed exceptions are $(p,e)=(7,8),(7,24),(7,40),(11,36)$, whose correct lower bounds are respectively $102,298,494,1095$. The conjecture is an experimentally supported strengthening of the corresponding theorem.
- **Lean endpoint:** `romik_conj44_odd`, `romik_conj44_even_exact`, `romik_conj44_even_lower`
- **Replay record:** `romik-358162-root-replay-opus55-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Opus 5.5; public-claim-100/replay-358162.log all_ok, 10 modules)`; acceptance record `romik-sequence-vanishing-bounds-358162-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/358162-romik-sequence-vanishing-bounds`

### 358270 · The strict lower-bound conjecture for the robust clique covering number

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 93)
- **Source:** Gábor Bacsó, Balázs Patkós, Zsolt Tuza and Máté Vizer, “The robust chromatic number of graphs”, arXiv:2305.01923 (2023).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every finite simple graph G with at least one edge, theta_1(G) > theta(G), where theta_1(G) = max over 1-selections f of theta(G - f(V)); equivalently theta_1(G) = theta(G) iff G is edgeless (Conjecture 1 of Bacso-Patkos-Tuza-Vizer). Proved; the MathDB isolate-free hypothesis is not needed.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $G$ be an isolate-free graph. Write $\theta(G)$ for its clique covering number and $\theta_1(G)$ for the corresponding robustness parameter, so that the general lower bound is $\theta_1(G)\geq\theta(G)$. **Strict lower-bound conjecture.** The inequality is strict unless $G$ is edgeless; equivalently, equality can occur only for edgeless graphs. This conjecture asks for a characterization of the equality case in the lower bound for the robust clique covering number. Its status is not established in the supplied source.
- **Lean endpoint:** `RobustCover.conjecture1`, `RobustCover.mathdb_358270`, `RobustCover.robust_theta_strict`, `RobustCover.robust_theta_required_strict`
- **Replay record:** `robust-cover-358270-root-final-replay-fable-20261118-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2)`; acceptance record `robust-clique-covering-equality-358270-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/358270-robust-clique-covering-equality`

### 358647 · Local weak convergence of random fullerenes to the planar hexagonal lattice

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 61)
- **Source:** Artur Bille, Victor Buchstaber, Simon Coste, Satoshi Kuriki and Evgeny Spodarev, “Random eigenvalues of graphenes and the triangulation of plane”, arXiv:2306.01462 (2025).
- **What is proved (exact):** Let F_n be uniform on isomorphism classes of fullerene graphs with n vertices (connected cubic planar graphs, every face a pentagon or hexagon), n ranging over the orders admitting a fullerene. Then F_n converges locally weakly (Benjamini-Schramm, rooted at a uniform vertex) to the hexagonal lattice.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** READING
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $F_n$ be a random **fullerene**, meaning a finite, connected, 3-regular planar graph with faces of degrees both 5 and 6, chosen uniformly from the set of fullerenes with $n$ vertices. A sequence of finite random graphs converges **locally weakly** to a random rooted graph if the rooted balls around uniformly chosen vertices converge in distribution. Let $\mathcal{H}$ denote the planar hexagonal lattice. **Local weak convergence conjecture for random fullerenes.** The sequence $(F_n)$ converges locally weakly towards $\mathcal{H}$. This concerns the local geometry of uniformly random fullerenes in the large-size limit. The source states that the claim remains open; it is part of the broader study of local weak limits of random planar graphs.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Ful.endpoint`, `Ful.localWeakConvergence`, `Ful.Helix.nonVacuous`

### 359778 · The three-component-intersection extremal conjecture for odd n

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 87)
- **Source:** Imre Leader, Žarko Ranđelović and Ta Sheng Tan, “A Note on Hamiltonian-Intersecting Families of Graphs”, arXiv:2309.00757 (2023).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every h >= 1, on n = 6h-1 labelled vertices there is a family of (1+6h+12h^2)*2^(C(n,2)-6h) graphs whose pairwise intersections (including a graph with itself) have at most three components, exceeding the conjectured bound ((n+3)^2/8)*2^(C(n,2)-n); hence Leader-Randelovic-Tan Conjecture 7 fails for arbitrarily large odd n (ratio -> 4/3).
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** LITERAL_STRONGER
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $[n]=\{1,2,\ldots,n\}$ with $n$ odd, and let $\mathcal{F}$ be a family of graphs on $[n]$. **Three-component-intersection conjecture.** If the intersection of any two graphs in $\mathcal{F}$ has at most three components, then $$ |\mathcal{F}|\leq \frac{(n+3)^2}{8}\frac{1}{2^n}\cdot 2^{\binom{n}{2}}. $$ For more than two components, the authors report that fixed-Hamilton-cycle constructions no longer appear optimal. For three components they suspect the bound is attained by a construction based on two half-sized cycles meeting at a point, although the displayed conjecture is the stated quantitative claim.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Theta359778.theta_family`, `Theta359778.lrt_conj7_fails`, `Theta359778.not_conjecture`
- **Replay record:** `theta-family-359778-root-final-replay-fable-20261118-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2)`; acceptance record `three-component-intersection-theta-359778-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/359778-three-component-intersection-theta`
- **Kernel-checked versus paper-only:** Lean checks the spanning-theta family, exact cardinality, component property and violation above every threshold. Auxiliary Lean theorems also check the exact cardinality ratio and its limit 4/3, both given in the paper.
- **Prior art searched:** `public-tracker-prior-art-sweep-sol62-20261119-r1`; sweep verdict PARTIAL_OVERLAP, top-up verdict PARTIAL_OVERLAP; 52 queries and routes recorded
- **Closest prior work read:** Berger et al., the connected-intersection case, Theorem 2 (full text) https://arxiv.org/html/1901.01616v1

### 360458 · The second q-analogue of Liu's Dwork congruence

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 143)
- **Source:** Victor J. W. Guo, “Dwork-type q-congruences through the q-Lucas theorem”, arXiv:2310.15207 (2023).
- **What is proved (exact):** The congruence holds for every m >= 1 and every n > 1 with n ≡ 1 (mod 4). Closest prior work: the block values in Guo's source, the base case from Guo and Zudilin (2020), and Guo's 2020 arbitrary-m result for n ≡ 3 (mod 4); the arbitrary-m statement modulo the square of the cyclotomic polynomial for n ≡ 1 (mod 4) was left open as Conjecture 6.2. The proof splits the left side into blocks of length n, shows that each block agrees with the right side's base value at every primitive n-th root of unity to first order (value and derivative), using a reciprocity under a shift of a parameter and a cancellation of the first-order shift errors between complementary blocks, and proves the base block by a terminating Dixon-type identity with an explicit WZ-style certificate. Lean works in the field of rational functions over Q with q the variable, states the congruence as coprimality of both denominators with Φ_n together with divisibility of the numerator of the difference by Φ_n^2, proves every denominator in the statement nonzero, and proves the base case rather than assuming it.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $m$ and $n$ be positive integers with $n\equiv1\;\allowbreak(\operatorname{mod}4)$ and $n>1$, and let $(a;q)_k$ denote the $q$-shifted factorial. **The q-analogue conjecture.** $$ \sum_{k=0}^{mn-1}\frac{(1+q^{4k+1})(q^2;q^4)_k^3}{(1+q)(q^4;q^4)_k^3}q^k \equiv \frac{[n]_{q^2}(q^3;q^4)_{(n-1)/2}}{(q^5;q^4)_{(n-1)/2}}q^{(1-n)/2}\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\frac{(\frac12)_k^3}{k!^3}\;\allowbreak(\operatorname{mod}\Phi_n(q)^2). $$ This is presented as a different q-analogue of the first case of Liu's supercongruence and as a refinement of an earlier congruence.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Guo62.guo_conj_6_2`

### 360905 · Intermediate-value conjecture for orientable total domination numbers

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 43)
- **Source:** Sarah E. Anderson, Tanja Dravec, Daniel Johnston and Kirsti Kuenzel, “Orientable total domination in graphs”, arXiv:2311.16307 (2023).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every finite graph admitting an orientation of positive indegree at each vertex, every integer between the minimum and maximum attained total domination numbers is itself attained by such an orientation. Total domination requires each vertex, including each set member, to have an in-neighbour in the set.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** LITERAL
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $G\in\mathcal{C}$ be a graph, and let ${\rm dom}_t(G)$ and ${\rm DOM}_t(G)$ denote its lower and upper orientable total domination numbers. For an orientation $f$ of $G$, write $G_f$ for the resulting oriented graph and $\gamma_t(G_f)$ for its total domination number. **Intermediate-value conjecture.** For every integer $c$ satisfying $$ {\rm dom}_t(G)\leq c\leq {\rm DOM}_t(G), $$ there exists an orientation $f$ of $G$ such that $$ \gamma_t(G_f)=c. $$ This extends the corresponding result known for complete graphs and complete bipartite graphs. The source attributes the conjecture to the authors of the cited earlier work, and no resolution is given here.
- **Lean endpoint:** `OrientableTotalDomination.conjecture_true : OrientableTotalDomination.Conjecture, where Conjecture := for every finite vertex type V and Boolean adjacency G with nonempty spectrum and every c with lo…`

### 361006 · Rational spherical triangle measurement conjecture

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 177)
- **Source:** Haiyang Wang, “Rational Spherical Triangles”, arXiv:2312.01633 (2023).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture holds: the rational solutions of L'Huilier's equation in the proper range are exactly the two-right-side family and the eight sporadic tuples of the source (one in Λ₁ and the seven of Λ₂), so by the source's Proposition 2.2 these are the only measurements of proper rational spherical triangles, and the seven with three distinct sides are the only such triangles. The proof is computer-assisted. In tangent coordinates the equation becomes a vanishing sum of 23 roots of unity. The new step is a line lemma: if the equation holds along a whole line of parameters through a proper point, the behaviour at the two ends of the segment forces the line into the two-right-side family. With the polygon inequality this gives a rank condition at every other solution, which shows that only the primes 2, 3, 5, 7 divide the common denominator and bounds their exponents, so the denominator divides 352,800 = 2⁵·3²·5²·7². An exact search over these denominators finishes the classification. Lean defines the solution set Ω₂ and the sets Λ₁, Λ₂ literally from the source and proves Ω₂ = Λ₁ ∪ Λ…
- **MathDB record statement:** Let a proper spherical triangle on the unit sphere have side lengths and area that are rational multiples of $\pi$, and let its measurement be the tuple $(E,a,b,c)$, with $a\le b\le c$. Define $\Lambda_1$ and $\Lambda_2$ as the sets of measurements given in the source. **Rational spherical triangle measurement conjecture.** The tuples in $\Lambda_1\cup\Lambda_2$ are the only measurements of proper rational spherical triangles. In particular, there are only finitely many measurements of proper rational spherical triangles with mutually distinct side lengths, namely, the seven measurements found by Little and Coxeter. This conjecture would classify all proper rational spherical triangles and implies that the mutually distinct-side cases are exactly the seven known examples.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Wang.wang_conj_1_6_Omega2`, `Wang.global_filtered_replay`, `Wang.wang_conj_1_6_Omega2_reverse`, `Wang.affine_line_family`, `Wang.prime_support`, `Wang.conductor_dvd_of_small_primes`
- **Replay record:** `rational-spherical-361006-root-replay-fable-20261123-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2; public-claim-100/replay-361006.log all_ok, 7,688 modules; three runs: default heap, 5,500 MB heap, and 14,000 MB heap…`; acceptance record `rational-spherical-triangles-classification-361006-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/361006-rational-spherical-triangles-classification`

### 361017 · Preperiod conjecture for extensions of subtraction sets of size at most three

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 159)
- **Source:** István Miklós and Logan Post, “Superpolynomial period lengths of the winning positions in the subtraction game”, arXiv:2312.02426 (2023).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture holds: for a subtraction game with at most three moves and any seed, if p + x is an extension of the move set for every move x (p the period of the outcome sequence), then the outcome sequence is purely periodic. The source's extension criterion, applied at n + p, pulls the losing positions of the periodic tail backward into the start of the sequence; what remains is to exclude an extra early losing position. Such a position would force all of its forward neighbours to be winning in the periodic tail, and a new counting lemma (Lemma K: inclusion–exclusion on three translates of the set of losing residues, with matching singleton and pair counts and a pointwise triple inclusion) shows that this cannot happen when there are at most three moves. Lean proves the literal conjecture, the version with the hypothesis only for k = 1, a stronger pure-periodicity statement, and Lemma K; it also proves that the statement fails for four moves ({1,2,6,11}) and that Lemma K fails for {2,5,6,7}.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $A$ be a finite subtraction set with $|A|\leq 3$, let $S\in\{0,1\}^{\alpha}$ be a seed, and write $p=\operatorname{Per}(A,S)$. An integer of the form $kp+x$ is an **extension** of $A$ for every $k\in\mathbb{N}_+$ and $x\in A$ under the condition stated. **Preperiod-extension conjecture.** If $kp+x$ is an extension of $A$ for all $k\in\mathbb{N}_+$ and $x\in A$, then $$ \operatorname{PrePer}(A,S)=0. $$ The statement is the converse of the cited invariant-extension proposition for sets of size at most three. The source notes that it suffices to check $k=1$, and that the converse is not true for nonempty seeds in the unrestricted setting.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Subtraction.conjecture2`, `Subtraction.conjecture2_k1`, `Subtraction.pure_of_period`, `Subtraction.lemmaK`
- **Replay record:** `subtraction-games-361017-root-replay-opus55-20261122-r1 (root, Claude Opus 5.5; public-claim-100/replay-361017.log all_ok, 6 modules)`; acceptance record `subtraction-games-preperiod-extension-361017-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/361017-subtraction-games-preperiod-extension`
- **Prior art searched:** `t1-sol-cross-361017-sol62-20261123-r1/CROSS-REVIEW-361017.md (GPT-6.2 Sol, the second model: CONFIRMED_TIER1, not routine 0.64, correct 0.99, not public 0.87; Lean endpoint matches the literal conjecture, no definition issue; paper PASS WITH EDITORIAL EDITS, translation signs fixed); revised paper used`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (candidate review and final check); see lane queries and routes recorded
- **Adversarial verdicts:** PASS (t1-sol-cross-361017-sol62-20261123-r1/CROSS-REVIEW-361017.md (GPT-6.2 Sol, the second model: CONFIRMED_TIER1, not routine 0.64, correct 0.99, not public 0.87; Lean endpoint matches the literal conjecture, no definition issue; paper PASS WITH EDITORIAL EDITS, translation signs fixed); revised paper used)

The argument is short and elementary; the contribution is the three-translate lemma. The source's separate remark that two-move games are purely periodic for arbitrary seeds is false under its printed recurrence, and its {3,5,11} example gives preperiod 11, not 12, u…

### 361098 · Odd-order graphs do not exhibit Laplacian perfect state transfer

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 123)
- **Source:** Steve Kirkland and Hermie Monterde, “Quantum walks on join graphs”, arXiv:2312.06906 (2023).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture of Kirkland and Monterde that Laplacian perfect state transfer does not occur in a simple connected unweighted graph of odd order is false. An explicit connected graph on 35 vertices has perfect state transfer between two vertices at time pi/2: two nonadjacent twin vertices u, v are joined to every vertex of a set W of 22 vertices, each vertex of W has 8 neighbours in a set R of 11 vertices, and each vertex of R has 16 neighbours in W. The Laplacian eigenvalues on the symmetric vector e_u + e_v are 0, 20 and 28, all divisible by 4, and the eigenvalue on e_u - e_v is 22, congruent to 2 modulo 4, so exp(i pi L / 2) maps e_u to e_v. The graph is the case k = 1 of a family of orders 36k^2 - 1 (r = 12k^2 - 1, c = 8k^2), proved on paper for all k. Lean checks the 35-vertex graph: it defines the conjecture for all simple connected graphs on an odd number of vertices with Mathlib's Laplacian and the complex matrix exponential, proves connectedness and the eigenvector identities by kernel computation, and derives the negation.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $X$ be a simple connected unweighted graph on an odd number of vertices. **Odd-order Laplacian PST conjecture.** Laplacian perfect state transfer does not occur in $X$. This extends the corresponding result for unweighted graphs on $3$ or $5$ vertices and follows for weighted join graphs under the additional assumption that the characteristic polynomial of the Laplacian has integer coefficients; the general case remains open.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Pool012.odd_order_laplacian_pst_refuted`, `Pool012.source_conjecture77_refuted`, `Pool012.source_explicit_counterexample`, `Pool012.witness_exact_transfer`
- **Replay record:** `odd-laplacian-pst-361098-root-replay-fable-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2; public-claim-100/replay-361098.log all_ok)`; acceptance record `odd-order-laplacian-pst-refutation-361098-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/361098-odd-order-laplacian-pst-refutation`
- **Prior art searched:** `t1-prelean-X12-opus-20261120-r1/REVIEW-361098.md (Claude Opus 5.5: witness and family verified in exact arithmetic for k = 1, 2, 3; exhaustive search of all graphs of odd order up to 9 found no counterexample; public and routine tests; the Lean statement and graph read against the source; CONFIRMED_TIER1)`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (candidate review and final check); see lane queries and routes recorded
- **Adversarial verdicts:** PASS (t1-prelean-X12-opus-20261120-r1/REVIEW-361098.md (Claude Opus 5.5: witness and family verified in exact arithmetic for k = 1, 2, 3; exhaustive search of all graphs of odd order up to 9 found no counterexample; public and routine tests; the Lean statement and graph read against the source; CONFIRMED_TIER1))
- **Status note:** round 3, accepted 123; refutation of the odd-order Laplacian perfect state transfer conjecture (Kirkland-Monterde) by a 35-vertex graph, first member of an infinite family of orders 36k^2 - 1; Lean 5 modules (the 35-vertex graph only); Opus CONFIRMED_TIER1 (correct 0.99, tier 1 rather than tier 2 0.75; exhaustive search to order 9 finds nothing)

### 361202 · The general equality conjecture for $H(m,\alpha)$ and $H_2(m,\alpha)$

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 149)
- **Source:** Wallace Peaslee, Attila Sali and Jun Yan, “An intermediate case of exponential multivalued forbidden matrix configuration”, arXiv:2312.11446 (2023).
- **What is proved (exact):** H(m, α) = H₂(m, α) for every m ≥ 3, and in fact for every α > 1: every triple-choice multigraph is matched or beaten in weight by a 2-recursive one. The source's edge-swap lemma gives only a closed vertex pair, and its Section 6 suggests combining closed sets without carrying it out. The proof normalizes cross-block edge weights by (α^{|A|}−1)(α^{|B|}−1) to force a closed pair of blocks, then uses size-weighted cloning and the convexity of α^x to homogenize the union without losing weight, iterating from singleton blocks. Lean defines triple-choice multigraphs, the weight, 2-recursiveness (Definition 1.3) and H, H₂ as maxima over nonempty finite sets, and checks small values (H(3,2) = 4, H(4,2) = 12 by exhausting all 81 choices, H₂(5,2) = 30, H₂(6,2) = 73) and that a non-2-recursive choice exists. The 'in particular' limit follows from the equality and the source's Theorem 6.2, which Lean does not formalize.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $H(m,\alpha)$ and $H_2(m,\alpha)$ be the quantities considered in the paper, with $m\geq 3$ and $\alpha\geq 2$, and define $$ \lambda(\alpha)=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{2^{j-1}}{\alpha^{2^j}}. $$ **General equality conjecture.** $$ H(m,\alpha)=H_{2}(m,\alpha)\quad\text{for all }m\geq 3\text{ and }\alpha\geq 2. $$ In particular, $$ \lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{2H(m,\alpha)}{m\alpha^m}=\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{2H_{2}(m,\alpha)}{m\alpha^m}=\lambda(\alpha). $$ This generalizes the conjectured equality at $\alpha=2$. The paper proves matching structural and asymptotic results for $H_2(m,\alpha)$, while the equality with $H(m,\alpha)$ is left open.
- **Lean endpoint:** `PSY.psy_conj_6_3`, `PSY.H_eq_H₂`, `PSY.recursive_dominates`
- **Replay record:** `exp-forbidden-361202-root-replay-opus55-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Opus 5.5; public-claim-100/replay-361202.log all_ok, 10 modules)`; acceptance record `exponential-forbidden-configuration-h-equals-h2-361202-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/361202-exponential-forbidden-configuration-h-equals-h2`
