### 361410 · Parity congruence conjecture for finite Mordell--Tornheim sums

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 134)
- **Source:** Crystal Wang and Jianqiang Zhao, “Mordell–Tornheim Zeta Values, Their Alternating Version, and Their Finite Analogs”, arXiv:2401.03380 (2024).
- **What is proved (exact):** All five assertions of Conjecture 3.4 of Wang and Zhao hold: for a prime p exceeding the weight plus one, every r >= 1 and every exponent vector s of positive integers, the finite sums Z_{p^r}^{d}(s) over compositions of p^r into d positive parts prime to p satisfy the stated divisibility by powers of p, the scaling laws in depths 4 and 5 relating level p^{r+1} to level p^r, and the general recurrence in r. Earlier work (Chen and Zhao; McCoy, Thielen, Wang and Zhao) treated only exponents all equal to 1, where just the last assertion applies; the source proved depth 3 by Bernoulli-number computations. The proof lifts the sum from level p^r to p^{r+1} keeping double precision (modulus p^{2r+1}), shows that the second moments of the carries vanish modulo p, and projects onto one reflection parity to gain a further factor of p. Lean states the sums and the five congruences literally in the integers modulo prime powers.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $r\in\N$, let $p$ be a prime, and let $\bfs\in\N^d$ satisfy $p>|\bfs|+1$, where $|\bfs|$ is the weight and $Z_{p^r}(\bfs)$ is the corresponding finite Mordell--Tornheim sum. **Finite Mordell--Tornheim congruence conjecture.** The following congruences should hold: 1. If $d=4$ and $|\bfs|$ is odd, then $$ Z_{p^r}(\bfs)\equiv p^{2r-2}Z_p(\bfs)\pmod{p^{2r-1}}. $$ 2. If $d=4$ and $|\bfs|$ is even, then $$ Z_{p^r}(\bfs)\equiv p^{r-1}Z_p(\bfs)\pmod{p^r}. $$ 3. If $d=5$ and $|\bfs|$ is even, then $$ Z_{p^r}(\bfs)\equiv p^{2r-2}Z_p(\bfs)\pmod{p^{2r-1}}. $$ In general, if $r\geq2$ and $d+|\bfs|$ is odd, then $$ Z_{p^r}(\bfs)\equiv0\pmod{p^{2r-2}}; $$ if $r\geq2$ and $d+|\bfs|$ is even, then $$ Z_{p^r}(\bfs)\equiv0\pmod{p^{r-1}}. $$ The conjecture extends parity-dependent congruences for finite Mordell--Tornheim sums beyond the cases proved earlier in the paper. It is supported by extensive n…
- **Lean endpoint:** `FiniteMT.wang_zhao_conjecture_3_4`

### 361823 · One-step separation conjecture for genus Tutte polynomials

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 150)
- **Source:** Tsuyoshi Miezaki, Manabu Oura, Tadashi Sakuma and Hidehiro Shinohara, “The Tutte polynomials of genus g”, arXiv:2402.06845 (2024).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every g ≥ 1 there are matroids with equal genus-g and different genus-(g+1) Tutte polynomials, so separation happens one genus up, the optimal constant in the source's Problem 5.11 (the source's Theorem 4.22 separates at genus ⌈√2·g⌉). For g ≥ 4 the new construction uses sparse paving matroids indexed by the even versus the odd vectors of {0,1}^g: an explicit bijection of g-tuples gives the equality at genus g, and a rigidity lemma (every output slot has a private input) shows that one extra slot detects the parity, giving a coefficient that differs at genus g+1. For g = 1, 2, 3 the pairs are the source's own examples (R6/Q6 and S_4n/S'_4n at n = 3, 4), with the equality and separation proved directly. Padding both matroids with i loops gives the infinite sequences. Lean defines the genus-g Tutte polynomial over Mathlib's matroids with the source's slot variables (singletons and pairwise intersections and unions), and proves the statement for every g with no hypothesis and nothing cited from the source.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $g$ be a positive integer, and let $\{M_i\}$ and $\{N_i\}$ be sequences of matroids. **One-step separation conjecture.** There exist two infinite sequences of matroids $\{M_i\}$ and $\{N_i\}$ such that $$ T^{(g)}(M_i)=T^{(g)}(N_i) $$ and $$ T^{(g+1)}(M_i)\neq T^{(g+1)}(N_i). $$ This conjecture asks whether the constant controlling the genus needed to distinguish infinite families can be reduced to one. The surrounding proposition establishes separation at a genus of the form $\lceil c_1g+c_2\rceil$; the conjecture that separation occurs already at $g+1$ remains open.
- **Lean endpoint:** `GenusTutte.miezaki_conj_5_12`
- **Replay record:** `genus-tutte-361823-root-replay-opus55-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Opus 5.5; public-claim-100/replay-361823.log all_ok, 13 modules)`; acceptance record `genus-tutte-one-step-separation-361823-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/361823-genus-tutte-one-step-separation`

'Infinite sequences' is met by padding one pair per g with loops, the way the source obtains its…

### 362603 · Linear-threshold conjecture for connected size Ramsey numbers of star matchings

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 107)
- **Source:** Fanghua Guo, Yanbo Zhang and Yunqing Zhang, “Matching-star size Ramsey numbers under connectivity constraint”, arXiv:2404.03175 (2024).
- **What is proved (exact):** For all positive integers n, p, m with m >= 5(n+p), the connected size Ramsey number of n vertex-disjoint stars K_{1,p} (red) against a star K_{1,m} (blue) equals n(m+p)-1: the minimum number of edges of a finite connected simple graph every red/blue edge colouring of which contains a red nK_{1,p} or a blue K_{1,m}. This proves the linear-threshold conjecture of Guo, Zhang and Zhang with c = 5; the source had the formula only for m >= (n^2+2pn+n-3)/2. Lean proves both the upper bound by construction and the lower bound for every connected graph with fewer edges. The constant 5 is not optimised; no constant c <= 2/3 can work (referee's computation at (n,p,m) = (2,1,2)).
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $nK_{1,p}$ be the disjoint union of $n$ copies of the star $K_{1,p}$, and let $K_{1,m}$ be the star with $m$ edges. The connected size Ramsey number $\hat r_c(G_1,G_2)$ is the minimum number of edges in a connected graph $G$ such that $G\to (G_1,G_2)$. **Linear-threshold conjecture.** There exists a positive constant $c$ such that for all positive integers $m$, $n$, and $p$ with $m\geq c(n+p)$, we have $$ \hat r_c(nK_{1,p},K_{1,m})=n(m+p)-1. $$ The conjecture proposes that the exact value established in the paper for the stronger condition $m\geq (n^2+2pn+n-3)/2$ remains valid once $m$ is merely linear in $n+p$.
- **Lean endpoint:** `StarRamsey.mathdb_362603`, `StarRamsey.connectedRamsey_eq_five`
- **Replay record:** `star-ramsey-362603-root-final-replay-fable-20261119-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2)`; acceptance record `connected-size-ramsey-star-matching-linear-threshold-362603-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/362603-connected-size-ramsey-star-matching-linear-threshold`
- **Prior art searched:** `candidates-review-R5-opus-20261119-r1/REVIEW-362603.md (Claude Opus 5.5, PASS, FLAGS none)`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (candidate review and final check); see lane queries and routes recorded

### 362667 · Polynomial formulas for truncated total depth in ordered trees

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 102)
- **Source:** Stoyan Dimitrov, Martin Minchev and Yan Zhuang, “BFS versus DFS for fixed-level targets in ordered trees”, arXiv:2404.05664 (2026).
- **What is proved (exact):** For ordered trees with n edges and the depth-first-search scores totalD and totalDTrunc of Dimitrov, Minchev and Zhuang, for every l >= 1 there are polynomials a_l and b_l of degree 2l-2 with non-negative integer coefficients and leading coefficients 2l-1 and l such that, for all n >= l, totalD(n,2l-1) - totalDTrunc(n,2l-1) = a_l(n) C(2n, n-2l-1) / (n+2l+2)^(2l-2) and totalD(n,2l) - totalDTrunc(n,2l) = b_l(n) C(2n+1, n-2l-2) / (n+2l+4)^(2l-2), with rising factorials in the denominators. This is Conjecture 7.6 of arXiv v1. The proof is a binomial-sum enumeration of the score difference followed by a pairing argument for non-negativity. Lean also confirms that the quadratic coefficient of b_3 is 1133, where Table 3 of v1 prints 113.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $n$ and $\ell$ be integers satisfying $n\geq \ell\geq 1$. Let $\operatorname{totalD}(n,k)$ denote the total quantity studied in the paper, and let $\operatorname{totalDTrunc}(n,k)$ denote its truncated version. For each $\ell$, let $a_{\ell}(n)$ and $b_{\ell}(n)$ be polynomials in $n$. **Polynomial-formula conjecture.** For all $n\geq \ell\geq 1$, \ $$ \operatorname{totalD}(n,2\ell-1)-\operatorname{totalDTrunc}(n,2\ell-1)=\frac{a_{\ell}(n)}{(n+2\ell+2)^{(2\ell-2)}}{2n \choose n-2\ell-1} \ $$ and \ $$ \operatorname{totalD}(n,2\ell)-\operatorname{totalDTrunc}(n,2\ell)=\frac{b_{\ell}(n)}{(n+2\ell+4)^{(2\ell-2)}}{2n+1 \choose n-2\ell-2}. \ $$ Here $a_{\ell}(n)$ and $b_{\ell}(n)$ have degree $2\ell-2$ and non-negative integer coefficients; the leading coefficient of $a_{\ell}(n)$ is $2\ell-1$, and the leading coefficient of $b_{\ell}(n)$ is $\ell$. The conjecture would provide a simplifi…

### 362959 · Orientation invariance conjecture for s-permutree lattices

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 111)
- **Source:** Eva Philippe and Vincent Pilaud, “Geometric realizations of the s-weak order and its lattice quotients”, arXiv:2405.02092 (2025).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every n, every tuple s of non-negative integers (zero entries allowed), every decoration of the other positions and every position k decorated up, changing the decoration at k from up to down changes neither the number of elements of the delta-permutree lattice (the quotient of the s-weak order by the lattice congruence generated by the decoration) nor, for any m, the number of faces of size m of its canonical join complex. This is Conjecture 76 of Philippe and Pilaud. Lean formalizes the objects literally: s-trees and their order with a proved lattice structure, s-arcs, the generated congruence (proved to contract exactly the forbidden arcs), the quotient lattice and canonical join representations defined lattice-theoretically; the proof identifies faces with marked arc diagrams by a sweep bijection and proves the forward direction of the source's Theorem 72 by a direct congruence construction.
- **MathDB record statement:** Fix an $n$-tuple ${\boldsymbol{s}}$ of non-negative integers and a decoration map ${\boldsymbol{\delta}}$ assigning one of the four local orientations—none, up, down, or up-down—to each index $i$ with $s_i\ne0$. Let $W_{\boldsymbol{s}}/\equiv_{\boldsymbol{\delta}}$ be the ${\boldsymbol{\delta}}$-permutree lattice, and let its canonical join complex be the simplicial complex formed by canonical join representations. **Orientation invariance conjecture for s-permutree lattices.** For fixed ${\boldsymbol{s}}$, changing any up decoration to a down decoration in ${\boldsymbol{\delta}}$ does not affect the cardinality of the ${\boldsymbol{\delta}}$-permutree lattice or the $f$-vector of its canonical join complex. The conjecture concerns invariants that remain unchanged under the indicated decoration change, while the source notes that other structures, such as the undirected cover graph and…
- **Lean endpoint:** `Permutree.philippe_pilaud_conj76`, `Permutree.diagram_invariance`, `Permutree.quotient_faces_equiv_diagrams`
- **Replay record:** `permutree-invariance-362959-root-final-replay-fable-20261119-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2)`; acceptance record `s-permutree-orientation-invariance-362959-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/362959-s-permutree-orientation-invariance`

### 364499 · The Jacobi-symbol conjecture for the integers $c_p$

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 99)
- **Source:** Deyi Chen and Zhi-Wei Sun, “On determinants involving (j^2-k^2p) and (jkp)”, arXiv:2408.14401 (2024).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every odd prime p, let c_p be the positive odd integer defined by Chen and Sun's determinant identities (D_p^(0)(w) = -w((p-1)c_p/2)^2 for p = 1 mod 4; D_p^(1)(w) = w(c_p sum_{k<=(p-1)/2}(k/p))^2 for p = 3 mod 4). Then the Jacobi symbol (p / c_p) = 1 (Chen-Sun, arXiv:2408.14401, Conjecture 1.1); existence and uniqueness of c_p are part of the formal statement.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $p$ be an odd prime. For $\delta\in\{0,1\}$, define $$ D_p^{(\delta)}(w)=\left|\left(\frac{j^2-k^2}{p}\right)+\left(\frac{jk}{p}\right)w\right|_{\delta\leqslant j,k\leqslant(p-1)/2}-\left|\left(\frac{j^2-k^2}{p}\right)\right|_{\delta\leqslant j,k\leqslant(p-1)/2}. $$ For $p\equiv1\pmod4$, let $c_p$ be the positive odd integer whose existence is asserted by the preceding theorem through $D_p^{(0)}(w)=-w((p-1)c_p/2)^2$; for $p\equiv3\pmod4$, let $c_p$ be the positive odd integer whose existence is asserted through $D_p^{(1)}(w)=w(c_p\sum_{k=1}^{(p-1)/2}(\frac{k}{p}))^2$. **Jacobi-symbol conjecture for $c_p$.** For any odd prime $p$, $$ \left(\frac{p}{c_p}\right)=1, $$ where $(\frac{p}{c_p})$ is the Jacobi symbol. The conjecture is based on numerical values of $c_p$ listed in the source. The supplied text gives no resolution or proof, so its status remains open.
- **Lean endpoint:** `ChenSun.chen_sun_conjecture`, `ChenSun.jacobi_cp_eq_one`, `ChenSun.chen_sun_conjecture_with_root`, `ChenSun.even_root_exists`, `ChenSun.odd_signed_root`
- **Replay record:** `chen-sun-364499-root-final-replay-fable-20261119-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2)`; acceptance record `chen-sun-jacobi-symbol-cp-364499-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/364499-chen-sun-jacobi-symbol-cp`
- **Prior art searched:** `public-tracker-prior-art-sweep-sol62-20261119-r1`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND, top-up verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND; 51 queries and routes recorded
- **Closest prior work read:** Chen–Sun, On determinants involving Legendre symbols, Conjecture 1.1 (full text) https://arxiv.org/html/2408.14401
- **Adversarial verdicts:** CLEARED (closecall-adjudication-A4-opus-20261119-r1, confidence 0.75)
- **Status note:** A4
- **Caveats the row carries:**
  - Modest significance: the p = 1 mod 4 half uses a Ren-Luo-type Vandermonde reduction mod p; the new idea is in the p = 3 mod 4 half (c_p mod 4 via a Pfaffian sign congruence).
  - Orientation is (p / c_p) as in the source; (c_p / p) = 1 is false (e.g. p = 43, 47, 67).
  - Bounded prior-art search (arXiv API, partly rate-limited; web search; ProbXiv; OpenAlex 0 citations); Google Scholar not used.

### 364626 · Finiteness conjecture for connected graphs with odd metamour period

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 80)
- **Source:** William Q. Erickson, Daniel Herden, Jonathan Meddaugh, Mark R. Sepanski, Mitchell Minyard and Kyle Rosengartner, “Limits and Periodicity of Metamour 2-Distance Graphs”, arXiv:2409.02306 (2024).
- **What is proved (exact):** For each positive odd integer k, there exists a finite collection of connected finite simple graphs whose least positive return to the identical labelled graph under repeated distance-exactly-two adjacency is k. Every connected finite simple graph of that exact period is isomorphic to a member of the list.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** LITERAL
- **MathDB record statement:** A graph's **metamour period** is the least positive integer $k$ for which its iterated metamour graph returns to the original graph, under the metamour operation used in the paper. Let $k$ range over the positive odd integers. **Finiteness conjecture.** For each odd $k\in \mathbb{Z}^+$, there exist only finitely many connected graphs with metamour period $k$. The paper has already classified the connected graphs with metamour period $3$: they are $C_7$ and $C_9$. The conjecture predicts analogous finiteness for every odd period, and the classification of such graphs remains open in general.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Metamour.Review.finite_native_least_labeled_odd_return`
- **Replay record:** `metamour-364626-independent-extracted-replay-sol62-20261117-r1`; acceptance record `metamour-odd-period-finiteness-364626-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/364626-metamour-odd-period-finiteness`
- **Kernel-checked versus paper-only:** Lean checks the exact-period covering collection and odd-return degree, diameter and order bounds. The paper discusses the known period-three classification and period-one convention.
- **Prior art searched:** `public-tracker-prior-art-sweep-sol62-20261119-r1`; sweep verdict PARTIAL_OVERLAP, top-up verdict PARTIAL_OVERLAP; 58 queries and routes recorded
- **Closest prior work read:** Erickson et al., Limits and Periodicity of Metamour 2-Distance Graphs (full text) https://arxiv.org/html/2409.02306
- **Adversarial verdicts:** SOLID (adversarial-triage-E9-sol62-20261119-r1); CONFIRMED_TIER1 (tier1-confirm-G4-opus-20261119-r1, confidence high)
- **Status note:** G4 Opus confirmed
- **Caveats the row carries:**
  - Short proof. The conjecture is 26 months old with 2 indexed citations, so tier 1 rests on it being an explicitly posed open conjecture that the source authors only settled at k=1,3.
  - Leonor's unpublished thesis is unread.
  - The bound is a Ramsey-tower bound. The row should not suggest a classification at k≥5.
  - No evidence of Claude authorship (sol62 lanes).
  - Labeled least return, finiteness up to isomorphism.
  - Leonor's unpublished thesis and several other named prior bodies remain unread. FAU abstract now read.

### 365088 · The complexity bound conjecture for S(e,23,e)-adic languages

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 154)
- **Source:** Thomas Garrity and Otto Vaughn Osterman, “On the Factor Complexity Associated with a Family of Multidimensional Continued Fraction Algorithms”, arXiv:2410.02032 (2026).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every directive sequence over the two substitutions of the TRIP-map family S(e,23,e), the factor complexity of the S-adic language satisfies p(n) ≤ 3n for every n ≥ 1; the bound is attained. The source proves the analogous bound for the family (e,e,e), outlines the method for (e,23,e) in its Section 10 with Propositions 69–71 stated without proof, and names the remaining work: characterize the bispecial words and control their lengths. The proof does this: every double-Gauss block is a 3-free word followed by a single 3, which gives unique parsing and the exact extension sets in both directions (the source's Proposition 71, here proved); the bispecial factors are classified into 23 types; and a new cross-age matching between bispecials of different levels controls their total extension count. Lean defines the two substitutions, the directive applied with d(0) outermost as the source's Definition 14 requires, the language as the factor closure of all images, and the complexity as the number of factors of length n, and proves the bound for every directive sequence, including degen…
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $\mathcal{L}$ be an $S(e,23,e)$-adic language, and let $p_\mathcal{L}(n)$ denote its factor-complexity function. **Complexity bound conjecture.** The complexity function $p_\mathcal{L}$ for any $S(e,23,e)$-adic language $\mathcal{L}$ satisfies $$ p_\mathcal{L}(n) \leq 3n. $$ This would extend the paper's classification of TRIP maps whose associated $S$-adic languages have complexity bounded above by $3n$ to the remaining map family $S(e,23,e)$ and its twins and conjugates.

### 365301 · Contact-order distance conjecture for colored graph representations

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 24)
- **Source:** Lily Adlin, Giovani Thai, Samuel Tiscareno and Ryan Tully-Doyle, “Cauchy Transforms of Colored Graphs in Two Variables”, arXiv:2410.10695 (2025).

The **contact order** is the order describing how the associated level curves approach one another near a boundary singularity. **Contact-order distance conjecture.** The contact order of the representation computed from vertex $i$ is twice the path distance from $i$ to the closest vertex colored $w$. The source reports computational evidence for this extension, but does not state a proof or resolution.

### 365943 · Nonexistence conjecture for cubic 8-circulant nut graphs

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 23)
- **Source:** Nino Bašić and Ivan Damnjanović, “On cubic polycirculant nut graphs”, arXiv:2411.16904 (2024).
- **What is proved (exact):** No finite simple cubic graph whose real adjacency kernel is one-dimensional and contains a vector nonzero at every vertex admits an automorphism with exactly eight orbits of equal size. This rules out a cubic eight-circulant nut graph at every order.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** LITERAL
- **MathDB record statement:** A **cubic $8$-circulant nut graph** is a cubic nut graph admitting an $8$-circulant structure. **Nonexistence conjecture for cubic $8$-circulant nut graphs.** There exists no cubic $8$-circulant nut graph. This is the only unresolved case in the cubic $\ell$-circulant nut graph existence problem; computational results suggest that such graphs might not exist, but no proof is given here.

### 366022 · Cell-count conjecture for face Schubert arrangements of polytopes

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 82)
- **Source:** Sebastian Seemann and Francesca Zaffalon, “How to stab a polytope”, arXiv:2412.00551 (2024).
- **What is proved (exact):** There is a full-dimensional eight-vertex rational polytope in ℝP³ whose space of stabbing projective lines avoiding every edge-span divisor has no bijection between its connected components and the realized projective Chow sign vectors, taken modulo simultaneous sign reversal. Two lines meeting the interior share one strict sign class but lie in different components.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** LITERAL
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $P\subseteq \mathbb{P}^{n-1}$ be a full-dimensional polytope, and let $P^{[k]}$ denote its $k$-stabbing set. Consider the $k$-face Schubert arrangement of $P$ inside $P^{[k]}$, and the sign vectors determined by its defining equations for $V\in P^{[k]}\setminus \mathcal{H}_P^k$. **Cell-count conjecture.** The number of cells in this arrangement is equal to the number of sign vectors realized by such spaces $V$. This would show that, for Schubert arrangements arising from polytopes, cells are uniquely determined by their realized sign vectors; the claim is presented as an expectation, and no resolution is supplied in the source.
- **Lean endpoint:** `G366022.mathdb_366022_counterexample`
- **Replay record:** `grassmannian-stabbing-366022-independent-extracted-replay-sol62-20261117-r1`; acceptance record `face-schubert-cell-count-366022-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/366022-face-schubert-cell-count`
- **Kernel-checked versus paper-only:** Lean checks the full-dimensional polytope, topology, component-to-sign surjection with a repeated fiber and no bijection. Semialgebraic finiteness of the components is proved in the written argument. Lean checks strict comparison conditional on finiteness and unconditional cardinal inequality/no bijection.
- **Prior art searched:** `public-tracker-prior-art-sweep-sol62-20261119-r1`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND, top-up verdict PARTIAL_OVERLAP; 57 queries and routes recorded

### 366271 · The forbidden-pattern characterization conjecture for one-stack one-queue layouts

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 136)
- **Source:** Deborah Haun, Laura Merker and Sergey Pupyrev, “Forbidden Patterns in Mixed Linear Layouts”, arXiv:2412.12786 (2024).
- **What is proved (exact):** Both characterizations of Conjecture 31 hold for every finite ordered matching. The content is part (i): with the vertex order fixed, deciding a 1-stack 1-queue layout is a 2-SAT problem, whose contradiction cycles can be arbitrarily long, so 2-SAT alone gives no finite obstruction set. The proof shows that for intervals, three-edge forcing configurations plus propagation decide colourability, and that the geometry of intervals shortens every contradiction path to at most one link. Hence every matching without such a layout contains one with at most six edges, and an enumeration of the 11,465 matchings with at most six edges gives exactly the 12 patterns of Figure 19a. Part (ii) is routine (a failure needs a crossing pair and a nesting pair, at most four edges; also the source's Lemma 27). Lean defines ordered matchings on natural-number endpoints, layouts as the source does, and containment as an injective edge map preserving every endpoint comparison; the figure patterns are literal endpoint tables; no size cap enters the statement.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let an ordered matching have a fixed non-separated layout. A **$1$-stack $1$-queue layout** uses one stack and one queue, and a **mixed layout on $1$ page** uses one page whose edges may be assigned to stacks or queues. The twelve and eight patterns referred to below are the patterns depicted in the source's figures. **Forbidden-pattern characterization conjecture.** The ordered matching admits $$ \text{a $1$-stack $1$-queue layout if and only if it avoids the 12 patterns depicted in the source's figure,} $$ and admits a mixed layout on one page if and only if it avoids the 8 patterns depicted in the source's figure. This conjecture proposes complete finite obstruction sets for the two smallest non-separated layout classes. It is consistent with the preceding computational enumeration of twelve and eight critical matchings, but the supplied source gives no proof or resolution.

### 366504 · The derivation construction conjecture for transposed Poisson n-Lie algebras

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 163)
- **Source:** Farukh Mashurov, “On the transposed Poisson n-Lie algebras”, arXiv:2501.01714 (2025).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture is false at n = 3 over every field of characteristic 0. The counterexample is a transposed Poisson 3-Lie algebra M₃ given by an explicit multiplication and bracket table, extended to polynomials in one variable t, with the derivation D = d/dt; the 4-ary operation μ built from D and the bracket fails the generalized Jacobi (Filippov) identity, with an explicit nonzero defect. The idea is that D = d/dt on the polynomial extension turns the Filippov defect of μ into a polynomial identity (a 'strong condition' whose first argument is itself a bracket) that would have to hold in the base algebra, and the table is chosen so that this degree-seven specialization is nonzero. Huang, Chen, Chen and Ding proved the construction for strong transposed Poisson n-Lie algebras, and Mashurov showed by a free-algebra computation that the strong condition can fail, without producing a counterexample to the construction. Lean states the conjecture with the bracket as a function with multilinearity and skew-symmetry hypotheses, over any field of characteristic 0, and proves its negation.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $n\geq 2$ be an integer. Let $(L,\cdot,[\cdot,\ldots,\cdot])$ be a **transposed Poisson $n$-Lie algebra**, and let $D$ be a derivation of both $(L,\cdot)$ and $(L,[\cdot,\ldots,\cdot])$. Define an $(n+1)$-ary operation $$ \mu(a_1,\ldots,a_{n+1})=\sum_{i=1}^{n+1}(-1)^{i-1}D(a_i)[a_1,\ldots,\hat{a}_i,\ldots,a_{n+1}], $$ where $\hat{a}_i$ denotes omission of the $i$-th entry. **Derivation construction conjecture.** Then $(L,\cdot,\mu(\cdot,\ldots,\cdot))$ is a transposed Poisson $(n+1)$-Lie algebra. This conjecture extends the known construction for transposed Poisson 2-Lie algebras and addresses the open question of constructing and classifying simple transposed Poisson $n$-Lie algebras. Its validity for arbitrary $n\geq 2$ and the stated derivations remains open.
- **Lean endpoint:** `TP366504.not_derivationConstructionConjecture`, `TP366504.mathdb366504_rat`, `TP366504.mathdb366504_complex`
- **Replay record:** `transposed-poisson-366504-root-replay-opus55-20261121-r1 (root, Claude Opus 5.5; public-claim-100/replay-366504.log all_ok, 6 modules)`; acceptance record `transposed-poisson-derivation-construction-366504-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/366504-transposed-poisson-derivation-construction`

### 366674 · A q-analogue of the Van Hamme–Sun supercongruence equivalence

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 146)
- **Source:** Victor J. W. Guo and Chen Wang, “Refinements of Van Hamme's (E.2) and (F.2) supercongruences and two supercongruences by Swisher”, arXiv:2501.09626 (2025).
- **What is proved (exact):** The congruence holds modulo [n]Φ_n(q)³ for every positive n ≡ 1 (mod 4). The proof finds a common finite q-WZ recurrence for the two sides under the parameter shift a → aq⁴, with matching boundary terms, which gives divisibility of the difference by Φ_n(q)⁴ at every primitive n-th root of unity, and treats the proper divisors d of n (whose Φ_d divide [n]) by a separate cancellation. Lean states the congruence as divisibility of the numerator of the reduced difference by [n]Φ_n(q)³ in ℚ[q], proves the only denominator in the statement nonzero, includes the review's required statement verbatim, and proves that the difference is not zero at n = 5.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $n\equiv 1\pmod{4}$ be a positive integer. Define the $q$-integer $[m]=(1-q^m)/(1-q)$, the $q$-shifted factorial $(x;q)_k=(1-x)(1-xq)\cdots(1-xq^{k-1})$, and let $\Phi_n(q)$ denote the $n$-th cyclotomic polynomial. **q-analogue conjecture.** $$ \sum_{k=0}^{n-1}(-1)^k[6k+1]\frac{(q;q^2)_k^3}{(q^4;q^4)_k^3}q^{3k^2} \equiv \sum_{k=0}^{n-1}(-1)^k[8k+1]\frac{(q;q^4)_k^3}{(q^4;q^4)_k^3}q^{2k^2+k} \pmod{[n]\Phi_n(q)^3}. $$ This is a proposed $q$-analogue of an equivalence between two supercongruences for primes congruent to $1$ modulo $4$; its validity for all positive integers $n\equiv1\pmod4$ remains open.
- **Lean endpoint:** `GuoWang.guo_wang_conj_4_1`, `ReviewEndpoint.guo_wang_conj_4_1`, `GuoWang.reviewed_source_form`, `GuoWang.poch_den_ne_zero`, `GuoWang.Clear.diff_five_num_ne_zero`

### 367377 · Kapranov–Schechtman's total-positivity conjecture for finite Coxeter groups

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 153)
- **Source:** Zhentao Wang, Jiawen Xie and Xuhang Zhang, “On the Total Positivity of Contingency Metamatrices”, arXiv:2503.02213 (2025).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture fails for the Coxeter group of type D_27: the 16×16 submatrix of M(D_27) with rows p = 0..15 and columns q = 12..27 has negative determinant (a 372-digit integer), so M(D_27) is not totally positive, not even totally nonnegative. The source proves the conjecture for type B and the exceptional types and does no type-D computation; every rank from 2 to 26 is totally positive (reproduced independently by two lanes, not formalized and not claimed), so no small-rank check finds the failure. The proof identifies parabolic subgroups with stabilizers of face vectors, counts double cosets by Burnside's lemma through fixed-face counts with a type-D gluing identity (Stembridge's fixed-face data are the closest prior work), and turns the result into an explicit integer formula for every entry. Lean works with the concrete group of even signed permutations of 27 coordinates and its 27 standard reflections (the D_27 fork), defines parabolics as generated subgroups and entries as Nat.card of Mathlib's double-coset quotient, proves the formula, evaluates all 256 entries of the minor…
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $(W,S)$ be a finite Coxeter group of rank $|S|=n$. For subsets $I,J\subset S$, let $W_I$ and $W_J$ be the parabolic subgroups of $W$ of types $I$ and $J$, respectively. Define the metamatrix $M(W)=(M_{pq})_{0\leq p,q\leq n}$ by $$ M_{pq}=\sum_{I,J\subset S,|I|=p,|J|=q}|W_I\backslash W/W_J|. $$ **Kapranov–Schechtman's conjecture.** The metamatrix $M(W)$ is totally positive. This extends the known type-$A$ result, where $W=S_n$, to arbitrary finite real reflection groups. The conjecture is proved in the source for type $B$ and the exceptional types, while the general finite-Coxeter-group case remains open.

### 367529 · Sign-change conjecture for Birch–Erdős representation functions

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 161)
- **Source:** Yuchen Ding, Honghu Liu and Zi Wang, “Note on a theorem of Birch and Erdős”, arXiv:2503.11676 (2025).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture holds: for all coprime q > p > 2, the difference f_{p,q}(n+1) − f_{p,q}(n) is strictly positive for infinitely many n and strictly negative for infinitely many n, where f_{p,q}(n) counts the representations of n as a sum of distinct numbers p^a q^b. Infinitely many increases follow from the authors' Corollary 3 (f is unbounded). The new part is the decreases: if f were eventually nondecreasing, a comparison at roots of unity would force certain twisted products over pure powers to vanish, while the exact product over all roots of unity gives a uniform lower bound; the contradiction needs p > 2 (for p = 2 the authors prove that f is monotone). Lean defines f by the source's literal representation count and proves both infinitely many strict increases and infinitely many strict decreases, with non-vacuity checks.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $f_{p,q}(n)$ be the Birch–Erdős representation function, with $p$ and $q$ relatively prime integers satisfying $q>p>2$. **Sign-change conjecture.** The sign of $$ f_{p,q}(n+1)-f_{p,q}(n) $$ changes infinitely often. The conjecture is presented as a possible example of the Chebyshev bias phenomenon; the source does not establish it.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Fpq.ding_liu_wang_conj3`, `Fpq.conj3`

### 368177 · The uri multiplicity threshold shuffle conjecture

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 167)
- **Source:** Annika Burmester and Ulf Kühn, “On post-Lie structures for free Lie algebras”, arXiv:2504.19661 (2025).
- **What is proved (exact):** The uri multiplicities satisfy threshold shuffle identity (i) of the source's Definition A.5 in full, and identity (ii) whenever the middle composition τ is nonempty; identity (ii) as printed, with no condition on τ, is false (at σ = τ = ∅, ϑ = (1), d₁ = d₂ = 0 it would assert 0 = −1). The corrected statement is what the source needs: the proof of its Theorem 5.11 uses (i) for adjacent factors and (ii) for factors separated by a nonempty word, its Example A.6(ii) follows that split, and the normalized solutions of its Remark A.8 are incompatible with the printed empty case. The proof expands the multiplicities over the cuts of a word, uses the compatibility of shuffle with deconcatenation and a divided-difference identity for the Bernoulli-type numbers B₁(m, n), and cancels the remaining alternating sum over cuts of the middle word, which is where τ ≠ ∅ enters. Lean states Definition A.5 and the multiplicities literally (with multiplicity 0 at non-positive thresholds, which implements the omission of inadmissible insertions), proves (i), proves (ii) for τ ≠ ∅, proves the counterexam…
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $\mathcal{C}$ be the set of compositions, let $\ell(\boldsymbol{\alpha})$ be the length of a composition, and let $\operatorname{ind}_a(\boldsymbol{\alpha})$ be its index at $a$. Define the uri multiplicities by $$ \mu_{a,\boldsymbol{\alpha}}=B_1(\ell(\boldsymbol{\alpha}),\operatorname{ind}_a(\boldsymbol{\alpha})), $$ where $$ B_1(m,n)=\frac{1}{m!}\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\binom{m}{k}B_k. $$ **Uri multiplicity threshold shuffle conjecture.** The uri multiplicities satisfy the threshold shuffle identities from the source's definition. ### 369952 · Failure of the matroid matching property in odd-dimensional rigidity matroids

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 81)
- **Source:** John Hewetson, Bill Jackson, Anthony Nixon and Ben Smith, “k-fold circuits and coning in rigidity matroids”, arXiv:2508.18838 (2026).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every k >= 2 and every n >= 2(2k+3)^2 + 2(2k+3) - 1, the generic rigidity matroid R_{2k+1}(K_n) does not have the matroid matching property: an explicit family of h edge pairs has maximum matching h-2 while the Dress-Lovasz min-max bound is h-1. Hewetson-Jackson-Nixon-Smith, arXiv:2508.18838, Conjecture 3.22 (v2) / 3.20 (v1).
- **MathDB record statement:** For integers $k\geq 2$ and sufficiently large $n$, consider the rigidity matroid ${\mathcal R}_{2k+1}(K_n)$ of the complete graph $K_n$ in dimension $2k+1$. **The matroid matching conjecture.** The matroid ${\mathcal R}_{2k+1}(K_n)$ does not have the matroid matching property for all $k\geq 2$ and sufficiently large $n$. The preceding proof establishes the corresponding even-dimensional result, while the odd-dimensional case is suspected to fail in this way for dimensions at least five and large complete graphs.
- **Lean endpoint:** `literal endpoint per CROSSWALK.md (independent extracted replay PASS, 41 modules)`

### 370688 · Switch-generation conjecture for the matching preorder

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 132)
- **Source:** Joseph Briggs, Jessica McDonald and Songling Shan, “Degree sequences realizing labelled perfect matchings”, arXiv:2510.01110 (2025).
- **What is proved (exact):** For perfect matchings N and M of the vertex set {1, ..., n}, write N ⪯ M when every weakly decreasing degree sequence that has a realization containing M also has one containing N. If N ⪯ M then there is a chain N = M_1 ⪯ M_2 ⪯ ... ⪯ M_k = M in which each step is one of the three switches of the source. This is Conjecture 11 of Briggs, McDonald and Shan. The proof shows that the preorder is the reverse Bruhat order on fixed-point-free involutions: a new separating-graph lemma produces, whenever a crossing-count inequality fails, a degree sequence realized with M but not with N; a direct induction on rank defects then moves from N toward M one switch at a time, and the source's Lemma 10(b) keeps every step inside the preorder. Lean defines matchings as edge sets, realizability by actual simple graphs with prescribed degrees, and the three switch types literally.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $\mathcal{M}_n$ be the set of the labelled perfect matchings under consideration, and let $\preceq$ be the preorder on this set. A **switch** is the local transformation defined earlier in the paper. **Switch-generation conjecture.** If $N,M\in\mathcal{M}_n$ satisfy $N\preceq M$, then there exists a sequence $$ N=M_1\preceq M_2\preceq\dots\preceq M_{k-1}\preceq M_k=M $$ such that $M_j$ is a switch of $M_{j+1}$ for every $j$. This is presented as an open conjecture about whether the preorder is generated by switches; the supplied parser gives no evidence of resolution.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Matching370688.EdgePM.conjecture_11`, `Matching370688.conjecture_11`
- **Replay record:** `matching-preorder-370688-root-replay-fable-20261120-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2; public-claim-100/replay-370688.log all_ok)`; acceptance record `matching-preorder-switch-generation-370688-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/370688-matching-preorder-switch-generation`

### 372205 · Existence of regular open and closed XOR-magic graphs of power-of-two order

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 15)
- **Source:** Sylwia Cichacz, Hubert Grochowski and Rita Zuazua, “Open XOR-magic odd graphs and closed XOR-magic even graphs”, arXiv:2512.19278 (2025).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every n >= 4 and every odd d with 5 <= d <= 2^n-5 there is a connected d-regular open XOR-magic graph of order 2^n, and for every even d with 4 <= d <= 2^n-6 a connected d-regular closed one. This proves the Cichacz-Grochowski-Zuazua existence conjecture for every degree not excluded by the known nonexistence result (Batal; source Prop. 24), which is the scope the MathDB record states; the printed closed endpoint 2^n-4 is impossible by that known result, so the conjecture as literally printed is false there (no credit claimed for that correction).
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** READING
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $n$ be a positive integer, and let $d$ be a degree. An **open XOR-magic graph** and a **closed XOR-magic graph** are graphs admitting, respectively, an open or closed XOR-magic labeling. The graphs below are required to have order $2^n$. For each degree $$ d\in\{5,7,\ldots,2^n-7,2^n-5\}, $$ there exists a $d$-regular open XOR-magic graph of order $2^n$. For each degree $$ d\in\{4,6,\ldots,2^n-6,2^n-4\}, $$ there exists a $d$-regular closed XOR-magic graph of order $2^n$. **Existence conjecture.** For every permitted degree $d$ in the two displayed sets, the corresponding regular open or closed XOR-magic graph exists. This would complete the existence picture apart from the degrees excluded by the previously stated nonexistence result and extend the constructions known for order $16$ and order $8$ to every order that is a power of two.
- **Lean endpoint:** `XorMagic.corrected_all : ∀ n : ℕ, 4 ≤ n → CorrectedAt n; XorMagic.not_printed_each_ge_four : ∀ n : ℕ, 4 ≤ n → ¬ PrintedAt n (also printed_not_all, printed_not_positive)`
- **Replay record:** `xor-magic-parent-final-r1/replay-result.json`; acceptance record `ledger.json#accepted`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/372205-all-corrected-odd-open-and-even-closed-xor-magic-degrees-at`

### 372803 · The minimum-fort-size conjecture for marginal observances

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 106)
- **Source:** Beth Bjorkman, Sean English and Johnathan Koch, “Cost-Benefit Analysis for PMU Placement in Power Grids”, arXiv:2601.19775 (2026).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture that minimum fort size at least 4 forces every marginal observance MObs(G;i), for i up to the power domination number, to be at least 4 is false. An explicit graph on 25 vertices, built from two copies of the Fano plane with two lines removed joined through a hub, has no fort of size at most 3, maximum observed-set sizes 11 and 14 with one and two sensors, and power domination number at least 3, so its second marginal observance is 3. (By the referee's computation the maximum observances are 0, 11, 14, 19, 25 and the power domination number is 4.) A generic random search over about 80 million graphs on 10 to 28 vertices found no counterexample; the witness comes from a structured construction.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $G$ be a graph. Write $\underline{f}(G)$ for the minimum fort size of $G$, let $\mathrm{MObs}(G;i)$ denote the marginal observance at step $i$, and let $\gamma_P(G)$ denote the power domination number. For a positive integer $k$, write $[k]=\{1,\ldots,k\}$. **Minimum-fort-size conjecture.** If $$ \underline{f}(G)\geq 4, $$ then $$ \mathrm{MObs}(G;i)\geq 4 $$ for all $i\in[\gamma_P(G)]$. This asks whether the relationship between minimum fort size and marginal observances established for smaller minimum fort sizes extends to minimum fort size at least four; its status is not resolved in the source.
- **Lean endpoint:** `FortObservance.minimumFort_marginalObs_refuted`
- **Replay record:** `fort-observance-372803-root-final-replay-fable-20261119-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2)`; acceptance record `minimum-fort-size-marginal-observance-372803-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/372803-minimum-fort-size-marginal-observance`

### 372900 · The shift-additivity conjecture for periodic binary sequences

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 166)
- **Source:** Qin Yuan, Chunlei Li and Xiangyong Zeng, “The structure and enumeration of periodic binary sequences with high nonlinear complexity”, arXiv:2602.01134 (2026).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture holds in its full range c ≥ ⌈n/2⌉. The source's Lemma 7 and Corollary 1 give b = d₂ when the two spacings and the two additivity parameters satisfy an inequality, which covers c ≥ ⌈(2n−1)/3⌉, and the authors say that their technique does not reach further. The new tool is a Run Lemma about the multiset of lengths of zero runs of the cyclic difference word of a sequence in the class B(n, c, d): comparing these run lengths before and after the shift handles the overlap that the published argument leaves open, and a reflection argument on cyclic windows finishes the proof. Lean states the source's Definitions 2 and 3 and the shift R^b literally for sequences Fin n → Bool, includes the membership of the original sequence in B(n, c, n − c) that the MathDB paraphrase leaves implicit, proves that the additivity parameter exists and is unique on the class, and proves the conjecture; it also shows by an example that the strict inequality in the hypothesis is needed.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $n$ be a positive integer, let $c\geq \left\lceil\frac{n}{2}\right\rceil$, and let $d=n-c$. Let $\mathcal{B}(n,c,d)$ denote the specified class of binary sequences, let $R^b(\mathbf{s}_n)$ denote the $b$-fold shift of $\mathbf{s}_n$, and let $\operatorname{add}(\mathbf{s}_n)$ denote its additivity parameter. Suppose that $0<b<n$ and that, for some $d_2$, $R^b(\mathbf{s}_n)\in\mathcal{B}(n,c,d_2)$ with $\operatorname{add}(R^b(\mathbf{s}_n))>\operatorname{add}(\mathbf{s}_n)$. **Shift-additivity conjecture.** Then $b=d_2$. The conjecture would extend the statements of the cited proposition and corollary from $c\geq\left\lceil\frac{2n-1}{3}\right\rceil$ to $c\geq\left\lceil\frac{n}{2}\right\rceil$, allowing the same conclusions about the unique relevant shift and the nonlinear complexity of the associated periodic sequence; the source reports experimental evidence but says that the prop…

### 374250 · Completeness conjecture for regular Inverse Treblecross positions

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 170)
- **Source:** Kai Liang and Muxi Li, “A Weak Solution of Inverse Treblecross”, arXiv:2604.16759 (2026).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture (as printed in version 1 of the source, which the MathDB record follows) holds: among boards of Inverse Treblecross with at most one X, those with Sprague–Grundy value at most 1 are exactly the boards of the regular set R together with [1X1], [1X4], [4X1] and [4X4], and each of these four has value exactly 1 and lies outside R. The source computes the values on R and tabulates small cases; what was missing is that no other board, of any length, has value at most 1. Mixed-residue cases are settled in two moves from the source's values for empty boards. When both gaps lie in the critical residue classes, a move at distance four supplies the missing option once the values of an auxiliary family of two-piece boards are known, and those are certified by a coupled induction: a finite abstract-interpretation certificate (each gap known exactly when small, otherwise by its residue modulo 10) checked for all gap lengths at once. Lean defines the game literally on lists of Booleans (a move fills an empty cell without creating three adjacent X's; the value is the mex of the opti…
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $R$ be the regular set of board states, let a state have at most one piece, and let $\mathcal{G}$ denote its Sprague–Grundy value. **Completeness conjecture.** The regular set $R$ contains all board states that possess at most one piece and satisfy $\mathcal{G} \le 1$, with exactly four isolated exceptions: $$ [1 X 1],\quad [1 X 4],\quad [4 X 1],\quad \text{and}\quad [4 X 4]. $$ These four states are described as isolated anomalies, and empirical data indicates that all other sufficiently long states in their residue classes have $\mathcal{G}>1$; the completeness of $R$ beyond the stated exceptions remains unproved.

### 374252 · Generalized reflectional periodicity conjecture for odd Inverse k-cross games

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 173)
- **Source:** Kai Liang and Muxi Li, “A Weak Solution of Inverse Treblecross”, arXiv:2604.16759 (2026).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture holds, and more is true: for every odd k = 2m+1 the Sprague–Grundy value of the empty board of length n is (n + [n mod (3m+2) ≥ k]) mod 2. With P = 3m+2, either player can force the game to end with exactly 2⌊n/P⌋ + [n mod P ≥ k] empty cells, even when one extra nim-heap of size one is added: the board is cut into blocks of k + (m+1) cells, each containing m+1 disjoint supports and only m high cells, and the defender answers inside the block the opponent just played in. So the length of the game is fixed and its parity gives the value; the bound G ≤ 1 and both identities follow from the formula. Lean defines the game on the sets of unmarked cells (a position is legal when every window of k cells contains an unmarked cell), the options and the Sprague–Grundy value as a minimum excluded value, proves the three statements of the conjecture and the closed formula for every m ≥ 0, and proves that the moves of this model are, move for move, the placements of the source's game on a Boolean board.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $k=2m+1$ with $m\geq1$, and consider an Inverse $k$-cross game with empty-board state $[a]$. Let $\mathcal{G}([a])$ denote its Sprague–Grundy value, and let $\oplus$ denote nim-sum. **Generalized reflectional periodicity conjecture.** For every such game, $\mathcal{G}([a])\leq1$, and $$ \mathcal{G}([2+3m+a])=\mathcal{G}([a])\oplus(m\bmod2), $$ $$ \mathcal{G}([2+5m-a])=\mathcal{G}([a])\oplus(m\bmod2). $$ For $k=2$, eventual periodicity is known through the identification with Dawson's Kayles, whereas for even $k>2$ eventual periodicity is unknown; the asserted uniform bound and reflectional periodicity for odd constraints remain conjectural.
- **Lean endpoint:** `OddInverseKCross.odd_inverse_kcross_bit`, `OddInverseKCross.odd_inverse_kcross_translation`, `OddInverseKCross.odd_inverse_kcross_reflection`, `OddInverseKCross.odd_inverse_kcross_formula`, `OddInverseKCross.boardCuts_source_move_iff`, `OddInverseKCross.odd_inverse_kcross_nonvacuous`
- **Replay record:** `inverse-kcross-374252-root-replay-fable-20261123-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2; public-claim-100/replay-374252.log all_ok, 1 module)`; acceptance record `inverse-kcross-odd-empty-boards-374252-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/374252-inverse-kcross-odd-empty-boards`

### 374966 · Lattice-realization conjecture for preorder lattice-point posets

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 83)
- **Source:** Frédéric Chapoton and Christos A. Athanasiadis, “Polytopes and posets associated to preorders”, arXiv:2605.26916 (2026).
- **What is proved (exact):** For the three-element preorder with two incomparable minima below one maximum, no finite graded lattice of rank three has M-triangle equal to the rational transmutation of the M-triangle of the preorder polytope's lattice-point poset. This refutes Conjecture 8.1.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** PART_ONLY
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $\tau$ be a preorder of size $n$, let $P_\tau$ be its lattice-point poset, let $M_{P_\tau}(x,y)$ be its $M$-triangle, and let $\overline{M}_{P_\tau}$ be the transmutation defined by $$ \overline f(x,y)=f\left(\frac{1-y}{1-xy},1-xy\right). $$ **Lattice-realization conjecture.** There exists a finite graded lattice $L_\tau$ of rank $n$ such that $$ \mathcal Z(P_\tau,t)=\mathcal Z(L_\tau,t),\qquad \overline M_{P_\tau}(x,y)=M_{L_\tau}(x,y). $$ In particular, the rank-generating polynomial of $L_\tau$ equals $h(\tau,t)$. The conjecture extends the arbor setting; the source gives examples for chain preorders but no general resolution.
- **Lean endpoint:** `A374966.conj81_false_at_corolla_native`
- **Replay record:** `preorder-corolla-374966-independent-extracted-replay-opus-20261118-r1`; acceptance record `preorder-lattice-realization-corolla-374966-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/374966-preorder-lattice-realization-corolla`
- **Kernel-checked versus paper-only:** Lean checks the native rank and transmutation obstruction for the three-element corolla. The written proof extends it to every corolla with at least two leaves and discusses the opposite corolla and zeta polynomial.

### 375588 · Conjectural interval-family homomorphisms from dihedral type to type A

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 168)
- **Source:** Arkady Berenstein, Jacob Greenstein and Jian-Rong Li, “Artin monoids, their homomorphisms and twins”, arXiv:2606.26776 (2026).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture holds in all three cases and for every n: the two half twists (positive lifts of the longest elements on the intervals [1,a] and [b,n−1]) satisfy the braid relation of length N in the positive braid monoid, so they define a monoid homomorphism from the dihedral Artin monoid Br⁺(I₂(N)). Cases I and II follow from a periodic-braid identity: rotation and overlap identities for half twists, proved by positive rewriting. Case III (N = 6) comes from a seven-move identity on four strands, transported to all sizes by cabling with strands of unequal width. The source proves the subfamilies N = 3 (of case II) and N = 4, b ≥ 2 (of case I) and reports computer verification for n ≤ 15. Lean works in the presented positive monoid on σ₁, …, σ_{n−1} with the braid and commutation relations, defines the half twists as explicit triangular positive words, and proves both the alternating relation and the existence of the homomorphism with no premise beyond the conjecture's hypotheses; an earlier group-level proof is kept.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $1\le b\le a<n-1$ and $a>1$. For the type-$A$ braid monoid, write $w_0^{[r,s]}$ for the longest element of the parabolic subgroup on the interval $[r,s]$. **Interval-family homomorphism conjecture.** The assignments $$ \widehat{T}_1\mapsto T_{w_0^{[1,a]}},\qquad \widehat{T}_2\mapsto T_{w_0^{[b,n-1]}} $$ define a homomorphism $\operatorname{Br}^+(I_2(N))\to\operatorname{Br}^+_n$ if one of the following holds: $$ N=\frac{2n}{n+b-a-2}\in2\mathbb Z_{>0}; $$ $$ a+b=n\quad\text{and}\quad N=\frac{n}{b-1}\in1+2\mathbb Z_{>0}; $$ $$ a+1=2(b-1),\quad a+b<n,\quad\text{and}\quad N=6. $$ The paper presents these as a conjectural infinite family of non-disjoint standard homomorphisms and reports verification for $n\le15$.
- **Lean endpoint:** `BGL.conj_6_35_monoid`, `BGL.conj_6_35_monoid_hom`, `BGL.conj_6_35_group`
- **Replay record:** `braid-halftwist-375588-root-replay-fable-20261123-r2 (root, Claude Fable 5.2; public-claim-100/replay-375588-r2.log all_ok, 14 modules)`; acceptance record `dihedral-half-twist-homomorphisms-375588-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/375588-dihedral-half-twist-homomorphisms`

### 380054 · Uniqueness of terminal transversals under $(213)\rightarrow(123)$ moves

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 64)
- **Source:** Zvezdelina Stankova, “Shape-Wilf-ordering of permutations of length 3”, arXiv:math/0609644 (2006).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every finite Young diagram with a transversal, rectangle-contained 213→123 swaps starting from that transversal have no infinite chain, and any two reachable transversals have a common descendant. There is exactly one reachable 213-avoiding transversal, attained by a finite maximal sequence, and every finite maximal sequence ends there.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** LITERAL_STRONGER
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $Y$ be the Young diagram under consideration, let $S_Y$ denote its transversals, and let $S_Y(213)$ denote the transversals avoiding the pattern $(213)$. For a transversal $T\in S_Y$, consider sequences of $(213)\rightarrow(123)$ moves, each replacing an occurrence of the pattern $(213)$ by $(123)$. **Uniqueness conjecture.** Starting with a transversal $T\in S_Y$, all sequences of $(213)\rightarrow(123)$ moves terminate in the same transversal $T'\in S_Y(213)$. This is a confluence assertion for the pattern moves and would make the $(213)$-avoiding terminal transversal canonically determined by the starting transversal. The supplied text does not indicate whether the assertion has been proved or remains open.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Young213.source_shape_complete`

### 380247 · The strengthened 2-criticality conjecture for row-isotoped abelian 2-groups

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 175)
- **Source:** Carlo Hamalainen, “New 2–critical sets in the abelian 2–group”, arXiv:math/0612338 (2006).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture holds for every order 2^s and every pair of rows less than three apart, for the greedy order that the source describes in words and uses throughout (bottom row first, right to left). For each such swap the greedy critical set is given by explicit blank-cell masks written in binary across block boundaries; each clue is the lexicographically least cell of a private intercalate, which gives 2-criticality, and in each row the blanks are forced one at a time along an acyclic forcing graph, which gives strong completion and top-down completion. The source proved the case where both rows lie in an aligned block of four (its Theorem 5.1) and checked the rest by computer through order 32; the new cases are the swaps that cross a block boundary, of which the odd adjacent ones are easy and the two distance-two families carry the substance. Lean defines the square by bitwise exclusive or and proves it equal to the source's recursive square, defines the greedy algorithm with its uniqueness test over all Latin squares of the order, 2-criticality by private intercalates, strong comp…
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $L_s$ be the latin square of order $n=2^s$, and let $\alpha_{k,k'}$ be the row isotopism that interchanges rows $k$ and $k'$ and fixes every other row. Suppose that $$ \left|k-k'\right|<3. $$ **Strengthened 2-criticality conjecture.** The set $\operatorname{gcs}(\alpha_{k,k'}L_s)$ is 2-critical and strong, and completes top down to $\alpha_{k,k'}L_s$. The theorem preceding this conjecture proves the claim under the additional hypothesis that $k$ and $k'$ lie among four consecutive indices beginning at a multiple of $4$. The authors expect that this stronger version remains true when that additional condition is weakened, but no resolution is given here.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Rowswap.conj_6_1`, `Rowswap.printed_order_counterexample`, `Rowswap.Ls_eq_recursiveSquare`, `Rowswap.printedOrder_eq_formula`

### 381408 · The Catalan triangle Laurent polynomial and positivity conjecture

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 100)
- **Source:** Victor J. W. Guo and Xiuguo Lian, “Proofs of two conjectures on Catalan triangle numbers”, arXiv:1806.02685 (2018).
- **What is proved (exact):** For positive integers n_1,...,n_m with n_{m+1}=n_1, every integer j, and nonnegative integers a, r with a <= n_1, the expression (1/[n_m+a]) [n_1+n_m choose n_1-a]_q^{-1} sum_{k=a}^{n_1} [2k][k]^{2r} q^{jk^2-(r+1)k} prod_{i=1}^m [n_i+n_{i+1} choose n_i+k]_q is a Laurent polynomial in q with integer coefficients, and its coefficients are nonnegative when 0 <= j <= m (Guo-Lian, arXiv:1806.02685, Conjecture 6.1; MathDB 381408 = 339461).
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $n_1,\ldots,n_m,n_{m+1}=n_1$ be positive integers. For an integer $j$ and non-negative integers $a,r$ with $a\leqslant n_1$, consider $$ \frac{1}{[n_m+a]}{n_1+n_m\brack n_1-a}^{-1}\sum_{k=a}^{n_1}[2k][k]^{2r}q^{jk^2-(r+1)k}\prod_{i=1}^{m}{n_i+n_{i+1}\brack n_i+k}. $$ **Catalan triangle Laurent polynomial and positivity conjecture.** This expression is a Laurent polynomial in $q$, and it has non-negative integer coefficients when $0\leqslant j\leqslant m$. This is presented as a stronger version of Theorem and as a generalization of a conjecture attributed to Guo and Wang; the supplied span does not state whether it has since been resolved.

### 381433 · Cardoso–Luz conjecture on adverse graphs

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 17)
- **Source:** Domingos M. Cardoso, “A survey on graphs with convex quadratic stability number”, arXiv:1811.05516 (2018).
- **What is proved (exact):** The cyclic Latin-square graph on the 36 cells of the order-six addition table has no isolated vertices, has least adjacency eigenvalue −3 and quadratic stability bound 6, and both values persist after deletion of the open neighborhood of each vertex. Its stability number is 5, so it is an adverse graph outside the class where the quadratic bound equals the stability number.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** LITERAL_STRONGER
- **MathDB record statement:** An **adverse graph** is a graph $H$ without isolated vertices such that $upsilon(H)$ and $lambda_{min}(H)$ are integers, and, for every vertex $i\in V(H)$, $$ \upsilon(H-N_H(i))=\upsilon(H),\qquad \lambda_{min}(H-N_H(i))=\lambda_{min}(H). $$ Here $upsilon(H)$ is the optimal value of the quadratic stability-number bound and $lambda_{min}(H)$ is the least eigenvalue of $H$. A graph is a **$\mathcal{Q}$-graph** when it belongs to the class $\mathcal{Q}$ defined in the paper. **Cardoso–Luz conjecture.** All adverse graphs are $\mathcal{Q}$-graphs. Computational tests with adverse graphs supported the conjecture, but it remains open. Its resolution would clarify the polynomial-time recognition of $\mathcal{Q}$-graphs.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Adverse381433.refutation_of_source_statement : ¬ SourceConjecture (with edge hypothesis); Adverse381433.refutation : ¬ CardosoLuzConjecture; IndependentReview.refutes_stricter_source (non-complete va…`
- **Replay record:** `adverse-parent-final-r1/replay-result.json`; acceptance record `ledger.json#accepted`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/381433-an-adverse-non-q-graph-from-the-cyclic-latin-square-of-order`
- **Kernel-checked versus paper-only:** Lean checks the graph, eigenvalue, quadratic and independence certificates, and a reviewer theorem also handles the stricter non-complete formulation. The paper explains the certificates and their origin in Latin-square transversals.
- **Prior art searched:** `uniform-prior-art-sweep-B2-sol62-20261119-r1`; sweep verdict PARTIAL_OVERLAP; 47 queries and routes recorded; routes refused: arXiv export API disallowed by https://export.arxiv.org/robots.txt; Google Scholar /scholar disallowed by https://scholar.google.com/robots.txt; MathDB not checked (recorded automated-access refusal); OpenAlex source: not run after OpenAlex 429; OpenAlex cita…

### 381790 · Exact formula conjecture for the rainbow-saturation coefficient

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 144)
- **Source:** Debsoumya Chakraborti, Kevin Hendrey, Ben Lund and Casey Tompkins, “Rainbow saturation for complete graphs”, arXiv:2212.04640 (2024).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjectured formula fails: f(514) ≤ 536 < 537. Take three parts A, B, C of t vertices with every edge between different parts and none inside a part; add one private vertex for each triangle abc, put all private vertices in a clique joined to everything else, and give the edge ab and the edges from each c to its private vertex for (a, b, c) one shared colour, every other edge its own colour. A clique uses at most one vertex of C, so it meets each shared colour at most once, except the full triangle with its own private vertex; this gives a member of the source's class with t³ + 3t vertices for k = t³ + 2, which beats the formula from t = 8 on. A variable-core version gives f(k) ≤ k + 3⌈(k−2)^{1/3}⌉ for every k ≥ 4. Since the source's Lemma 3.3 proves α_{k+2} = f(k), the printed chain is false; together with the source's lower bound (its Lemma 3.10, quoted and not formalized), α_r − r = Θ(r^{1/3}), against the square-root growth the formula predicts. Lean states the source's three properties literally with colours in ℕ (the source's own palette), proves the refutation, the t-fami…
- **MathDB record statement:** For each integer $k\geq 4$, let $f(k)$ be the auxiliary extremal function from the paper and let $\alpha_{k+2}$ be the coefficient in the asymptotic formula for rainbow saturation, so that $\operatorname{sat}(n,\mathcal{R}(K_{k+2}))=\alpha_{k+2}n+O(1)$. **Exact coefficient conjecture.** For every $k\geq 4$, $$ \alpha_{k+2}=f(k)=k+\left\lceil\frac{-1+\sqrt{4k-3}}{2}\right\rceil. $$ This conjecture is proposed as a way to close the gap between the lower and upper bounds for rainbow saturation by determining the auxiliary function $f(k)$ exactly. The supplied text does not state whether it has been resolved.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Rainbow381790.chlt_conj_5_1_false`, `Rainbow381790.f_514_lt_conjFormula`, `Rainbow381790.f_family_le`, `Rainbow381790.f_le_all_k`, `Rainbow381790.conj_fails_on_family`

### 382130 · The local reversal edge conjecture for characteristic imset polytopes

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 65)
- **Source:** Petter Restadh, “Diameters of the Characteristic Imset Polytopes”, arXiv:2302.03647 (2023).
- **What is proved (exact):** There is a five-vertex DAG C with eight arrows for which making vertex 4 a sink (one-based numbering) yields a DAG D on the same skeleton that is not Markov equivalent to C. The segment between their characteristic imsets is not an edge of the convex hull of characteristic imsets of all acyclic orientations of their skeleton; the certificate also shows it is not a face.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** LITERAL
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $\mathcal{G}$ be a directed acyclic graph with skeleton $G$, and let $i$ be a vertex. Define $\mathcal{G}_{\downarrow i}$ by orienting every edge between $i$ and a neighbor toward $i$, while leaving all other edges as in $\mathcal{G}$. Write $c_\mathcal{G}$ and $c_{\mathcal{G}_{\downarrow i}}$ for their characteristic imsets, and let $\CIM_G$ denote the characteristic imset polytope associated with $G$. **Local reversal edge conjecture.** If $\mathcal{G}$ and $\mathcal{G}_{\downarrow i}$ are not Markov equivalent, then $$ \operatorname{conv}\left(c_\mathcal{G},c_{\mathcal{G}_{\downarrow i}}\right) $$ is an edge of $\CIM_G$. A linear bound on the diameter of $\CIM_G$ would follow from this conjecture; the source establishes quadratic bounds in general and linear bounds for trees and for the whole polytope, but does not resolve the conjecture for arbitrary skeletons.
- **Lean endpoint:** `ImsetSink.source_negation : ¬ ImsetSink.UniversalSinkEdge`, `ImsetSink.explicit_five_counterexample`
- **Replay record:** `imset-sink-independent-strict-replay-sol-r1/REPLAY-AND-FIDELITY-v1.md`; acceptance record `imset-sink-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/382130-counterexample-to-the-characteristic-imset-sink-edge-conject`
- **Kernel-checked versus paper-only:** Lean checks both DAGs, same skeleton, sink operation, non-equivalence, all imset coordinates and the real convex nonface argument. The paper gives the same certificate and discusses the motivation in diameter bounds.

### 382161 · The affine type B distance conjecture

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 169)
- **Source:** Joel Brewster Lewis and Bridget Eileen Tenner, “Bargain hunting in a Coxeter group”, arXiv:2302.04404 (2023).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture holds for every n: the cheapest way to write an affine signed permutation w of type B as a product of reflections of that group, where a reflection costs its depth, costs dis(w)/2 + bl_C(w) − bl_B(w). Lower bound: every reflection changes the displacement by at most twice its cost, and a crossing-count lemma shows that each good cut that is not very good (an odd block) forces one extra unit of cost, because a product of reflections of the type B group restricted to a block that is preserved by all of them has an even number of sign changes. Upper bound: a greedy rule that always applies an efficient swap, and switches the partner of the swap whenever the obvious choice would close an odd block, reaches the identity at exactly that cost; its termination and invariants are proved. The source proves the analogous formulas where no branching occurs and in finite type D; in affine type B no depth formula was available. Lean defines the group as permutations of the integers with the periodicity, negation and parity conditions, takes costs over lists of reflections each of w…
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $w$ be an affine signed permutation in $\widetilde{S}^B_n$. An integer $j\in[n-1]$ is **good** if $w$ restricts to a bijection from $\pm[j]$ to itself, and it is **very good** if this restriction is an even-signed permutation. Define $$ \operatorname{bl}^{\widetilde{C}}(w)=1+\#\{\text{good values for }w\},\qquad \operatorname{bl}^{\widetilde{B}}(w)=1+\#\{\text{very good values for }w\}. $$ Here $\$(w)$ and $\operatorname{dis}(w)$ denote the quantities defined for affine signed permutations in the paper. **The affine type B conjecture.** If $w\in\widetilde{S}^B_n$, then $$ \$(w)=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{dis}(w)+\operatorname{bl}^{\widetilde{C}}(w)-\operatorname{bl}^{\widetilde{B}}(w). $$ This conjecture extends the finite-type formula to affine type $\widetilde{B}$. If true, it would imply $\$(w)-\operatorname{dis}(w)/2\leq n$ for all $w\in\widetilde{S}^B_n$; the corresponding bounde…

### 382260 · Gago–Coroničová-Hurajová–Madaras conjecture on low-betweenness uniform graphs

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 133)
- **Source:** Babak Ghanbari, David Hartman, Vít Jelínek, Aneta Pokorná, Robert Šámal and Pavel Valtr, “Structure of betweenness uniform graphs with low values of betweenness centrality”, arXiv:2401.00347 (2023).
- **What is proved (exact):** A connected graph in which every vertex has the same betweenness centrality, with that common value below 1, is the complement of a disjoint union of stars K_{1,l} of one common size l. This is Conjecture 2 of Ghanbari, Hartman, Jelinek, Pokorna, Samal and Valtr (the MathDB record's title credits other authors, whose different conjecture that paper disproved). The source proved the statement for components that are themselves uniform (its Theorem 17) and excluded small l case by case. The proof here replaces uniformity by Jensen's inequality in the source's excess argument and adds one new lemma, that every vertex is close to at least l + 1 edges of the complement, together with the equality case. Lean defines betweenness by counting shortest walks over unordered pairs of other vertices, proves the shortest-walk counting the argument needs, and includes guard theorems against the wrong normalizations.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $G$ be a **low-betweenness BUG**, meaning a betweenness-uniform graph whose betweenness value is less than $1$. Write $\overline{G}$ for its complement. **Gago–Coroničová-Hurajová–Madaras conjecture.** If $G$ is a low-betweenness BUG, then its complement $\overline{G}$ is a disjoint union of stars of the same size. This would classify the low-betweenness betweenness-uniform graphs and show that the construction from disjoint unions of equally sized stars yields all such examples. The conjecture is presented as open in the source.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Betweenness.lowBUG_complement_equal_stars`

### 382287 · Local antimagic chromatic number of even-by-odd flower-bouquet graphs

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 138)
- **Source:** Gee-Choon Lau, Wai Chee Shiu, M. Nalliah and K. Premalatha, “Construction of local antimagic 3-colorable graphs of fixed even size – matrix approach”, arXiv:2404.18049 (2024).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every even r >= 2 and odd s >= 3 the graph rFB(s) has a local antimagic labeling with exactly three vertex sums, so its local antimagic chromatic number is 3 (each blade contains a triangle, so 3 is a lower bound). The source proved the odd-by-odd case and posed this one; its method is blocked for odd s by a parity obstruction in the column sums of its labeling matrix. The new ingredient is a 21-blade trade gadget, found by a role-aware exact-cover search, that rebalances the parity across blades; with it the source's matrix supply and grouping extend to all even r and odd s, with ten small exceptional cases settled by explicit tables. Lean defines the literal graph (hub adjacent to every blade vertex, the two path edges in each blade), labels 1..|E| with |E| computed from the graph, the colour count over all vertices, and the chromatic number as an infimum shown to be attained.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $r$ and $s$ be integers, with $r$ even and $r\ge 2$, and $s$ odd and $s\ge 3$. Let $rFB(s)$ denote the corresponding flower-bouquet graph, and let $\chi_{la}(G)$ be the local antimagic chromatic number of a graph $G$. **The even-by-odd flower-bouquet conjecture.** $$ \chi_{la}(rFB(s))=3. $$ The paper proves the analogous equality for odd $r,s\ge 3$, while the even-$r$, odd-$s$ case is posed as an open problem.
- **Lean endpoint:** `RFB.conj_5_1`, `RFB.conj_5_1_attained`

### 323535 · Ilinca–Kahn conjectured bipartite matching entropy bound

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 92)
- **Source:** Liviu Ilinca and Jeff Kahn, “Asymptotics of the Upper Matching Conjecture”, arXiv:1205.4342 (2012).
- **What is proved (exact):** For each integer m≥3, the simple bipartite graph K_{m,m+1} disjoint union K_{1,1}, with parts of sizes ℓ=m+1 and M=m+2, has exactly (m+1)! size-ℓ matchings and violates the Ilinca–Kahn inequality strictly. Its base-two log matching count exceeds Σₓ[log₂(dₓ!)−log₂Γ(dₓ−tₓ+1)]/tₓ with tₓ=ℓdₓ/M.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** LITERAL_STRONGER
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $G$ be a bipartite graph on $X\cup Y$ with $|X|=\ell$ and $|Y|=M\geq \ell$. For each $x\in X$, let $d_x$ be its degree, and define $$ \psi(d,t)=t^{-1}\bigl[\log d!-\log \Gamma(d-t+1)\bigr], $$ where $\Gamma$ is the usual gamma function. Let $\Phi_\ell(G)$ denote the number of matchings of size $\ell$ in $G$. **Ilinca–Kahn conjectured matching bound.** If, for each $x\in X$, $t_x=\ell d_x/M$, then $$ \log\Phi_\ell(G)\leq \sum_{x\in X}\psi(d_x,t_x). $$ This proposed inequality is a precise version of an earlier entropy bound discussed in the paper. The source proposes it as a conjecture and does not provide a proof or resolution.
- **Lean endpoint:** `ilinca_kahn_conj31_not`, `ilinca_kahn_conj31_false`, `ik_family_violation_base_two`, `ik_family_count`
- **Replay record:** `ilinca-kahn-323535-root-final-replay-fable-20261119-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2)`; acceptance record `ilinca-kahn-matching-bound-323535-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/323535-ilinca-kahn-matching-bound`
- **Kernel-checked versus paper-only:** Lean checks the actual injection count, the all-m≥3 strict violation and the universal negation. The paper additionally compares the m=1 and m=2 family members and discusses log zero under extended-real conventions.
- **Prior art searched:** `public-tracker-prior-art-sweep-sol62-20261119-r1`; sweep verdict PARTIAL_OVERLAP, top-up verdict PARTIAL_OVERLAP; 52 queries and routes recorded
- **Closest prior work read:** Davies–Jenssen–Perkins, A proof of the Upper Matching Conjecture for large graphs (full text) https://arxiv.org/html/2004.06695
- **Adversarial verdicts:** NOT_COUNTED (adversarial-triage-Esol-sol62-20261119-r1)
- **Status note:** E-sol: nine-vertex counterexample found by brute force in under a second; no smaller one; Ilinca-Kahn report no test range
- **Caveats the row carries:**
  - The accepted witness is disconnected and includes a forced edge. Both parts have positive degrees and the matching count is positive, so this fits the source convention. The forced edge changes the global aspect ratio and is essential to this violation.
  - The accepted infinite family remains a valid stronger statement. The source's preferred constant-left-degree integral-t case is unaffected.
  - The two local public archive snapshots and the Principia id list contain no substantive match for the target, nearby IDs 323530–323539, or arXiv:1205.4342. The 2013 journal body remains unread.

### 326555 · Nonexistence of a CMDRR$(5,3)$

### 327031 · The domination-number conjecture for total graphs of finite rings

- **Outcome:** REFUTED
- **Source:** David Dolžan and Polona Oblak, “The total graphs of finite rings”, arXiv:1401.1941 (2014).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjecture of Dolzan and Oblak that the total graph of a ring R with R/J a product of matrix rings M_{n_i}(F_i), n_i >= 2, has domination number min_i (n_i(|F_i| - 1) + 1) is false: for R = M_2(F_3) the formula predicts 5, but the four matrices 0 and E12 + E21 + c E22 (c in F_3) dominate the total graph, so the domination number is at most 4. Lean defines zero-divisors, the total graph, domination and the minimum as the source does, states the conjecture for arbitrary rings with an actual ring isomorphism from the quotient by Mathlib's Jacobson radical, proves that the radical of M_2(F_3) is zero, and derives the negation.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $R$ be an arbitrary ring with Jacobson radical $J$, and suppose that $$ R/J=M_{n_1}(F_1) \times \ldots \times M_{n_k}(F_k), $$ where each $F_i$ is a finite field and $n_i\geq 2$ for all $i$. For a finite ring $A$, let $\tau(A)$ denote its total graph and let $\gamma(\tau(A))$ denote the domination number of that graph. **Domination-number conjecture.** $$ \gamma(\tau(R))=\min_i\{n_i(|F_i|-1)+1\}. $$ The preceding theorem establishes the corresponding upper bound without the restrictions $n_i\geq 2$. The examples of $M_2(\mathbb{Z}_2)$ and $M_3(\mathbb{Z}_2)$ attain this bound, while factors with $n_i=1$ can give a strictly smaller domination number; the equality in the stated generality is therefore proposed but not established here.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Total327031.mathdb327031`, `Total327031.literal_counterexample`
- **Replay record:** `total-graph-m2f3-327031-root-replay-fable-20261120-r1 (public-claim-100/replay-327031.log: all_ok, 5 modules)`; acceptance record `not in ledger (round 2; pipeline complete; see packet)`; submission folder `total-graph-m2f3-327031-formal-paper-sol61-20261120-r1 (paper: round2-final-check-327031-sol61-20261120-r1/PAPER-327031-revised.md)`

A strict reader may side with the dissent.

### 328452 · The maximal-size conjecture for the main cohort

- **Outcome:** REFUTED
- **Source:** Michael Albert and Mathilde Bouvel, “A general theory of Wilf-equivalence for Catalan structures”, arXiv:1407.8261 (2014).
- **What is proved (exact):** Conjecture 15 of the arXiv version of Albert and Bouvel (for every n >= 3 the main cohort is larger than any other cohort of arch systems of size n) is false: at n = 78 the main cohort has exactly 33882709435158403490429948661355518 members (a Motzkin number), while another cohort, that of 74 nested arches around one arch holding three leaves, contains an explicit family of 34231997388422588678130382724372607 arch systems. Lean uses exactly the source's four rules, proves the main cohort's size by a counting recurrence, proves the witness lies outside the main cohort, and proves the lower bound by a bijection.
- **MathDB record statement:** For each positive integer $n$, let ${{\mathcal M}}_n$ denote the main cohort of arch systems with $n$ arches, and let a cohort be an equivalence class under $\sim$. **Main-cohort maximality conjecture.** For every positive integer $n\geq 3$, the size of ${{\mathcal M}}_n$ is greater than the size of any other cohort of an arch system of size $n$. The main cohort is already known to dominate the avoidance generating functions of cohorts in the relevant termwise sense, but its maximality in number of elements is left as an open problem.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Arch.mathdb_328452_false`, `Arch.witness_check`
- **Replay record:** `arch-cohorts-328452-root-replay-fable-20261120-r1 (public-claim-100/replay-328452.log: all_ok, 27 modules)`; acceptance record `not in ledger (round 2; pipeline complete; see packet)`; submission folder `arch-cohorts-328452-formal-paper-sol61-20261120-r1 (paper: round2-final-check-328452-sol61-20261120-r1/PAPER-328452-revised.md)`
