- **Closest prior work read:** Albert and Bouvel, journal version of the source (2015), sections 7.1 and 8.1: the structural description of cohorts, the stated doubt, and the open question without an example (full text) (see round2-confirm-G17-opus-20261120-r1/CONFIRM.md for the links)
- **Adversarial verdicts:** round2-review-328452-sol61-20261120-r1/REVIEW-328452.md (GPT-6.1 Sol, non-author lane: SEARCH_EASY with the flag JOURNAL_DOUBT; refutation confirmed independently); round2-confirm-G17-opus-20261120-r1/CONFIRM.md (Claude Opus 5.5, before Lean: provisional tier 2; texts quoted; both numbers reproduced; no prior example found); round2-final-check-328452-sol61-20261120-r1/FINAL-REVIEW-328452.md (GPT-6.1 Sol: PASS WITH EDITORIAL EDITS; COUNTS; no definition issue); round2-confirm-G19-opus-20261120-r1/CONFIRM.md (Claude Opus 5.5, after Lean: CONFIRMED_TIER2 final, confidence 0.82; the Lean relation has exactly the source's four rules; transcribed definitions reproduce the source's cohort counts for n <= 10)

- **Caveats the row carries:**
  - The authors already doubted the conjecture: the journal version keeps it only as 'tempting to formulate', says 'We are now inclined to believe that cohorts that are larger than the main cohort may exist even if this does not happen before a very large value of n', describes a failed attempt to build one, and asks to prove or disprove it. They gave no example. Describe the result as refuting the arXiv conjecture and answering the question the journal left open.
  - 78 is not the first crossing: Opus found a different cohort that already beats the main cohort at 77 arches (not in Lean). The 78-arch witness is built around a 4-arch configuration the source draws as an example.
  - The mechanism is a short growth argument (marking a leaf multiplies the count by about n while an atom costs a constant factor), so a crossing had to occur; a strict reader might call the example a routine computation. The search for the crossing takes seconds.

### 332371 · Common-basis conjecture for partitions of a matroid ground set

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 52)

- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** LITERAL_STRONGER
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $k\geq 2$, and let $M$ be a matroid of rank $r$ on a ground set $E$ with $|E|=kr+1$. Suppose $x,y\in E$ are such that both $E\setminus\{x\}$ and $E\setminus\{y\}$ can be partitioned into $k$ pairwise disjoint bases. **Common-basis conjecture.** There exist partitions of $E\setminus\{x\}$ and $E\setminus\{y\}$ into $k$ pairwise disjoint bases that share a common basis. This is the second conjecture introduced as closely related to White's conjectures. It expresses a common-basis condition for two complementary decompositions and is not resolved in the supplied text.

### 332420 · Characterization of fixed ordering configurations by formal expansion fixedness

- **Outcome:** REFUTED
- **Source:** Emeric Gioan, Kevin Sol and Gérard Subsol, “Orientations of Simplices Determined by Orderings on the Coordinates of their Vertices”, arXiv:1602.01641 (2016).
- **What is proved (exact):** The expansion characterization conjecture of Gioan, Sol and Subsol (a linear ordering configuration is fixed if and only if it is formally fixed by expansion) is false: an explicit five-point configuration has positive determinant on every compatible realization, while every permitted cofactor expansion contains a minor that takes both signs.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $\mathcal{E}$ be a set of point labels and $\mathcal{B}$ a set of coordinate indices. A linear ordering configuration $\mathcal{C}$ on $(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{B})$ is **fixed** when the sign or non-nullity of $det(M_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{B}})$ is determined by the prescribed coordinate orderings. It is **formally fixed by expansion** when some cofactor expansion of $det(M_{\mathcal{E},\mathcal{B}})$ with respect to a pair $(e_i,e_j)$ has formal sign $\fbox{+}$ or $\fbox{-}$, using the signs of the induced lower-dimensional determinants. **Expansion characterization conjecture.** The configuration $\mathcal{C}$ is fixed if and only if $\mathcal{C}$ is formally fixed by expansion. The implication from formal expansion fixedness to fixedness is established, but the converse is presented as a conjecture in the supplied text.
- **Lean endpoint:** `OrderingConfiguration.mathdb_332420`, `OrderingConfiguration.five_point_counterexample`
- **Replay record:** `ordering-configuration-332420-root-final-replay-fable-20261119-r1`; acceptance record `not in ledger (pipeline complete; see packet)`; submission folder `ordering-configuration-332420-formal-paper-sol62-20261119-r1 (paper: final-paper-check-tier2-batch-opus-20261119-r1/PAPER-332420-revised.md)`

### 334899 · The generalized parallel-loop sorting conjecture

- **Outcome:** REFUTED
- **Source:** Sam Hopkins, Thomas McConville and James Propp, “Sorting via chip-firing”, arXiv:1612.06816 (2016).
- **What is proved (exact):** The generalized parallel-loop sorting conjecture of Hopkins, McConville and Propp is false: with 16 chips and loops at {-5, 1, 2, 3, 4} both unlabeled endpoint hypotheses hold, yet a legal firing sequence ends with chip 3 to the right of chip 4.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $S\subseteq\mathbb{Z}$, and let $r(\mathbb{Z}\langle S\rangle)$ be obtained from $\mathbb{Z}\langle S\rangle$ by replacing every edge, including every loop, by $r$ parallel copies. Suppose $n\in\mathbb{N}$ satisfies $$ \mathrm{min}(\widetilde{n\delta_0}^{\mathbb{Z}\langle S\rangle})<\mathrm{min}(\widetilde{(n-1)\delta_0}^{\mathbb{Z}\langle S\rangle}) $$ and $$ \mathrm{max}(\widetilde{n\delta_0}^{\mathbb{Z}\langle S\rangle})>\mathrm{max}(\widetilde{(n-1)\delta_0}^{\mathbb{Z}\langle S\rangle}). $$ **Generalized parallel-loop sorting conjecture.** Then $r(\mathbb{Z}\langle S\rangle)$ sorts $\Delta^{rn}$ for each $r\geq1$. This simultaneously generalizes the looped-path and parallel-edge conjectures, but the source supplies no proof of the general assertion.

### 336131 · The uniform central graph appendage conjecture for central graphs of diameter greater than one

- **Outcome:** REFUTED
- **Source:** Sul-Young Choi and Jonathan Needleman, “On Central-Peripheral Appendage Numbers of Uniform Central Graphs”, arXiv:1705.07982 (2017).
- **What is proved (exact):** Conjecture 2 of Choi and Needleman (if diam(C) > 1, diam(P) = 4 and r(P) = 3 then the uniform central appendage number A_ucg(C,P) equals 6) is false: for C the path on three vertices and an explicit connected 12-vertex graph P with diameter 4 and radius 3 there is a 20-vertex uniform central graph whose centre induces C, whose common eccentric set induces P, and which has five further vertices, so A_ucg(C,P) <= 5.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $C$ and $P$ be graphs, let $\operatorname{diam}(C)$ and $\operatorname{diam}(P)$ denote their diameters, let $r(P)$ denote the radius of $P$, and let $A_{ucg}(C,P)$ be the corresponding appendage number. **The uniform central graph appendage conjecture.** If $$ \operatorname{diam}(C)>1, $$ and $$ \operatorname{diam}(P)=4,\qquad r(P)=3, $$ then $$ A_{ucg}(C,P)=6. $$ The preceding results show that in this case the appendage number is either $5$ or $6$; value $5$ would occur exactly when a certain covering number equals $2$, but no such example was found and impossibility was not proved.
- **Lean endpoint:** `UCG.mathdb336131_negation`, `UCG.mathdb336131_counterexample`

- **Prior art searched:** `round2-review-336131-sol61-20261119-r1 and round2-final-check-336131-sol61-20261119-r1 and round2-confirm-G1-opus-20261119-r1`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (journal version read; OpenAlex and Crossref list no citing works; later papers of both authors and Klavzar-Narayankar-Lokesh checked; Principia id list and four local clones; GitHub by id and arXiv id); 12 queries and routes recorded; routes refused: ProbXiv HTTP 402; arXiv keyword API HTTP 429; Semantic Scholar HTTP 429
- **Closest prior work read:** Choi and Needleman, Electron. J. Graph Theory Appl. 8(1) (2020) 157-180, Theorem 4.1 and the reduction on p. 178 (A_ucg is 5 or 6, and 5 exactly when a covering number equals 2) (journal PDF read: definitions, Section 4, Section 5 examples, Section 6 conjecture) https://www.ejgta.org/index.php/ejgta/article/download/776/pdf_132
- **Adversarial verdicts:** round2-review-336131-sol61-20261119-r1/REVIEW-336131.md (GPT-6.1 Sol, non-author lane: mathematics, fidelity and prior art pass; eligibility left to root because the source states no tested range); round2-final-check-336131-sol61-20261119-r1/FINAL-REVIEW-336131.md (GPT-6.1 Sol, non-author lane: PASS WITH EDITORIAL EDITS; COUNTS as easy search; no definition issue affecting the endpoint); root (Claude Fable 5.2): recomputed radius 3, diameter 4, centre, common eccentric set and the parameters of P by breadth-first search; ruled tier 2; round2-confirm-G1-opus-20261119-r1/CONFIRM.md (Claude Opus 5.5: CONFIRMED_TIER2, confidence 0.75; agrees with root's ruling; witness re-checked; not a small change to the source's heptagonal prism)

  - The source's own Theorem 4.1 already shows the value is 5 exactly when a covering number of P equals 2; the content here is the example, found by a SAT search. The journal (p. 178) says only that the authors had not found such a P.

### 342414 · The edge-pattern convex-combination conjecture

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 21)
- **Source:** François Pirot and Jean-Sébastien Sereni, “Fractional chromatic number, maximum degree and girth”, arXiv:1904.05618 (2021).
- **What is proved (exact):** There is an eight-vertex cubic edge-rooted pattern in the source's sense, of exact depth two and girth six, whose constraint vector is not weaker than any finite convex combination of constraint vectors of acyclic cubic edge-rooted patterns of exact depth two. The positive rational vector (1/1000, 7/27, 1/3) gives a value below one on the pattern and above one on every tree constraint.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** LITERAL
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $P$ be a $d$-regular edge-rooted pattern of depth $r$ and girth $2r+2$. For each edge-rooted pattern, let $\mathbf{e}(P)$ denote its constraint vector, and let $\mathcal{T}'_r(d)$ be the specified family of $d$-regular edge-rooted trees of depth $r$. The constraint $\mathbf{e}(P)$ is **weaker** than a convex combination of constraints from this family in the sense that there exist $T_1,\dots,T_m\in\mathcal{T}'_r(d)$ and $\lambda_1,\dots,\lambda_m\in[0,1]$ with $$ \sum_{i=1}^m\lambda_i=1 $$ and, for every $\bm{\alpha}\in{\left(\mathbb{Q}^+\right)}^{r+1}$, $$ \bm{\alpha}^{\!\top}\left(\sum_{i=1}^m\lambda_i\mathbf{e}(T_i)\right)\ge 1\quad\implies\quad\bm{\alpha}^{\!\top}\mathbf{e}(P)\ge 1. $$ This would explain why the bounds obtained from edge-rooted trees remain valid at girth $2r+2$, and would generalize the cases checked for $r=2$ and $d\in\{3,4\}$.
- **Lean endpoint:** `EdgePattern.TreeModelsR5.refutation : ¬ EdgePattern.Conjecture2.{0}`
- **Replay record:** `edgepattern-parent-final-r1/replay-result.json`; acceptance record `ledger.json#accepted`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/342414-a-cubic-counterexample-to-edge-pattern-convex-domination`
- **Kernel-checked versus paper-only:** Lean checks the pattern, exact-depth family classification, maximum independent-set expectations and separation for real convex weights. The written argument also covers the depth-at-most reading via the one shallower form.
- **Prior art searched:** `uniform-prior-art-sweep-B2-sol62-20261119-r1`; sweep verdict PARTIAL_OVERLAP; 48 queries and routes recorded; routes refused: arXiv export API disallowed by https://export.arxiv.org/robots.txt; Google Scholar /scholar disallowed by https://scholar.google.com/robots.txt; MathDB not checked (recorded automated-access refusal); OpenAlex source: not run after OpenAlex 429; OpenAlex cita…
- **Closest prior work read:** Pirot–Sereni, Fractional chromatic number, maximum degree and girth, §4.2.2 and Lemmas 9–10 (full text) https://arxiv.org/abs/1904.05618v6
- **Adversarial verdicts:** NOT_COUNTED (adversarial-triage-E2-sol62-20261119-r1)
- **Status note:** E2 Sol: eight-vertex failure at d=3, r=2 found by LP search in 1.1 s; source evaluated this parameter pair only for its chosen weight [root: filed tier 2]
- **Caveats the row carries:**
  - The source checked their optimized bound at a particular weight, not the stronger implication for all weights. NOT_COUNTED applies the brief’s tested-range exclusion to the parameter pair. At the authors’ weight, the verified eight-vertex separating pattern satisfies the inequality.

- **Outcome:** REFUTED
- **Source:** Wiam Belkouche, Abderrahim Boussaïri, Soufiane Lakhlifi and Mohamed Zaidi, “Matricial characterization of tournaments with maximum number of diamonds”, arXiv:1906.04672 (2019).
- **What is proved (exact):** The conjectured maximum number of diamonds in a tournament of order n = 1 mod 4, (n+3)(n-1)(n-2)(n-3)/96, is exceeded at n = 17: an explicit tournament has 702 diamonds against the bound 700 (Lean certifies at least 702).

The supplied text does not state a resolution, so its status is open.

- **Source:** C. M. Mynhardt and A. Roux, “Irredundance Trees of Diameter 3”, arXiv:2104.09004 (2021).
- **What is proved (exact):** The path P₉ is isomorphic to the irredundance graph of an explicit finite simple graph. Its irredundance graph has as vertices the maximum-cardinality irredundant sets and joins two sets when one arises from the other by swapping one vertex along an edge.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** PART_ONLY
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $P_n$ denote the path on $n$ vertices and $C_n$ the cycle on $n$ vertices. An **IR-graph** is a graph arising as an irredundance graph. **Non-IR conjecture for paths and cycles.** The path $P_n$ is not an IR-graph for each $n\geq 3$, and the cycle $C_n$ is not an IR-graph for each $n\geq 5$. These are open problems concerning which graphs can occur as irredundance graphs. They are stated together in the paper's list of conjectures and open problems and are attributed there to Mynhardt (2019).

- **MathDB record statement:** Let $G$ be a doubled 3-regular graph. Perform the YY-$\Delta$ transformation on all wyes in all possible ways to obtain a graph $G'$ of smallest size. A **chain of 3-cycles** is a chain of one or more 3-cycles, each connected by a vertex that is also doubly adjacent to one more vertex, with the 3-cycles at either end having one vertex with exactly 4 neighbours and no connections of size greater than 2. **Minimality criterion conjecture.** The graph $G'$ is a minimal graph if and only if it does not contain such a chain of 3-cycles. The conjecture proposes that, for doubled 3-regular graphs, checking the more restricted subgraphs illustrated in the first column of the paper's figure suffices to determine minimality. The source gives examples and discusses related reducible subgraphs, but provides no proof or resolution.

### 352795 · The sparsity conjecture for minimally AP-irreducible sign pattern matrices

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 71)
- **Source:** Sunil Das, “Sign patterns that allow algebraic positivity”, arXiv:2112.00442 (2022).
- **What is proved (exact):** There is a 5×5 zero-diagonal minimally AP-irreducible sign pattern with exactly nine nonzero entries, exceeding 2·5−2. In the source definition, the support and A₊−A₋ᵀ are irreducible, every row and column contains a plus, and each single nonzero deletion breaks AP-irreducibility.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** LITERAL
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $A$ be a minimally AP-irreducible sign pattern matrix of order $n$ whose diagonal entries are all zero. A sign pattern matrix $A$ is **minimally AP-irreducible** if it is AP-irreducible and deleting any nonzero entry destroys AP-irreducibility. **Sparsity conjecture.** The matrix $A$ has at most $2n-2$ nonzero entries. The paper establishes this bound under the additional hypothesis that $A[\alpha,\beta]$ contains no $+$ for any two distinct irreducible components $\alpha$ and $\beta$ of $A_+$. The conjecture asks whether the same bound holds without that additional hypothesis; its status is not resolved in the supplied text.
- **Lean endpoint:** `APRefutation.witness`, `APRefutation.refutation`, `APRefutation.expanded_refutation`, `Fresh independent expanded unconditional audit`
- **Replay record:** `ap-minimal-sparsity-independent-strict-replay-sol-r1/REPLAY-AND-FIDELITY-v1.md`; acceptance record `ap-minimal-sparsity-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/352795-the-sparsity-conjecture-for-minimally-ap-irreducible-sign-pa`
- **Kernel-checked versus paper-only:** Lean checks the concrete entry pattern, three AP criteria and every individual nonzero deletion. The paper gives the same witness using directed connectivity and deletions.
- **Prior art searched:** `uniform-prior-art-sweep-B6-sol62-20261119-r1`; sweep verdict PARTIAL_OVERLAP; 35 queries and routes recorded; routes refused: arXiv API: export.arxiv.org/robots.txt disallows all paths (main arXiv also disallows /api); no API query issued.; Google Scholar: robots.txt disallows /scholar; no search request issued. ### 352827 · Unit-gap conjecture for diameters of Schrijver graphs

- **What is proved (exact):** In the Schrijver graphs on cyclically stable 12-subsets with disjointness adjacency, SG(27,12) has diameter at least eight and SG(28,12) has diameter at most six. Thus the conjectured diameter difference in {0,1} for 2≤r≤k−2 fails at k=12, r=3.

It is based on computations and is open; the paper notes that the analogous differences are in $\\{0,1\\}$ for $k\leq 5$, while a larger gap occurs at the transition from $r=1$ to $r=2$ when $k\geq 6$.

### 352946 · The exact $(k+1)$-metric dimension of three-dimensional grids

- **Outcome:** REFUTED
- **Source:** Mercè Mora, María José Souto Salorio and Ana Dorotea Tarrío-Tobar, “Resolving sets tolerant to failures in three-dimensional grids”, arXiv:2112.08768 (2021).
- **What is proved (exact):** Conjecture 21 of Mora, Souto-Salorio and Tarrio-Tobar (the exact (k+1)-metric dimension of three-dimensional grids in the range alpha_m <= k < alpha_M) is false: for the grid P3 x P3 x P4 and k = 13 the hypotheses hold (12 <= 13 < 15), an explicit set of 30 vertices is 14-resolving, so dim_14 <= 30, while the conjectured value is min(32, 34) = 32. The Lean development proves that the graph distance of the grid is the coordinate-sum distance and defines the metric dimension as a minimum.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $k,n_1,n_2,n_3\ge 2$, and let $P_{n_1}\Box P_{n_2}\Box P_{n_3}$ be the three-dimensional grid graph. Write $\alpha_m(n_1,n_2,n_3)$ and $\alpha_M(n_1,n_2,n_3)$ for the quantities defined in the paper, and let $\dim_{k+1}$ denote the $(k+1)$-metric dimension. **The conjecture.** If $$ \alpha_m(n_1,n_2,n_3)\le k<\alpha_M(n_1,n_2,n_3), $$ then $$ \dim_{k+1}(P_{n_1}\Box P_{n_2}\Box P_{n_3})=\min\left\{4k-2\alpha_m(n_1,n_2,n_3)+4,\ n_1n_2n_3-(n_1-2)(n_2-2)(n_3-2)\right\}. $$ The preceding results determine the exact value when $k<\alpha_m(n_1,n_2,n_3)$ and show that the displayed upper bound holds throughout the remaining range where a $(k+1)$-resolving set exists. The conjecture proposes the exact value in the intermediate range $\alpha_m(n_1,n_2,n_3)\le k<\alpha_M(n_1,n_2,n_3)$; when $\alpha_m=\alpha_M$, which occurs if $\min\{n_1,n_2,n_3\}=2$, the metric dimension is already completely…

### 355016 · Categorical genericity conjecture for the combinatorial derived matroid

- **Outcome:** REFUTED
- **Source:** Olga Kuznetsova, Ragnar Freij-Hollanti and Relinde Jurrius, “Combinatorial Derived Matroids”, arXiv:2206.06881 (2022).
- **What is proved (exact):** Conjecture 43 of Kuznetsova, Freij-Hollanti and Jurrius (Conjecture 8.4 in arXiv v3: for a representable matroid M and every representation R, the combinatorial derived matroid delta M is above the Oxley-Wang derived matroid delta_OW(R) in the weak order, that is, every dependent set of delta M is dependent in delta_OW(R)) is false: for an explicit rank-4 matrix over F_7 with 8 columns, four circuits form a dependent set of delta M under the source's closure operations while their circuit vectors are linearly independent, for every choice of circuit vectors.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $M$ be a representable matroid with set of circuits $\mathcal{C}$. For matroids on a fixed ground set, use the **weak order** defined by $M_1\geq M_2$ when every dependent set of $M_1$ is dependent in $M_2$. **Categorical genericity conjecture.** Then $$ \delta M\geq\delta_{OW}(R) $$ for every representation $R$ of $M$, where the order is the weak order on matroids with ground set $\mathcal{C}$. This claims that the combinatorial derived matroid is the generic, maximal derived matroid in the categorical weak-order sense.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Derived355016.mathdb_355016_not_universal`, `Derived355016.mathdb_355016_counterexample`, `Derived355016.represented_matroid_counterexample`, `Derived355016.refutation_every_system`

### 355115 · The diagonal-monomial reachability conjecture for psd-stable polynomials

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (strict-decrease form)
- **Source:** Giulia Codenotti, Stephan Gardoll and Thorsten Theobald, “Combinatorics and preservation of conically stable polynomials”, arXiv:2206.10913 (2022).
- **What is proved (exact):** Conjecture 5.13 of Codenotti, Gardoll and Theobald (from every monomial in the support of a psd-stable polynomial a diagonal monomial of the support can be reached by linear, double and transposition steps that decrease the distance and stay in the support) is false when 'decrease' is strict, which is the source's usage: an explicit psd-stable cubic in the entries of a 3 x 3 symmetric matrix (a product of three forms u^T Z u) has a support monomial from which no diagonal monomial is reachable. Lean proves psd-stability over every complex symmetric matrix with positive definite imaginary part. Under non-strict decrease every monomial of this witness reaches a diagonal one, so that form is not refuted.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $f$ be a psd-stable polynomial in symmetric variables, and let $Z^\beta$ be a monomial appearing in a psd-stable polynomial. A **diagonal monomial** is a monomial $Z^\alpha$ appearing in $f$ whose variables are diagonal variables. The distance between monomials is measured by the norm used for exponent vectors, and linear, double, and transposition steps are the support-preserving monomial moves defined in the paper. **Diagonal-monomial reachability conjecture.** For any monomial $Z^\beta$ appearing in a psd-stable polynomial, there is a diagonal monomial $Z^\alpha$ appearing in $f$ which can be reached by a sequence of linear, double and transposition steps which decrease the distance from $\beta$ to $\alpha$ and which never leave the support of $f$. This conjecture proposes that the determinant's connectedness under support-preserving combinatorial moves extends to all psd-stable…

### 355140 · Improved upper bounds for Staller–Maker–Breaker domination numbers

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 4)
- **Source:** Csilla Bujtás and Pakanun Dokyeesun, “Fast winning strategies for Staller in the Maker-Breaker domination game”, arXiv:2206.12812 (2022).
- **What is proved (exact):** A seven-vertex theta graph with branch lengths 2, 2 and 4 has minimum degree two and finite Staller-start winning number four, exceeding the proposed bound of three. Thus the conjunction of the Staller-start and Dominator-start bounds for finite winning numbers is false.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** PART_ONLY
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $G$ be a graph on $n$ vertices, with minimum degree $\delta(G)$. ### 355218 · Line-middle graph inequality conjecture for outer-connected domination

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 94)
- **Source:** Farshad Kazemnejad, Behnaz Pahlavsay, Elisa Palezzato and Michele Torielli, “Connected and outer-connected domination number of middle graphs”, arXiv:2206.15439 (2022).
- **What is proved (exact):** (If overruled) Conjecture 7.6 of Kazemnejad-Pahlavsay-Palezzato-Torielli, gamma~c(L(G)) <= gamma~c(M(G)) for all graphs of order >= 2, is false: two copies of K_{2h} joined by a three-edge path (h>=2, order 4h+2) have gamma~c(M)=2h+2 < gamma~c(L)=C(2h,2)+h+1, under both empty-complement conventions; no counterexample exists below order 10.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $G$ be a graph of order $n\ge 2$, and let $L(G)$ and $M(G)$ denote its line graph and middle graph, respectively. The **line-middle graph inequality conjecture** asserts that $$ \gamma{\tilde{}} _{c}(L(G)) \le \gamma{\tilde{}} _{c}(M(G)). $$ The inequality is supported by the preceding results, including strict inequality for wheels of order at least five; equality occurs for at least one connected graph of order four. A classification of graphs for which equality holds is posed separately as an open problem, while the general inequality remains open.

### 356266 · Hurlbert's ordering conjecture for universal cycles of permutations

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 49)
- **Source:** Rachel Kirsch, Clare Sibley and Elizabeth Sprangel, “Graph Universal Cycles: Compression and Connections to Universal Cycles”, arXiv:2209.14198 (2022).
- **What is proved (exact):** There is an Euler tour of the overlap graph on all permutations of four letters, using each permutation exactly once with matching reduced suffix and prefix at every cyclic transition, that no cyclic word realizes by its ordered windows. The impossibility holds over any linearly ordered alphabet.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** LITERAL
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $S_n$ be the set of permutations of $[n]$, and let $\mathcal{O}(n)$ be the clustered graph of overlapping $n$-permutations: its edges are the permutations in $S_n$, and consecutive permutations overlap in their last and first $n-1$ entries up to reduction. **Hurlbert's ordering conjecture.** Given any Euler tour of the clustered graph of overlapping permutations (that is, an ordering of $S_n$ such that each consecutive pair of permutations overlaps), there is a universal cycle for permutations that covers $S_n$ in the same order. Universal cycles correspond to Euler tours in this graph, but it remains open whether every such ordering can be realized by a universal cycle.
- **Lean endpoint:** `MathDB356266.conjecture_false : ¬ MathDB356266.Conjecture, where Conjecture := for all n N and every t : Fin (N+1) -> Fin (n+1) -> Fin (n+1) with EulerOrdering t (every row a permutation, rows distin…`

- **Kernel-checked versus paper-only:** Lean checks the full overlap tour and its strict comparison cycle. The paper also translates the obstruction via the source correspondence to graph universal cycles and discusses classical related mechanisms.
- **Prior art searched:** `uniform-prior-art-sweep-B4-sol62-20261119-r1`; sweep verdict PARTIAL_OVERLAP; 37 queries and routes recorded; routes refused: arXiv keyword API: https://export.arxiv.org/robots.txt disallows all paths; Google Scholar: HTTP 403 on the first lane key-term query (local antimagic chromatic number); stopped automated requests to that host; MathDB: not checked under the recorded automated…

### 358412 · Well-balanced orientation conjecture for maximum total arc-connectivity

- **Outcome:** REFUTED
- **Source:** Florian Hörsch, “On orientations maximizing total arc-connectivity”, arXiv:2305.08688 (2023).
- **What is proved (exact):** Conjecture 2 of Horsch (every graph has an orientation that maximizes total arc-connectivity and is well-balanced in the sense of Nash-Williams) is false: for an explicit simple graph on 8 vertices with 14 edges, one orientation has total arc-connectivity at least 75, while every well-balanced orientation has at most 74, so no maximizing orientation is well-balanced. Lean also proves that well-balanced orientations of the witness exist.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $G$ be a graph, let $\vec{G}$ be an orientation of $G$, and let $tac(\vec{G})$ denote its total arc-connectivity. An orientation **maximizes total arc-connectivity** when it maximizes $tac(\vec{G})$ among all orientations of $G$. **Well-balanced orientation conjecture.** Every graph $G$ has an orientation $\vec{G}$ that maximizes $tac(\vec{G})$ and is well-balanced. The source presents this as a stronger statement than the conjecture about 2-edge-connected graphs and does not give a resolution.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Horsch.mathdb358412`, `Horsch.source_counterexample`, `Horsch.witness_bounds`, `Horsch.no_wellBalanced_maximizer`, `Horsch.wellBalanced_exists`

### 359279 · Linear-bound multiset reconstruction conjecture for tableau minors

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 22)
- **Source:** William Q. Erickson, Daniel Herden, Jonathan Meddaugh, Mark R. Sepanski, Cordell Hammon, Jasmin Mohn and Indalecio Ruiz-Bolanos, “Young tableau reconstruction via minors”, arXiv:2307.13161 (2023).
- **What is proved (exact):** There are two distinct standard Young tableaux with 12 cells whose multisets of minors obtained by eight successive jeu de taquin deletions, counted over all deletion sequences, are equal. This proposed linear bound is supported by computer verification for $k\leq 5$, and concerns reconstructibility from multisets of $k$-minors.

### 359413 · The conjecture on Diamond and Trapezoid 2×3×3 Abalone constellations

### 360964 · Conjecture that interval and integer Schur numbers coincide

- **Outcome:** REFUTED
- **Source:** Lucas Aragão, Jonathan Chapman, Miquel Ortega and Victor Souza, “On the number of monochromatic solutions to multiplicative equations”, arXiv:2311.18742 (2024).
- **What is proved (exact):** Conjecture 6.4 of Aragao, Chapman, Ortega and Souza (the interval Schur number I(r) equals the Schur number S(r) for all r) is false at r = 4: every 4-colouring of {2,...,89} has a monochromatic solution of x + y = z, which by scaling gives I(4) <= 89/2, while the classical colouring of {1,...,44} gives S(4) >= 45. The exact value of I(4) is not determined.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $S(r)$ be the least positive integer $N$ such that every $r$-colouring of $[N]$ contains a monochromatic solution to $x+y=z$, and let $I(r)$ be the supremum of the real numbers $T>1$ for which the half-open interval $[1,T)$ admits a sum-free $r$-colouring. **Interval Schur number conjecture.** For all $r$, we have $$ I(r)=S(r). $$ The equality would make the bounds for $I(r)$ match up to logarithmic factors. The supplied text presents this as a belief and gives no resolution.
- **Lean endpoint:** `IntervalSchur.mathdb_360964`, `IntervalSchur.intervalI_four_lt_schurS`, `IntervalSchur.intervalI_four_le`, `IntervalSchur.schurS_four_ge`, `IntervalSchur.schurS_four_minimum`, `IntervalSchur.finite_grid89`
- **Replay record:** `interval-schur-360964-root-replay-fable-20261120-r3 (public-claim-100/replay-360964.log: all_ok, 119 modules, every rebuilt olean matches the author's; the earlier -r1 run was stopped for slowness an…`; acceptance record `not in ledger (round 2; pipeline complete; see packet)`; submission folder `interval-schur-360964-formal-paper-sol61-20261119-r2 (paper: PAPER.md in that folder; the final check required no edits)`
- **Prior art searched:** `candidates-review-R1-opus-20261119-r1 and round2-final-check-360964-sol61-20261120-r1`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (source arXiv v2 and Combinatorica 45 (2025); Semantic Scholar citing list; later work on continuous Rado numbers is 2-colour only; Principia, mio-qwq, omega and openai/math clones; omega issue search by root); 12 queries and routes recorded; routes refused: OpenAlex HTTP 429 (round 1 review)
- **Closest prior work read:** Aragao, Chapman, Ortega, Souza, arXiv:2311.18742v2 (Combinatorica 45 (2025) 64), Section 6.3: 41 <= I(4) <= 45 and Conjecture 6.4 (full text) https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.18742
- **Adversarial verdicts:** candidates-review-R1-opus-20261119-r1/REVIEW-360964.md (Claude Opus 5.5: SEARCH_EASY; correct, literal, no prior art found; only I(4) < 45 is new, S(4) = 45 is classical; a generic SAT solver settles the finite grid in 17 s); round2-final-check-360964-sol61-20261120-r1/FINAL-REVIEW-360964.md (GPT-6.1 Sol: PASS; COUNTS; no definition issue; the proved bound 44.5 suffices)
- **Status note:** discovery by GPT-6.2 Sol (round 1, shard 023); adversarial review by Claude Opus 5.5 (round 1, SEARCH_EASY); Lean by two GPT-6.1 Sol lanes (round 2); final check by GPT-6.1 Sol; root (Claude Fable) replayed the Lean packet
- **Caveats the row carries:**
  - The source proved 41 <= I(4) <= 45 and said it had not settled whether I(4) = S(4); the first grid finer than the integers, at the first open number of colours, already decides it. Easy to find, laborious to certify: the kernel-checked certificate has 184,658 steps.
  - The Opus review computed the sharper bound I(4) <= 42 by SAT; Lean proves only I(4) <= 44.5, which is enough.
  - The endpoint quantifies over r >= 1, the source's reading; S(4) is proved to be a genuine minimum, so no default value does the work.

### 364095 · The set-palette conjecture for complete graphs

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 5)
- **Source:** Monika Pilsniak and Mariusz Wozniak, “A note on edge colorings distinguishing all triangles in a graph”, arXiv:2407.19050 (2024).
- **What is proved (exact):** Every edge coloring of K₅ using labels from a four-element set gives two different triangles with identical sets of edge colors. Thus it is false that for every n≥4 the least number of labels that distinguishes all triangles of Kₙ by their set palettes is n−1.

### 364205 · Largest rigid facet conjecture for Vietoris–Rips complexes of hypercubes

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 9)
- **Source:** Joseph Briggs, Ziqin Feng and Chris Wells, “Facets in the Vietoris–Rips complexes of hypercubes”, arXiv:2408.01288 (2024).
- **What is proved (exact):** In the signed eight-dimensional Hamming cube there is a 34-point subset of diameter exactly five, maximal under inclusion among sets of diameter at most five, with a distance-five partner for each point. Thus the greatest size of a rigid maximal diameter-r set need not be 2ʳ when r≤n.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** LITERAL
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $Q_n=\\{\pm1\\}^n$ be the Hamming cube with Hamming distance. A subset is **maximally diameter-$r$** if its diameter is $r$ and adjoining any point outside it produces diameter greater than $r$; it is **rigid** if every point has a point at distance equal to the diameter. Let $b'(n;r)$ be the size of the largest maximally diameter-$r$, rigid subset of $Q_n$, equivalently the largest such rigid facet in $\operatorname{\mathcal{VR}}(Q_n;r)$. **Largest rigid facet conjecture.** $$ b'(n;r)=2^r $$ whenever $n\geq r$. The subcube $Q_r\times\\{\mathbf 1_{n-r}\\}$ supplies an extremal candidate of size $2^r$, but the conjectured upper bound is not proved in the source.

### 365453 · The local antimagic chromatic number conjecture for joins of matching and null graphs

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 40)

At r=s=1,m=2 there is no bijective edge labelling by 1 through the edge count with distinct sums across adjacent vertices and at most three distinct vertex sums.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** LITERAL
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $r,s\ge 1$ and $m\ge 2$. Let $(2s+1)P_2$ be the disjoint union of $2s+1$ copies of the two-vertex path, let $O_m$ be the null graph on $m$ vertices, let $\vee$ denote the graph join, and let $2r((2s+1)P_2\vee O_m)$ be the disjoint union of $2r$ copies of this join. **Local antimagic chromatic number conjecture.** $$ \chi_{la}\bigl(2r((2s+1)P_2\vee O_m)\bigr)=3. $$ This is posed as an open problem in the conclusion, extending results on local antimagic chromatic numbers for joins of special graph families. The claim predicts a constant value of $3$ throughout the stated parameter range.

### 366258 · Treewidth-connectivity partition conjecture for graphs

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 84)
- **Source:** Stijn Cambie, François Dross, Kolja Knauer, Hoang La and Petru Valicov, “Partitions of planar (oriented) graphs into a connected acyclic and an independent set”, arXiv:2412.11774 (2025).
- **What is proved (exact):** There is a finite simple undirected graph on 16 vertices and 34 edges with treewidth at most three and vertex connectivity at least three, for which every independent vertex set has an induced complement that fails at least one of treewidth at most two and vertex connectivity at least two. This negates the conjecture quantified over finite simple graphs and integer k at k=3.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** LITERAL
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $G$ be a graph, and let $k$ be an integer such that $G$ has treewidth at most $k$ and connectivity at least $k$. A vertex set inducing a graph of treewidth at most $k-1$ and connectivity at least $k-1$ is denoted by $\mathcal{T}$, and an independent set is denoted by $\mathcal{I}$. **Treewidth-connectivity partition conjecture.** The vertices of $G$ can be partitioned into an induced graph $\mathcal{T}$ of treewidth at most $k-1$ and connectivity at least $k-1$, and an independent set $\mathcal{I}$. This is proposed as a generalization of a theorem for graphs of treewidth at most $2$; the source leaves it as a belief rather than an established result.

### 366371 · The majorization characterization conjecture for ordinal spaces embeddable in the line

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 8)
- **Source:** Karsten Keller and Evgeniy Petrov, “Ordinal spaces”, arXiv:2412.17391 (2024).
- **What is proved (exact):** There is a six-point ordinal space with no ties between distinct off-diagonal pairs whose displayed enumeration has the majorization property for nondecreasing chains of every positive length, including repeats and permutations of the successive pieces, but which does not embed into the real line preserving distance comparisons. Thus the majorization property of an enumeration does not imply line embeddability.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** LITERAL
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $(X,\delta)$ be an ordinal space with $|X|=n\in\mathbb N^+$, and let $x_1,x_2,\ldots,x_n$ be an enumeration of its points satisfying the majorization property. Write $X\hookrightarrow\mathbb R^1$ for embeddability of $X$ in the real line. **Majorization characterization conjecture.** If such an enumeration exists, then $$ X\hookrightarrow\mathbb R^1. $$ The paper proves that line embeddability implies the existence of an enumeration with the majorization property and states sufficiency for $n\leqslant4$, while explicitly saying that it cannot determine whether sufficiency holds for $n>5$; the general assertion is therefore open.

### 367612 · Positive order polynomials for width-two and shifted or cylindric skew-shape posets

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (width-two clause)

The classes under consideration are posets of width two, cell posets of cylindric skew shapes, and cell posets of shifted skew shapes; these classes are closed under taking lower and upper ideals. **Positivity conjecture.** The order polynomials of all three classes have nonnegative coefficients: posets of width two, cell posets of cylindric skew shapes, and cell posets of shifted skew shapes. The metatheorem described immediately before this conjecture reduces the problem for these stable classes to nonnegativity of the linear coefficient. The source presents the claims as conjectural.

### 368219 · Automorphism count from companion multiplicities

- **Outcome:** REFUTED
- **Source:** Stefan Mitrovic, “Linear function of a poset”, arXiv:2504.20975 (2025).
- **What is proved (exact):** Mitrovic's conjecture that the number of automorphisms of a poset equals its minimum companion multiplicity fails for the six-element poset V + Lambda (0<4, 0<5, 1<3, 2<3): it has 4 automorphisms and minimum companion multiplicity 8.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $P$ be a poset with $n$ elements, and let $\mathcal{A}_1,\ldots,\mathcal{A}_k$ be the mutually different collections of type $\mathrm{Comp}(P,\omega)$ for $\omega\in\Sigma_P$. Suppose that $\mathcal{A}_i$ appears for $l_i$ different $P$-listings, so that $l_1+\cdots+l_k=n!$. Let $A(P)$ denote the number of automorphisms of $P$. **Automorphism-count conjecture.** For any poset $P$, $$ A(P)=\mathrm{min}\{l_i\}. $$ The preceding discussion establishes the lower bound $A(P)\leq l_i$ for every $i$ and proposes that the smallest companion multiplicity exactly recovers the automorphism count; no resolution is supplied in the excerpt.
- **Lean endpoint:** `PosetCompanions.mathdb_368219`

### 369290 · Propagation-time interval conjecture for minimum zero forcing sets of hypercubes

- **Outcome:** REFUTED

- **MathDB record statement:** Let $Q_d$ be the $d$-dimensional hypercube graph. A **minimum zero forcing set** is a zero forcing set of cardinality equal to the zero forcing number of $Q_d$; its propagation time is the number of parallel forcing rounds needed to color all vertices. Let $\operatorname{pt}(Q_d)$ and $\operatorname{PT}(Q_d)$ denote the minimum and maximum propagation times, respectively, among minimum zero forcing sets of $Q_d$. A graph has a **full propagation time interval** if it has minimum zero forcing sets with every integer propagation time between $\operatorname{pt}(Q_d)$ and $\operatorname{PT}(Q_d)$. **Propagation-time interval conjecture.** For every $d\geq 2$, $$ \operatorname{pt}(Q_d)=1 \quad\text{and}\quad \operatorname{PT}(Q_d)=2^{d-2}. $$ Moreover, $Q_d$ has a full propagation time interval. The preceding results establish the existence of minimum zero forcing sets with propagation times…

### 369595 · The edge-redundant rigidity conjecture for complete graphs in $\ell_\infty^d$

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 96)
- **Source:** James Cruickshank, Sean Dewar and Derek Kitson, “Algebraic connectivity in normed spaces”, arXiv:2508.00134 (2025).
- **What is proved (exact):** No well-positioned framework of K₈ in ℓ∞³ is infinitesimally rigid and stays so after every single-edge deletion. This refutes the universal threshold n≥2d+2 at d=3,n=8, while an explicit well-positioned framework of K₉ in ℓ∞³ does have that property.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** LITERAL
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $d\geq 2$, let $n\geq 2d+2$, and let $K_n$ denote the complete graph on $n$ vertices. A graph is **edge-redundantly rigid** in a normed space if it has a framework that is infinitesimally rigid and remains infinitesimally rigid after deleting any edge. **Edge-redundant rigidity conjecture.** For every $d\geq 2$ and every $n\geq 2d+2$, the complete graph $K_n$ is edge-redundantly rigid in $\ell_\infty^d$. The preceding proposition rules out $K_{2d+1}$, so the conjecture asserts that the stated threshold is sufficient as well as necessary. The supplied excerpt gives no evidence that the conjecture has been resolved.
- **Lean endpoint:** `CDK.not_edgeRedundantlyRigid_K8`, `CDK.not_conjecture`, `Review369595.cdk_source_refuted`, `CDK.K9.k9_edgeRedundantlyRigid`

- **Kernel-checked versus paper-only:** Lean checks the original normed-space rigidity definitions, geometric and combinatorial bridges, unsatisfiability via a verified checker, and K₉. The positive extension to n≥10 and the resulting exact complete-graph threshold in dimension three are established in the paper.

### 373779 · Gatzweiler–Krattenthaler conjecture on positivity of Stanton fractions

- **Outcome:** REFUTED
- **Source:** Mona Gatzweiler, Fabián Levicán-Santibáñez and Atsuro Yoshida, “Cyclotomic generating functions, empty weighted complete intersections and positivity”, arXiv:2603.22226 (2026).
- **What is proved (exact):** Conjecture 4 of Gatzweiler and Krattenthaler (Conjecture 1.3 in Gatzweiler, Levican-Santibanez and Yoshida: for positive integers m >= n and non-negative integers a_1,...,a_n, if prod (1-q^{m+i})^{a_i} / prod (1-q^i)^{a_i} is a polynomial in q then its coefficients are non-negative) is false: for n = 38, m = 58, a_6 = a_9 = a_18 = a_38 = 1, a_32 = 2 and all other a_i = 0 the quotient is an integer polynomial of degree 348 with coefficient -1 at q^67. Stanton's original symmetric conjecture and Gatzweiler-Krattenthaler's symmetric Conjecture 3 are not affected.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $m,n$ be positive integers with $m\geq n$, let $\mathbf{a}=(a_1,\ldots,a_n)$ be a sequence of non-negative integers, and consider $$ \frac{\prod_{i=1}^n(1-q^{m+i})^{a_i}}{\prod_{i=1}^n(1-q^i)^{a_i}}. $$ **Gatzweiler–Krattenthaler conjecture.** If this fraction is a polynomial in $q$, then it has non-negative coefficients. This is presented as a strengthening of Stanton's conjecture, removing both the assumed polynomiality for every non-negative $m$ and the symmetry condition on $\mathbf{a}$. The supplied text does not report a proof or disproof of this strengthened claim.

### 375742 · Conjecture on non-full-weight cyclic codes over \(\mathbb F_3\)

- **Outcome:** REFUTED
- **Source:** Yangcheng Li and Pingzhi Yuan, “Cyclic Codes and Cyclically Covering Subspaces over Finite Fields”, arXiv:2607.02239 (2026).
- **What is proved (exact):** Conjecture 4.1 of Li and Yuan (for a prime n > 3 with ord_n(3) odd and r = (n-1)/ord_n(3), the number of cyclic codes over F_3 of length n with no full-weight codeword equals the sum of binom(r,j) for 0 <= j <= r/2) is false at n = 109, where ord_109(3) = 27 and r = 4: the family of shift-invariant subspaces of F_3^109 with no all-nonzero vector has at most 9 members, while the formula gives 11. Lean proves the bound for actual submodules, with the factorization of X^109 - 1 into irreducibles proved inside Lean; the exact value 9 is not claimed in Lean.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $n>3$ be a prime, and let $\operatorname{ord}_n(3)$ denote the multiplicative order of $3$ modulo $n$. Set $$ r=\frac{n-1}{\operatorname{ord}_n(3)}. $$ Let $\mathcal N(n,3)$ denote the family of cyclic codes over $\mathbb F_3$ containing no full-weight codeword. **The cyclic-code counting conjecture.** If $\operatorname{ord}_n(3)$ is odd, then $$ |\mathcal N(n,3)|=\sum_{j=0}^{\frac r2}\binom{r}{j}. $$ The formula refines a lower bound for $|\mathcal N(n,3)|$; the case $n=13$ gives $11$ cyclic codes, while the conjecture remains unproved in the stated generality.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Cyclic375742.mathdb375742_literal`, `Cyclic375742.mathdb375742`, `Cyclic375742.count109_le_nine`, `Cyclic375742.count109_ne_eleven`

### 377269 · Hamiltonian-path distance congruence conjecture for Cartesian products of directed cycles

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (ledger result 72)
- **Source:** David Austin, Heather Gavlas and Dave Witte, “Hamiltonian Paths in Cartesian Powers of Directed Cycles”, arXiv:math/0110073 (2001).
- **What is proved (exact):** In the Cartesian product of directed cycles of lengths (2,2,3), the distinct vertices u=(0,0,0) and v=(0,0,1) have shortest directed distance 1, and the distance is −1 modulo the gcd 1, but there is no directed Hamiltonian path from u to v. Hence the universal equivalence fails both for arbitrary endpoints and for distinct endpoints.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** LITERAL
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $X$ be the Cartesian product of $k$ directed cycles of lengths $m_1,m_2,\ldots,m_k$, where $k\geq 3$ and each $m_i\geq 2$. For vertices $u$ and $v$ of $X$, let $d_X(u,v)$ denote the length of a shortest directed path from $u$ to $v$. **Hamiltonian-path distance congruence conjecture.** There is a Hamiltonian path from $u$ to $v$ if and only if $$ d_X(u,v)\equiv -1\pmod{\gcd(m_1,m_2,\ldots,m_k)}. $$ This generalizes the coprime-length case, in which the congruence condition is automatic, and remains open according to the supplied source.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Torus377269.expanded_unconditional`, `Torus377269.expanded_distinct_unconditional`, `Independent377269.literal_refutation`, `Independent377269.literal_distinct_refutation`

### 377725 · The layered extremal word conjecture

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (layered-maximiser clause; the density equality is true)
- **Source:** A. Burstein, Peter Hästö and T. Mansour, “Packing patterns into words”, arXiv:math/0212343 (2003).
- **What is proved (exact):** Conjecture 2.8 of Burstein, Hasto and Mansour (for a set Pi of layered patterns, delta'(Pi) = delta(Pi), and among maximal Pi-containing strings in [k]^n there is one which is layered) is false through its second clause: for Pi = {11223, 13222}, n = 13 and k = 4 the word 1133422222222 has 337 occurrences while every layered word of length 13 has at most 336, so no layered word is maximal. The first clause (the density equality) is true by Barton (2004) and is not refuted.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $\Pi$ be a set of layered patterns, and let $\delta(\Pi)$ denote their packing density and $\delta'(\Pi)$ the corresponding density when the alphabet size is fixed as in the paper. A string is **layered** if it is formed from layers, and a maximal $\Pi$-containing string is one attaining the maximum number of occurrences of patterns in $\Pi$. **Layered extremal word conjecture.** If $\Pi$ is a set of layered patterns, then $$ \delta'(\Pi)=\delta(\Pi), $$ and among maximal $\Pi$-containing strings in $[k]^n$, there is one which is layered. This conjecture asserts that restricting to layered strings does not reduce the packing density for sets of layered patterns, and that a layered extremal string always exists. The supplied context does not establish the claim or give a resolution status.
- **Lean endpoint:** `Layered377725.mathdb377725`, `Layered377725.not_source_conjecture`, `Layered377725.requiredEndpoint`, `Layered377725.no_layered_maximizer`, `Layered377725.layered_score_bound`

### 378276 · The minimal critical-set size of $L(9)$

- **Outcome:** REFUTED
- **Source:** Richard Bean, “Critical sets in the elementary abelian 2- and 3- groups”, arXiv:math/0403006 (2004).
- **What is proved (exact):** Conjecture 2 of Bean (the minimum size of a critical set in L(9), the addition table of the elementary abelian group Z_3 x Z_3, is 29) is false: an explicit set of 28 entries of L(9) has L(9) as its unique completion to a Latin square of order 9, and deleting any one entry gives a set with a second completion, so it is a critical set and the minimum is at most 28. The exact minimum is not determined.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $L(9)$ denote the Latin square of order $9$, and let $C$ be a critical set in $L(9)$. The minimum cardinality of a critical set in $L(9)$ is **the $L(9)$ minimal critical-set conjecture.** $$ 29. $$ The preceding discussion gives computational evidence for lower bounds near this value and notes that proving optimality, or finding a smaller solution, remains unresolved. Thus the conjecture asserts that the minimum is exactly $29$.

The interval is suggested by computations on random permutations for $n=100$ and $n=300$. The problem of obtaining improved asymptotic estimates, or exact formulas, remains open.

### 347063 · Linear-growth conjecture for Split Smallest and Combine Smallest games

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 90)
- **Source:** Ruoci Li, Xiaonan Li, Steven J. Miller, Clayton Mizgerd, Chenyang Sun, Dong Xia and Zhyi Zhou, “Deterministic Zeckendorf Games”, arXiv:2006.16457 (2020).
- **What is proved (exact):** From n copies of F₁, Combine Smallest in Li et al.'s Conjecture 1.4 takes cn+O(√n log n) moves, where c=5/2−φ+Σ_{j≥2}1/F_{2j}≈1.20648117. This resolves the part left open after Cusenza et al. proved the Split Smallest slope. The MathDB record transcribes the terminal decomposition as the starting position.
- **MathDB record statement:** A Zeckendorf game starts from the Zeckendorf decomposition of $n$ and proceeds by legal combining and splitting moves; the **Split Smallest** and **Combine Smallest** games are deterministic strategies, and the number of moves is measured as the total number of moves in the resulting game. **Linear-growth conjecture.** For Split Smallest, the number of moves grows linearly with $n$, with the asymptotic constant appearing numerically to be the square of the golden mean. For Combine Smallest games, the number of moves grows linearly with $n$, with the constant appearing numerically to be approximately $1.206$. The paper presents these claims as data-supported conjectures for the two deterministic games whose behavior was not rigorously determined there.

### 352193 · Symmetry classification conjecture for board isomorphisms

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 70)
- **Source:** Ary Shaviv, “Board games, random boards and long boards”, arXiv:2110.05416 (2021).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every odd n≥3, give each n×n compass board the directed graph of all positive ray steps from each cell, with the upper-left and center vertices fixed as named roots. Among maps induced by a cell permutation and one global permutation of the eight directions, exactly identity and physical main-diagonal reflection preserve rooted digraph isomorphism for every board, with the isomorphism allowed to depend on the board.
- **Relation of the Lean endpoint to the MathDB record:** SOURCE_NOT_RECORD
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $\mathcal{D}$ be the set of eight arrow directions, let $\operatorname{Mat}_{n\times n}(\mathcal{D})$ be the set of boards, and let $\operatorname{Sym}(\operatorname{Mat}_{n\times n}(\mathcal{D}))$ be the group of coordinate permutations and direction permutations that preserve graph isomorphism for every board. Let $R$ be reflection through the main diagonal, defined by $R(A)_{ij}=(a_{ji})^T$. **Symmetry classification conjecture.** $$ \operatorname{Sym}(\operatorname{Mat}_{n\times n}(\mathcal{D}))=\{Id,R\}. $$ The reflection $R$ is known to belong to this symmetry group, so the assertion is that it is the only nontrivial universal symmetry of boards. The source provides no resolution of this classification question.
- **Lean endpoint:** `BoardV1.source_group_endpoint`, `BoardV1.all_odd_source_groups`, `BoardV1.literal_endpoint`

### 358938 · The equivalence of NT-balanced and ultra NT-balanced graphs

- **Outcome:** REFUTED (source conjecture)
- **Source:** Linus Tang, “Extremal Bounds on Peripherality Measures”, arXiv:2306.15702 (2023).
- **What is proved (exact):** Tang's Conjecture 6.3 (every NT-balanced graph is ultra NT-balanced) is false: a 24-vertex 5-regular Cayley graph on S_3 x Z_4 is NT-balanced but not ultra NT-balanced. Lean also proves that the implication as paraphrased in the MathDB record holds trivially.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $G$ be a graph. For vertices $u,v$ and an integer $a$, let $n_a(u,v)$ be the number of vertices whose distance difference relative to $u$ and $v$ equals $a$. Call $G$ **NT-balanced** when $n_a(u,v)=n_a(v,u)$ for every pair of vertices $u,v$ and every relevant integer $a$. Call it **ultra NT-balanced** when, for every pair $u,v$, there is an involution of the vertex set pairing vertices counted by $n_a(u,v)$ with vertices counted by $n_a(v,u)$. **NT-balance equivalence conjecture.** Every NT-balanced graph is also ultra NT-balanced. The paper proves that ultra NT-balanced graphs are closed under Cartesian products and uses this to construct arbitrarily large examples. Whether every NT-balanced graph admits the required involutions remains open.
- **Lean endpoint:** `TangNT.tang_conjecture_false`, `TangNT.exists_nt_balanced_not_ultra`, `TangNT.mathdb_literal_implication`
- **Replay record:** `nt-balanced-358938-root-final-replay-fable-20261119-r1`; acceptance record `not in ledger (pipeline complete; see packet)`; submission folder `nt-balanced-358938-formal-paper-sol62-20261119-r1 (paper: final-paper-check-358938-opus-20261119-r1/PAPER-358938-revised.md)`
- **Prior art searched:** `shard013-candidate-review-sol62-20261119-r1/REVIEW-358938.md (Sol: PASS for the source conjecture); final-paper-check-358938-opus-20261119-r1/CHECK-358938.md (PASS WITH EDITORIAL EDITS)`; sweep verdict NO_PRIOR_FOUND (candidate review and final check; Principia id list, GitHub by id); see lane queries and routes recorded
- **Adversarial verdicts:** shard013-candidate-review-sol62-20261119-r1/REVIEW-358938.md (Sol: PASS for the source conjecture); final-paper-check-358938-opus-20261119-r1/CHECK-358938.md (PASS WITH EDITORIAL EDITS)
- **Status note:** reserve result, pipeline complete, NOT in ledger: the MathDB record changes Tang's '<' to 'equals', which makes the recorded implication trivially true; Tang's Conjecture 6.3 as printed is refuted

### 331690 · The achromatic number bound by the harmonic index

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 97)
- **Source:** Baoyindureng Wu and Clive Elphick, “Upper bounds for the achromatic and coloring numbers of a graph”, arXiv:1511.00537 (2015).
- **What is proved (exact):** PROVED for graphs with an edge (Wu-Elphick 2015, Conjecture 4.1): for every finite simple graph G with at least one edge, psi(G) <= 2R'(G), where R'(G) = sum over edges uv of 1/max{d(u), d(v)}. More strongly, every complete colouring (proper or not) uses at most 2R'(G) colours, so the pseudoachromatic and Grundy numbers obey the same bound (Conjecture 4.2). The literal 'for any graph' fails exactly on nonempty edgeless graphs (psi = 1, R' = 0). These are the only exceptions, and Lean proves this exact truth set.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $G$ be a graph. The **achromatic number** $psi(G)$ is the largest number of colors in a complete proper vertex coloring, and $R'(G)$ denotes the inverse degree-based index used in the source. **The achromatic-number conjecture.** $$ \psi(G)\leq 2R'(G). $$ The source presents this as a conjecture motivated by the preceding bounds. Its status is not resolved in the supplied text.
- **Lean endpoint:** `fin_complete_colouring_le_two_randicPrime`, `fin_achromatic_le_two_randicPrime`, `fin_truth_set`, `wuElphickConjecture41`, `not_wuElphickConjecture41Literal`, `wuElphickConjecture42`
- **Replay record:** `achromatic-331690-root-final-replay-fable-20261119-r1 (root, Claude Fable 5.2)`; acceptance record `achromatic-number-modified-randic-bound-331690-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/331690-achromatic-number-modified-randic-bound`
- **Prior art searched:** `public-tracker-prior-art-sweep-sol62-20261119-r1`; sweep verdict PARTIAL_OVERLAP, top-up verdict PARTIAL_OVERLAP; 50 queries and routes recorded
- **Closest prior work read:** Wu–Elphick, Upper bounds for the achromatic and coloring numbers of a graph (full text) https://arxiv.org/html/1511.00537v2
- **Adversarial verdicts:** BLOCKED_ON_SOURCE (closecall-adjudication-A3-opus-20261119-r1, confidence 0.8)
- **Status note:** Lin-Wang 2025
- **Caveats the row carries:**
  - Proved for every graph with at least one edge. The literal 'for any graph' is false only on nonempty edgeless graphs (psi = 1 > 0 = 2R'), which the source's conventions implicitly exclude. Do not write 'for any graph'.
  - The MathDB title says 'harmonic index', but its statement uses R' (the modified Randic index). Since R' <= H, the harmonic-index reading also follows.
  - Significance modest: a short elementary counting proof, not a deep result.
  - Pending: Lin-Wang DAM 2025 full text must confirm that it does not already prove psi <= 2R' or Gamma <= 2R'.

### 365084 · The finite-cycle character-value coincidence conjecture for symmetric groups

- **Outcome:** PROVED (ledger result 79)
- **Source:** David A. Craven, “On the irreducible character degrees of symmetric groups and their multiplicities”, arXiv:2410.01726 (2025).
- **What is proved (exact):** For every finite set X of test permutations and every k >= 1, for all sufficiently large n, S_n has k distinct irreducible complex characters whose values agree on every permutation with the cycle type of a test in X extended by fixed points. In fact they agree on every permutation moving at most d letters (d = max arity), which also gives Craven's Conjecture 3.6 for m = d. Craven, arXiv:2410.01726, Conjecture 3.5.
- **MathDB record statement:** Let $X$ be a finite set of permutations, and let $k\geq 1$ be an integer. For a permutation $g\in S_n$, its cycle type is the multiset of lengths of its cycles. **Finite-cycle character-value conjecture.** For all sufficiently large $n$, there exist $k$ distinct irreducible characters of $S_n$ whose values on permutations $g\in S_n$ coincide for all $g$ whose cycle type belongs to $X$. The conjecture asks for arbitrarily large collections of distinct irreducible characters that cannot be distinguished by their values on any prescribed finite set of cycle types. The source contrasts it with an adaptive procedure for determining characters from values and gives computational examples, but leaves the conjecture open.
- **Lean endpoint:** `LiteralColoring.finite_cycle_character_value_coincidence`
- **Replay record:** `character-moments-365084-independent-extracted-replay-sol62-20261117-r1`; acceptance record `symmetric-character-value-coincidence-365084-acceptance.json`; submission folder `mathdb-submissions/365084-symmetric-character-value-coincidence`
- **Prior art searched:** `public-tracker-prior-art-sweep-sol62-20261119-r1`; sweep verdict PARTIAL_OVERLAP, top-up verdict PARTIAL_OVERLAP; 48 queries and routes recorded
- **Closest prior work read:** Craven, Experimental Mathematics version of record (full text) https://pure-oai.bham.ac.uk/ws/portalfiles/portal/266642612/On_the_Irreducible_Character_Degrees_of_Symmetric_Groups_and_their_Multiplicities.pdf
- **Adversarial verdicts:** BLOCKED_ON_SOURCE (closecall-adjudication-A1-opus-20261119-r1, confidence 0.85 that it clears once Katriel is read; 0.9 that it is no…)
- **Status note:** Katriel 1997
- **Caveats the row carries:**
  - Stable (fixed-point-padded) reading of Conjecture 3.5; the literal reading with fixed points counted is vacuous
  - The k=2 case is elementary; the contribution is the uniform k-tuple construction
  - Katriel 1997 and the T&F final version must be read before going public
  - Bounded literature review; no human peer review
